# Codex conversation 01a12887-2c41-7751-8a94-12c4e0f990aa

## assistant

Finished the skills audit using the Karpathy agent and an independent verification pass.

The biggest problem is **stale instructions and overly broad triggers**, rather than simply having too many skills.

The highest-priority repairs are:

- **Provider confusion:** global `claude-api` and `academy-guide` incorrectly substitute Codex names into Anthropic instructions.
- **Incomplete reviews:** some code-review instructions miss uncommitted changes.
- **Unexpected actions:** certain inspection, QA and Workspace workflows proceed into assignments, publication or other writes.
- **Wrong destinations:** older skills use obsolete paths or write operational material into immutable source folders.
- **Overlapping triggers:** some design, YouTube and authoring skills compete unnecessarily.

Cleanup candidates include the old `obsidian-vault` workflow, superseded `lsat-u-topic-builder`, broadly triggered examples and overlapping authoring routes. **Useful references and deliberate runtime copies should be preserved.** Ingest, query, lint, persona creation and Karpathy teaching serve different purposes.

Your finished files:

- [Findings and ranked recommendations](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/audits/skills-2026-10-09/SUMMARY.md)
- [Every entry, its disposition and justification](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/audits/skills-2026-10-09/ALL-SKILL-DISPOSITIONS.md)
- [Independent verification](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/audits/skills-2026-10-09/PARENT-REVIEW.md)

**What I ran:** reviewed bodies and variants, checked routing, verified all **1,316 listed file hashes**, and tested the Git review omission in an isolated fixture.

**What came out:** complete dispositions for **1,242 entries**. Another **74 historical snapshots** were inventoried only. Live workflows and every supporting script were not exhaustively tested.

**What changed:** audit reports only. **No skill was deleted, deactivated or rewritten.**

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
These AGENTS.md instructions replace all previously provided AGENTS.md instructions.

# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

## Shared Windows CLI tools

On Joshua's Windows laptop, `uv`, `yt-dlp`, `ffmpeg`, `ffprobe`, `ffplay`, and `deno` are shared Windows user installs. Read `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\references\ai-workspace\windows-cli-toolchain.md` for their paths, versions, usage, and PATH refresh steps. Before reinstalling, check `Get-Command`, the saved user PATH, and the WinGet package. A running IDE may have stale PATH; refresh the shell or restart that IDE. Keep Python dependencies in project environments.

<!-- HAND-OWNED since 2026-07-17. This file is the authority for Codex — edit it directly. On any major change, mirror it into CLAUDE.md so Claude and Codex stay in sync. -->
# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| LLM Wiki schema (source, page, ingest/query/lint rules) | `system/schema/llm-wiki.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Shared Windows CLI tools for Codex, Claude Code, and Antigravity | `references/ai-workspace/windows-cli-toolchain.md` — installed paths, PATH refresh, and usage for `uv`, `yt-dlp`, FFmpeg, and Deno |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| Current local work in Claude/Codex cloud | `setup/github-sync/README.md`; private `codex/local-sync/<machine>/<checkout>` branches and generated `.cloud-sync/` metadata/conversation exports. Cloud edits use separate task branches. |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — intake queue; `ingest` copies and hash-verifies clips into `raw/sources/clips/`; processed originals stay until removal is authorized, so inbox presence alone does not mean unprocessed |
| Application code | `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `../ai-os-projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

Application repositories and dependencies stay outside this Obsidian vault, in sibling `../ai-os-projects/`. Do not create a junction from the vault back into that directory.

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**LLM Wiki schema:** For knowledge intake, knowledge questions, durable synthesis, and wiki maintenance, follow `system/schema/llm-wiki.md`. Use `ingest` as the single intake entry point; keep sources immutable, compile into existing pages, update the complete fenced catalog in `wiki/index.md`, and append changes to `wiki/log.md`. Preserve domain homes and live-system authority. Mechanical tasks retain the retrieval exceptions above.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **LSAT teaching:** use project-owned `lsat-u-voice` and `references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md`; this Joshua-selected standard takes precedence over older vendor voice blends.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Shared Windows command tools:** use `uv`, `yt-dlp`, `ffmpeg`, `ffprobe`, `ffplay`, and `deno` from the Windows user PATH. For versions, locations, how to invoke them, or a stale terminal environment, read `references/ai-workspace/windows-cli-toolchain.md`. Check before reinstalling; restart the affected IDE when its inherited PATH is stale.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **Git remote is enabled.** `origin` is GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/ai-os`, decided 2026-09-29). The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.
- **New laptop restore:** GitHub contains tracked ai-os files and skills. It deliberately excludes `.env`, `raw/`, app repos, credentials, and machine sessions. Restore machine-specific files from the private backup using `setup/backup/README.md` and its `README - START HERE.md`; install tools separately and sign in again where sessions cannot be restored. Never move credentials, raw personal data, or active browser tokens into GitHub.
- **Authorized cloud transfer (2026-10-07):** private sync branches also carry portable skills, memory and conversation exports. Joshua explicitly authorized credential transfer; implementation uses the authenticated encrypted package described in `setup/github-sync/README.md`; plaintext secrets and its unlock key stay outside GitHub and Drive. Cloud authentication and native browser sessions still require separate verification.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `transcript-intake` | retrieving, filing or reconciling student/session transcripts; separate from knowledge `ingest` |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex and Claude both activate the LLM Wiki ingest, query, and lint workflows. Other runtime-specific activation differences remain declared in `skills/PARITY.md`. Never have separate runtimes writing the same page concurrently.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

</INSTRUCTIONS>

## developer

<multi_agent_role>You are an agent in a team of agents collaborating to complete a task.

You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents. All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent.
`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.

When you provide a response in the final channel, that content is immediately delivered back to your parent agent.
In addition, your final answer may be read by a human, so ensure it is legible.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: NEW_TASK | MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
You may also see them addressed as to=/root/..., which indicates your identity is /root/...

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"`.</multi_agent_role>

## developer

<multi_agent_mode>Any earlier instruction enabling proactive multi-agent delegation no longer applies. Do not spawn sub-agents unless the user or applicable AGENTS.md/skill instructions explicitly ask for sub-agents, delegation, or parallel agent work.</multi_agent_mode>

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 5.3 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"27ab98","wall_time_seconds":0.4616751,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":3205,"output":"---\\ntype: synthesis\\nsubject: Seven evidence-supported Karpathy building and teaching rules\\ncreated: 2026-10-08\\nupdated: 2026-10-08\\naliases: []\\ntags: [karpathy, teaching, evidence]\\nsources: [../sources/karpathy/blog/recipe.md, ../sources/karpathy/blog/undergraduate-study-advice.md, ../sources/karpathy/blog/understand-backprop.md, ../sources/karpathy/blog/microgpt.md, ../sources/karpathy/blog/calculator.md, ../sources/karpathy/x/claude-coding-notes.md, ../sources/karpathy/animals-vs-ghosts.md, ../sources/karpathy/lectures/vmj-3s1tku0.md, ../sources/karpathy/lectures/pacmpygffxo.md, ../sources/karpathy/lectures/l8prsuu81pu.md]\\n---\\n\\n# Seven strongest rules for building and teaching\\n\\nFinalized 2026-10-08 after [the collected-source compilation audit](../../research/karpathy-brain-2026-10-08/COMPILATION-AUDIT.md). These seven recurring patterns are a synthesis of reviewed public evidence, not Karpathy\'s endorsement, private mind or a claim that every historical implementation followed them. Each has two distinct original source artifacts with exact quotation support. Separate artifacts corroborate recurring advice; they do not establish independent experimental replication. Automatic-caption wording remains uncorrected and is not audio-verified.\\n\\n**Evidence** below describes his advice or demonstration. **Application** is our practical implementation and grading standard. Repository ownership, model-generated prompts, third-party contributions and syndicated copies were not used as sole proof of personal authorship. Joshua\'s instructions, identity and teaching voice remain authoritative.\\n\\n## 1. Explain and reconstruct the mechanism\\n\\n**Evidence:** His study guidance distinguishes active reconstruction from passive understanding; his backprop article recommends implementation to solidify mechanism-level understanding.\\n\\n> Reading and understanding IS NOT the same as replicating\\n\\n[Study advice](../sources/karpathy/blog/undergraduate-study-advice.md), study section; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/other-first-party/cs.stanford.edu_people_karpathy_advice.html.md). Publication date unknown.\\n\\n> get you to write backprop manually and help you solidify your understanding.\\n\\n[Yes you should understand backprop](../sources/karpathy/blog/understand-backprop.md), conclusion, 2016-12-19; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/medium/karpathy.medium.com_yes-you-should-understand-backprop-e2f06eab496b.md).\\n\\n**Application:** Explain input, transformation and output using a small example, then invite the learner to reproduce or explain one step. Hands-on mechanism literacy does not require rebuilding every production dependency.\\n\\n## 2. Start with one correct small case and a baseline\\n\\n**Evidence:** His recipe recommends getting a specific implementation working before generalizing. In Makemore he starts the neural pipeline with one word rather than the full dataset.\\n\\n> get that to work, and then generalize it later making sure that I get the same result.\\n\\n[Recipe](../sources/karpathy/blog/recipe.md), “generalize a special case,” 2019-04-25; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/github-blog/karpathy.github.io_2019_04_25_recipe.md).\\n\\n> [01:06:43] all the words just yet because I want\\n> [01:06:45] everything to be manageable. Uh so,\\n> [01:06:47] let\'s just do the first word, which is\\n\\n[Makemore lecture](../sources/karpathy/lectures/pacmpygffxo.md), 2022-09-07; [exact raw captions](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/youtube/transcripts/PaCmpygFfXo.md). The preceding cue says not to take all words yet.\\n\\n**Application:** Establish one small end-to-end case and a baseline, then introduce one justified change at a time. A teaching toy and a production system have different success requirements. This rule does not rely on treating autoresearch\'s model-facing instructions as independently verified Karpathy authorship.\\n\\n## 3. Predict, run and compare\\n\\n**Evidence:** His recipe describes hypotheses followed by validation or investigation; Makemore predicts normalized row sums and unchanged results before running the vectorized calculation.\\n\\n> make concrete hypotheses about what will happen and then either validate them with an experiment or investigate until we find some issue.\\n\\n[Recipe](../sources/karpathy/blog/recipe.md), “The recipe,” 2019-04-25; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/github-blog/karpathy.github.io_2019_04_25_recipe.md).\\n\\n> [00:44:04] taking its sum. We expect this to be\\n> [00:44:06] one.\\n\\n[Makemore lecture](../sources/karpathy/lectures/pacmpygffxo.md), normalization check, 2022-09-07; [exact raw captions](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/youtube/transcripts/PaCmpygFfXo.md). At 00:44:16 he runs the calculation; 00:44:19 reports the same result.\\n\\n**Application:** State the expected behavior before executing a meaningful check, report what happened, and explain any mismatch. Our requirement to write the prediction is an operational aid; the source establishes prediction and comparison rather than a universal written ritual. Expected output never substitutes for observed output.\\n\\n## 4. Expose a counterexample and repair its cause\\n\\n**Evidence:** Micrograd exposes a repeated-node derivative bug with a tiny counterexample; the backprop article traces why clipping a forward error accidentally eliminates the needed gradient.\\n\\n> [01:22:39] so here\'s the simplest case that shows\\n> [01:22:42] the bug\\n\\n[Micrograd lecture](../sources/karpathy/lectures/vmj-3s1tku0.md), repeated-node example, 2022-08-16; [exact raw captions](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/youtube/transcripts/VMj-3S1tku0.md). It expects derivative two, exposes overwritten contributions and repairs them with accumulation.\\n\\n> The problem is on line 291.\\n\\n[Backprop article](../sources/karpathy/blog/understand-backprop.md), DQN clipping example, 2016-12-19; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/medium/karpathy.medium.com_yes-you-should-understand-backprop-e2f06eab496b.md). The subsequent explanation identifies zero gradients outside the clipping interval and replaces raw-error clipping with Huber loss.\\n\\n**Application:** Use a meaningful edge or failing case to make a cause inspectable, repair it and check the repaired behavior. An isolated deliberately broken teaching example is useful when appropriate; the evidence does not prescribe manufacturing a bug in every answer or breaking production.\\n\\n## 5. Verify the relevant behavior, not merely a successful run\\n\\n**Evidence:** His coding notes recommend tests with explicit success criteria. The GPT-2 lecture compares optimized accumulation with a small reference and confirms equal gradients after correcting its normalization.\\n\\n> Get it to write tests first and then pass them.\\n\\n[Claude coding notes](../sources/karpathy/x/claude-coding-notes.md), 2026-01-26, post `2015883857489522876`; [raw ledger](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/posts.jsonl), selected own-author `text` field for that ID.\\n\\n> [02:43:32] that the U gradients are now identical\\n\\n[GPT-2 reproduction lecture](../sources/karpathy/lectures/l8prsuu81pu.md), reference comparison, 2024-06-09; [exact raw captions](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/youtube/transcripts/l8pRSuU81PU.md). The caption\'s “U” is preserved, not silently corrected.\\n\\n**Application:** Run checks that test the requested behavior against a reference, invariant or expected outcome. Report commands, results and untested limits. A failed actual run still ran but cannot justify “works.” Local checks do not certify live, production or device acceptance.\\n\\n**Historical counterevidence:** [Neuraltalk2\'s beam-search comment](../sources/karpathy/repos/supplemental/repos--karpathy--neuraltalk2--misc--LanguageModel-lua.md) says it is not fully unit-tested and accepts plausible outputs; [runq.c](../sources/karpathy/repos/final-three/repos--karpathy--llama2-c--runq-c.md) explicitly calls chat an unsafe proof of concept. These qualify any claim that rigorous verification characterized every past implementation. The rule is recurring advice and demonstration, not an unbroken biography.\\n\\n## 6. Make assumptions and uncertainty visible\\n\\n**Evidence:** His coding notes criticize unchecked assumptions. Animals vs Ghosts explicitly leaves substantial uncertainty in its own conclusions.\\n\\n> the models make wrong assumptions on your behalf and just run along with them without checking.\\n\\n[Claude coding notes](../sources/karpathy/x/claude-coding-notes.md), 2026-01-26, post `2015883857489522876`; [raw ledger](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/posts.jsonl), selected own-author `text` field.\\n\\n> with double digit percent uncertainty\\n\\n[Animals vs Ghosts](../sources/karpathy/animals-vs-ghosts.md), his own “As for my take” discussion, 2025-10-01; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/bearblog/karpathy.bearblog.dev_animals-vs-ghosts.md). This is his qualification, not the opening exposition of Sutton\'s view.\\n\\n**Application:** Expose material assumptions, contradictions and uncertainty; resolve what the available evidence can resolve, then ask about consequential remaining ambiguity. Keep dated capability claims attributed. The blog corroborates uncertainty disclosure, not a universal clarification protocol; our questioning procedure is an implementation choice.\\n\\n## 7. Keep only complexity that earns its place\\n\\n**Evidence:** Microgpt pursues a minimal algorithmic teaching core; Calculator praises low dependency and operating burden. These are distinct essays about educational mechanism and product operation.\\n\\n> I cannot simplify this any further.\\n\\n[Microgpt](../sources/karpathy/blog/microgpt.md), opening, 2026-02-12; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/github-blog/karpathy.github.io_2026_02_12_microgpt.md).\\n\\n> almost zero \\"dependency footprint\\"\\n\\n[I love calculator](../sources/karpathy/blog/calculator.md), self-contained artifact discussion, 2024-09-08; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/bearblog/karpathy.bearblog.dev_i-love-calculator.md).\\n\\n**Application:** Prefer a readable implementation with only the abstractions, dependencies and services the task needs. Preserve correctness while simplifying. Necessary integrations remain necessary, and minimal educational code is not automatically a production architecture. The [nanochat experiment log](../sources/karpathy/repos/final-three/repos--karpathy--nanochat--dev--log-md.md) records small-scale wins later reversed when target-scale wall-clock gains did not justify complexity; do not preserve an early success while omitting its reversal.\\n\\n## Quote and independence audit\\n\\nOn 2026-10-08 every displayed quotation above was checked as an exact substring of its linked raw artifact, including timestamped caption cues. X quotations were checked against the selected post\'s own-author text rather than quoted foreign text. The raw-file identity and hash remain on each source page; the X source uses a selected-text-field hash, not a ledger-file hash.\\n\\n| Rule | Distinct source identities | Independence result |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| 1 | Stanford personal study advice; Medium backprop article | Separate writings; shared author/theme, no syndicated duplicate |\\n| 2 | 2019 Recipe; 2022 Makemore lecture `PaCmpygFfXo` | Article and separate teaching demonstration |\\n| 3 | 2019 Recipe; 2022 Makemore lecture `PaCmpygFfXo` | Article and explicit prediction/check demonstration |\\n| 4 | 2022 Micrograd lecture `VMj-3S1tku0`; 2016 backprop article | Distinct repeated-node and Q-error-clipping cases |\\n| 5 | X post `2015883857489522876`; 2024 GPT-2 lecture `l8pRSuU81PU` | Advice and separate reference-comparison demonstration |\\n| 6 | X post `2015883857489522876`; 2025 Animals vs Ghosts | Different subjects/artifacts, not the essay\'s syndicated X copy |\\n| 7 | 2026 Microgpt; 2024 Calculator | Separate educational and product essays |\\n\\nThe same source can support multiple different rules; two passages within one source never count as two locations for a single rule. Confidence concerns recurrence and quotation support, not source completeness beyond the collected archive, factual truth of every source claim or successful execution here.\\n\\n## Grade the answer against observable evidence\\n\\nFor each rule mark pass, needs improvement, or not applicable, with one concrete reason. Do not award a pass for merely repeating a rule. Questions without code may have execution marked not applicable. Source-specific claims link to the source pages. Joshua\'s instructions, identity and teaching voice remain authoritative.\\n\\nRelated: [index](index.md), [hot](hot.md), [methods](methods.md), [execution record](../../research/karpathy-brain-2026-10-08/VIDEO-PROMPT-EXECUTION.md).\\n\\r\\n"}'}]

The isolated fixture below proves this Git limitation. |\\r\\n| 2 | `bootcamp-parity-audit` | Three mandatory benchmark anchors were absent at both literal legacy and current-root equivalents. | Resolve existing authoritative benchmarks before asserting parity; do not invent replacements. |\\r\\n| 2 | `guided-drill-assignment` | Transcript inspection/recommendation triggers lead into an assignment workflow and compete with the current assignment executor. | Separate inspect, recommend and assign modes. A transcript-only request must make no live write. No duplicate live draft was created in this audit. |\\r\\n| 2 | `qa` | General QA trigger instructs immediate GitHub issue publication without review. | Distinguish report-only QA from explicitly authorized issue filing. |\\r\\n| 2 | `test-driven-development` | Commands deleting/restarting existing implementation solely because a failing test was not written first. | Preserve user work; use meaningful red-green regression tests proportionately. Never delete existing work as a ceremony. |\\r\\n| 2 | `resolving-merge-conflicts`, `setup-pre-commit` | Stage-all/automatic commit defaults risk including unrelated work. Hook setup also lacks preserve/merge guidance for existing hook configuration. | Capture baseline, stage only owned paths, preserve existing hooks and honor explicit commit scope. |\\r\\n| 2 | `youtube-full`, `watch`, inactive transcript aliases | Broad YouTube triggers select keyed/credit-consuming services; watch adds another secret home and cleanup deletion. | Free existing caption collector first, explicit optional paid fallback and bounded authorization, central secret route, preserve captures until removal authorized. |\\r\\n| 2 | Codex `lovable-prompt` | Requires Supabase MCP tools absent from this session. | Route through an available approved transport. The current global system skill already has the correct capability; the catalog copy is stale. |\\r\\n| 3 | `decision-mapping`, Codex `research-deep` | Misspelled `domain-modelling` route and an absent hardcoded research validator path. | Resolve exact installed dependencies before invocation; use the skill\'s actual support directory. |\\r\\n| 3 | Literal Claude `Skill` calls in Codex copies | No native Skill invocation tool is exposed in this Codex session. | Read the appropriate skill entry point or use the supported host route. Other redesign instructions fabricate organic data/dates. | Make aesthetics explicit optional presets; preserve the project\'s design system, facts and accessibility. |\\r\\n| 3 | `webapp-testing`, `web-artifacts-builder`, `teach` | Universal networkidle is not UI readiness; artifact builds default into cwd and discourage testing; learning workspace defaults can clutter the vault. | Select sibling application/learning homes, assert actual readiness and run relevant checks before completion. |\\r\\n| 3 | `setup-matt-pocock-skills`, `setup-ts-deep-modules` | Select one instruction file when this project requires shared rules in both manuals. | Host/project overlay must mirror shared pointers without regenerating hand-owned manuals. |\\r\\n\\r\\n## Cleanup candidates, preserving information\\r\\n\\r\\n| Candidate | Recommended disposition | Why |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| `obsidian-vault` for this AI OS | Leave inactive/deactivate its current-vault trigger | Targets `/mnt/d/Obsidian Vault/AI Research` and a flat no-folder layout, conflicting with this structured vault. Current ingest/query/lint own the work. |\\r\\n| `lsat-u-topic-builder` | Retire broad entry after preserving useful references | `bootcamp-builder` explicitly supersedes it; old Mac search and unsafe source/duplicate heuristics remain. |\\r\\n| `example-command`, `example-skill` | Reference-only | Educational samples have value but should not compete with real creator workflows. |\\r\\n| `brand-guidelines` | Explicit Anthropic-brand only | Its palette is Anthropic\'s, not Joshua\'s. |\\r\\n| `using-superpowers` | Narrow or explicit invocation | Catch-all every-conversation loading adds routing and approval friction. |\\r\\n| `high-end-visual-design` | Explicit aesthetic preset | A valid style choice should not override every frontend brief. |\\r\\n| `to-issues` and `to-tickets` | Consolidation candidate | Share vertical-slice breakdown; to-tickets adds local tracker/native edges and expand-contract. Preserve compatibility and approval semantics. |\\r\\n| `writing-beats` and `writing-shape` | Consolidation candidate | Both shape a fixed source pile collaboratively; beat mode can share one authoring core. Keep `writing-fragments` as the separate exploration phase. |\\r\\n| `writing-great-skills` and `writing-for-agents` | Shared reference candidate | Repeated vocabulary and hierarchy guidance can live once, with skill-specific mechanics retained. |\\r\\n| `ubiquitous-language` and domain modeling glossary routes | Consolidation candidate | Avoid competing `UBIQUITOUS_LANGUAGE.md` and `GLOSSARY.md` truths; preserve explicit ambiguity and relationship analysis. |\\r\\n| Inactive YouTube aliases | Keep inactive, optional provider reference | They offer overlapping keyed TranscriptAPI routes. Do not reactivate a dozen broad names; preserve any unique search/channel/playlist references. |\\r\\n| Original Nate handoff copies | Keep historical reference | Customized current handoff supersedes them. Their broad body triggers conflict with the frontmatter\'s explicit-only boundary if reactivated. |\\r\\n\\r\\nNo usage telemetry was gathered. An unfamiliar specialized skill is not proven useless. There is no recommendation to blanket-delete GWS helpers, media tools, runtime copies or specialist references.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Keep these core boundaries\\r\\n\\r\\n- `ingest`: add a new source and compile knowledge.\\r\\n- `second-brain-query`: answer from saved knowledge with evidence and freshness escalation.\\r\\n- `second-brain-lint`: check knowledge structure, contradictions, stale claims and missing pages.\\r\\n- `audit`: system health; `skill-lint`: package checks; `skill-doctor`: actual usage evidence. These do different jobs.\\r\\n- `persona-agent-creator`: build another documented-method agent; `karpathy-teach`: use the existing reviewed teaching agent.\\r\\n- Claudex provider pairs and compatibility wrappers: deliberate runtime directions in `PARITY.md`, not accidental duplicates.\\r\\n- GWS domain/helper skills: API surfaces and specific actions, not dozens of copies of one workflow.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Trigger counterexamples to use during repairs\\r\\n\\r\\nThese are static instruction comparisons, **not observed native selector execution**.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Request | Correct intent | Current collision | Acceptance requirement |\\r\\n|---|---|---|---|\\r\\n| Create a persona agent for this author | Persona factory | Global Codex-api catches unstated-provider agent tasks | Factory route; no API/provider assumption |\\r\\n| Read this student\'s transcript and tell me where they struggle | Inspection | Guided-drill assignment continues into execution | No assignment/send/live write |\\r\\n| Give me the transcript of this YouTube URL | Free caption extraction first | Broad keyed/credit routes | Keyless collector or explicit limitation; paid fallback only authorized |\\r\\n| Review my uncommitted changes | Worktree review | Three-dot HEAD range | Includes actual dirty/staged/untracked scope |\\r\\n| Polish this UI using our current design system | Preserve existing visual language | Presets impose contradictory universal aesthetics | Follow actual design system; motion/accessibility preserved |\\r\\n| Improve this small approved configuration | Direct authorized scoped edit | Generic brainstorming imposes new approval ceremony | Existing authorization honored |\\r\\n| What do my saved notes say about this? | Query | Generic old Obsidian workflow | Current schema/index retrieval, no alternate vault |\\r\\n\\r\\n## What was checked and what was not\\r\\n\\r\\nFull semantic entry-point reads were recovered after the original concatenated output truncated. Exact byte copies inherit a fully reviewed base; nonidentical variants require reviewed complete differences and a fully reviewed base. SHA-256 receipts identify physical files. LF-normalized exact text is explicitly distinguished from byte identity. Fresh hash reconciliation prevents stale review receipts from being silently counted.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe full per-entry table records scope and recommended disposition. Historical snapshots are inventoried and preserved; unmatched inactive entry points are reviewed separately. Supporting scripts, vendor payloads, assets and external plugin cache trees were not exhaustively executed. Their presence and selected routes were checked where relevant. This is not a claim every file on the machine was read or every third-party skill installed in every possible plugin root was tested.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe existing validator checks project activated/catalog drift and Claude/Codex parity; it does not certify owned global body correctness or Gemini. Add bounded owned-global/provider sanity and Gemini checks rather than mirroring every external vendor package into this repo.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Workspace helper findings\\r\\n\\r\\nThe fresh global GWS review found a broken shared-auth prerequisite in the synced `gws-gmail-read` copy: its `../gws-shared/SKILL.md` target is absent. Top-level GWS prerequisites are present. Other concrete corrections are the Forms `forms.update` prose versus `batchUpdate` inventory, apparent agent-task/message-stream resources mixed into Workspace Events, an unread-mail query described as inbox triage, and meeting preparation that promises linked-document contents without demonstrating retrieval. The shared GWS prerequisite does require write/delete confirmation, so local omissions are evaluated against that existing policy rather than reported as universally unguarded writes.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Recipe and global routing recovery\\r\\n\\r\\nThe fresh recipe/persona review preserves useful Google Workspace endpoint references but identifies concrete contract mistakes. `recipe-find-free-time` creates an event after a find-only request; `recipe-plan-weekly-schedule` queries 2025 free/busy while inserting a 2026 event; `recipe-copy-sheet-for-new-month` renames hardcoded sheet ID 123 instead of the response ID. Other recipes promise initial presentation slides, feedback questions, an attachment or bounded vacation dates without supplying those steps. The Gmail filter recipe removes INBOX during a generic labeling workflow, and the expense tracker appends a sample expense as real data. Repair these narrow contracts before reuse, rather than deleting all recipes.\\r\\n\\r\\nRole personas such as event coordinator or researcher are convenience routers, not evidence-based replicas of a real person\'s method. Keep their distinct Workspace recipes but narrow automatic publishing and role-wide triggers. The reusable `persona-agent-creator` remains a different capability.\\r\\n\\r\\nCurrent Gemini Superwhisper still contains an older settings.json/modeKeys workflow while current Claude/Codex bodies use the revised SQLite workflow. Align that stale runtime copy without discarding vocabulary/replacement guidance.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Additional full-body recovery findings\\r\\n\\r\\nThe long-body review adds concrete repair candidates without discarding the underlying capability:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Graphify:** the chunk contract requests JSON output while later success depends on a saved disk file; basename-only graph IDs can collide across folders, and updated files may leave stale graph content. Reconcile file contracts and refresh behavior before reuse. Its independent raw intake should use the existing ingest route.\\r\\n- **LSAT Lab transcription and mega-set:** require verified capture before advancing the current item; preserve deletion authorization and reconcile obsolete Mac shortcuts/log paths with the actual Windows workflow.\\r\\n- **NotebookLM:** reconcile `task_id` versus `artifact_id` across the described polling/download steps. This is a static contract inconsistency, not a live service failure claim.\\r\\n- **Plugin helpers:** reconcile optional grep with `pipefail`, settings reload guidance and wrapper contracts; native host tool discovery still requires verification.\\r\\n- **Design taste:** define or remove the undefined `LAYOUT_VARIANCE` parameter and make perpetual motion an explicit aesthetic choice. Emil\'s design guidance already contains reduced-motion instructions, so missing reduced-motion support is not a supported finding.\\r\\n- **Skill writing:** preserve unrelated work instead of treating deletion of every untested existing implementation as universally appropriate. Keep specialized creator and plugin-development knowledge as references rather than flattening it into one generic skill.\\r\\n\\r\\n## AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md\\r\\n\\r\\nBoth current root manuals were read in full. Shared routing and guardrails agree in the inspected text; runtime identity differences are deliberate. They define the canonical root, immutable source evidence, outside-vault application homes, dual-runtime copies, explicit deletion permission and live-system authority. No manual changes were needed for this audit. Legacy skill instructions should adapt to those authorities. The old session-handoff F26 drift is resolved and is not a current defect.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Safe behavioral evidence\\r\\n\\r\\nThe parent ran an isolated temporary Git repository: commit one file, then edit it without committing. Predicted the committed three-dot range would miss the edit while worktree diff would show it. Observed `git diff HEAD...HEAD` was empty; `git diff` contained `+changed`. This demonstrates the documented review scope defect. The proposed correction is to add explicit worktree/staged/untracked inventory, not change the production skills during an audit. The temporary fixture was cleaned by its isolated TemporaryDirectory lifecycle; no AI OS commit or mutation occurred.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe parent also ran `gws gmail +send --help`, which explicitly supports `--draft` and `--dry-run`. The booking draft flag is valid. No message or draft was sent/created. The parent also ran `gws forms forms --help`: `batchUpdate` is available while `update` is absent, and `create` copies only title fields. This supports the Forms instruction and empty-form recipe findings without making a live API write.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Seven-rule grades\\r\\n\\r\\nBased on the [reviewed method](../../wiki/karpathy/rules.md), not a claim about Karpathy\'s endorsement. See the independent [parent review and behavioral receipt](PARENT-REVIEW.md).\\r\\n\\r\\n| Rule | Grade | Concrete evidence |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| Mechanism reconstruction | Pass | Trace request, trigger, selected route and resulting side effect; show inspection vs assignment counterexample. |\\r\\n| Small baseline | Pass | One file with one uncommitted edit isolates commit-range review scope. |\\r\\n| Prediction/run/comparison | Pass | Parent predicted empty committed range vs visible worktree diff, then observed both. |\\r\\n| Counterexample/fix | Pass for diagnostic proposal | Fixture exposes the omission and worktree comparison corrects the diagnostic coverage. Production instruction repair is intentionally not applied. |\\r\\n| Relevant execution | Pass within audit scope | Safe Git fixture, actual CLI help, filesystem/hash/coverage checks. No claim live skill workflows ran. |\\r\\n| Explicit assumptions | Pass | Static routing is separated from native selection; telemetry, payload and external cache limits explicit. |\\r\\n| Justified simplicity | Pass | Consolidate repeated entry routes while preserving specialist knowledge and deliberate runtime differences. |\\r\\n\\r\\n## What I ran\\r\\n\\r\\nCatalog/global entry inventory, full semantic body and complete variant reviews, hashes and coverage reconciliation, selected support/route probes, current manuals/PARITY checks, parent isolated Git fixture and read-only GWS help.\\r\\n\\r\\n## What came out\\r\\n\\r\\nRanked repair and cleanup recommendations, exact per-entry dispositions and scope evidence. The main risk is stale or overbroad instructions, not simply the number of files.\\r\\n\\r\\n## What I changed\\r\\n\\r\\nAudit reports and evidence ledgers only. No skill was deleted, deactivated, rewritten, committed or published; no live data or configuration was changed by this audit.\\r\\n\\r\\n"}'}]

text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"python -c \"from pathlib import Path; import sys; sys.stdout.reconfigure(encoding='utf-8'); names='lsatu-cc-handoff chat-export code-review requesting-code-review guided-drill-assignment consultation-inquiry-drafts consultation-followup-drafts qa test-driven-development resolving-merge-conflicts setup-pre-commit bootcamp-parity-audit decision-mapping research-deep using-git-worktrees'.split(); root=Path('skills/claude'); [(print('\\n### '+n+'\\n'+(root/n/'SKILL.md').read_text(encoding='utf-8'))) for n in names if (root/n/'SKILL.md').exists()]\"",max_output_tokens:24000}));
