[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 4.6 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Warning: truncated output (original token count: 17577)\nTotal output lines: 1\n\n{"chunk_id":"55af21","wall_time_seconds":0.4271216,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":16678,"output":"\\r\\n### lsatu-cc-handoff\\r\\n---\\r\\nname: lsatu-cc-handoff\\r\\ndescription: Operational skill for the LSAT U build loop. Trigger whenever Joshua is working on the LSAT U codebase and needs to translate a UI screenshot, complaint, idea, or bug into a Claude Code execution prompt. Also trigger on phrases like \\"send to CC,\\" \\"paste box,\\" \\"create a CC handoff,\\" \\"fix this and send to terminal,\\" \\"make a CC prompt,\\" \\"send this to claude code,\\" or whenever Joshua sends a screenshot of lsatprep.study or the lovable.dev preview with a complaint or change request. The skill enforces the analyze then write-brief-to-vault then give-paste-box pattern, the file path conventions, the hard rules (no em dashes, verbatim content, brutalist palette, no silent errors, direct commits), and Joshua\'s voice rules (terse, opinionated, no em dashes, modules not phases). This skill is the indexed front door for the build loop. The deeper rules live in LSATU memory and the lsat-u-architecture skill.\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# LSAT U Build Loop: Claude Code Handoff\\r\\n\\r\\nThis is the operational skill for translating Joshua\'s input into a Claude Code execution prompt. Use it every time the loop runs.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe loop: input -> analyze -> write brief to vault -> hand paste box back to Joshua -> CC executes -> Joshua returns with results.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Phase 1: Analyze before writing\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore writing a single line of brief, do all of the following:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Read the actual current code.** Do not write recommendations from memory. The LSATU repo is at `~/ai-os-projects/lsatu/`. The topic modules live in `src/topics/<topic>/`. Read the relevant view files, data files, and store files before recommending changes.\\r\\n\\r\\n2. **Read the existing vault docs.** Curriculum work lives in `~/ai-os/raw/sources/lsat-u/curriculum/<topic>/`. Look for `_BUILD_SPEC*.md`, `MODULE_PLAN.md`, `_HANDOFF/`, `_source/`, `_archive/`. Existing briefs that contradict the new direction must be archived (move to `_archive/`).\\r\\n\\r\\n3. **Verify content authenticity.** Joshua authors the LSAT curriculum. Claude does not invent LSAT content. When auditing quiz files, drill banks, or lesson body content, check whether items are verbatim from `_source/` files or from git history (e.g. `git show <sha>:<path>`), or if they were fabricated by a prior Claude Code run. Flag fabrications to a `_CONTENT_AUDIT.md` rather than silently rewriting.\\r\\n\\r\\n4. **If a screenshot was provided.** Describe what is visible. List specific visual problems with concrete CSS-class-level fixes. Cross-reference against the rendering component (LessonBody.tsx, ModuleView.tsx, the per-quiz renderers) to identify the root cause.\\r\\n\\r\\n5. **Push back where appropriate.** If Joshua\'s stated direction will introduce complexity (adding features, more sidebar entries, more drill types, more chrome), say so. The bootcamp default is simpler-not-more. Brutalist preservation is non-negotiable but everything else should reduce cognitive load.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Phase 2: Write the build brief to vault\\r\\n\\r\\nFile path convention: `~/ai-os/raw/sources/lsat-u/curriculum/<topic>/<BRIEF_NAME>.md`\\r\\n\\r\\nName patterns Joshua uses:\\r\\n- `MODULE_PLAN.md` (architecture-defining)\\r\\n- `<DOMAIN>_<ACTION>.md` like `LESSON_READABILITY_FIX.md`, `MODULE_QUALITY_UPGRADE.md`, `MODULE_CLOSEOUT.md`\\r\\n- `_AUDIT_<DATE>.md` for audit findings\\r\\n- `_HANDOFF/<NN>_<NAME>_BRIEF.md` only if Joshua already has that subfolder convention going\\r\\n\\r\\n**Required sections in every brief:**\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Frontmatter line: `For: Claude Code`, `Direct commits to main. Push after each step.`\\r\\n2. Goal: one or two sentences.\\r\\n3. Required reading: explicit list of files CC must read before writing code. Always includes the brief itself, the relevant view files, the architecture spec (`_BUILD_SPEC_v2.0.md` or `MODULE_PLAN.md`), and `~/ai-os/CLAUDE.md`.\\r\\n4. Hard rules. Always include:\\r\\n   - No em dashes. `grep -rn \'—\' src/topics/<topic>/` must return zero before push.\\r\\n   - Verbatim curriculum content. Do not paraphrase walkthroughs, vocab callouts, worked examples, or LSAT stimuli.\\r\\n   - Brutalist palette preserved. Same `brutal-*` tokens. Same `.topic-abstract` wrapper.\\r\\n   - Every Supabase mutation returns `{ ok, error }`. No silent errors. Errors surface as toasts.\\r\\n   - No `(supabase as any)`. Regenerate types if schema changes.\\r\\n   - Direct commits to main. Push at the end of each step.\\r\\n   - Do not invent LSAT content. Stop and ask if a source is missing.\\r\\n5. Numbered steps. Always called \\"Step 1, Step 2, ...\\" not \\"Phase 1, Phase 2.\\" Joshua dislikes the word \\"phase.\\"\\r\\n6. Per step: target files, what changes, expected outcome, commit message.\\r\\n7. Verification block: explicit checklist for the final push (em dash check, build, dev walk, mobile viewport, reduced motion, keyboard-only when relevant).\\r\\n8. Done-state report template using fenced code block with `DONE:` header, commits list, verification checks, open issues.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Brief length target:** 100 to 250 lines. Tight. Concrete. No filler.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Archive superseded briefs.** When a new brief replaces a prior plan in the same domain, move the old one to `_archive/<NAME>_superseded.md`. The vault stays clean.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Phase 3: Hand the paste box back to Joshua\\r\\n\\r\\nAfter the brief is saved, give Joshua a paste-box prompt as a markdown code block. The prompt must:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Reference the brief by full vault path with `~/Documents/Obsidian\\\\ Vault/...` (escape the space).\\r\\n2. Tell CC to \\"execute end to end. Commit and push after each step.\\"\\r\\n3. Restate the hard rules in one short paragraph.\\r\\n4. List the steps in one-line summaries.\\r\\n5. Note any migrations or external actions required (e.g., \\"Apply migration via Supabase dashboard if CLI not linked\\").\\r\\n6. End with: \\"If a step blocks, stop and post status. Otherwise run all N steps and post the done-state report. Go.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n**The paste-box format is the deliverable Joshua actually uses.** It must be inside a single fenced code block so he can copy with one click. No surrounding commentary inside the code block.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Phase 4: Voice and tone rules when writing to Joshua\\r\\n\\r\\n- No em dashes anywhere. Use colons, periods, or restructure.\\r\\n- Terse. Direct. Strong opinionated recommendations, not hedged suggestions.\\r\\n- Push back when Joshua\'s direction would introduce complexity or break existing rules.\\r\\n- Skills-check rule: before creating, modifying, or recommending any skill, read `~/ai-os/raw/sources/lsat-u/skills-reference/00_skills-strategy.md` and `01_skills-detailed-evaluation.md` first. Conform to documented patterns.\\r\\n- Modules not phases.\\r\\n- Don\'t repeat what already shipped. Just acknowledge and move on.\\r\\n- Don\'t ask multiple-choice questions when Joshua has given clear autonomy. Make the call, document it, move.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Common brief types\\r\\n\\r\\n**Audit brief** — finding gaps in shipped work. Output: `_AUDIT_<DATE>.md` plus a follow-up fix brief.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Build brief** — implementing new feature or major restructure. Output: `<DOMAIN>_PLAN.md` or `<DOMAIN>_<FEATURE>.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Fix brief** — closing specific bugs or visual issues. Output: `<DOMAIN>_FIX.md` or `<DOMAIN>_READABILITY_FIX.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Closeout brief** — finishing items left open by a prior pass. Output: `<DOMAIN>_CLOSEOUT.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Cleanup brief** — removing zombies, archiving superseded files, regenerating types. Output: `<DOMAIN>_CLEANUP.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Anti-patterns to avoid\\r\\n\\r\\n- Writing a brief without reading the current code first.\\r\\n- Using \\"Phase 1, Phase 2\\" language. Always \\"Step 1, Step 2\\" or \\"Module 1, Module 2.\\"\\r\\n- Adding more sidebar entries when the conversation context is \\"simplify.\\"\\r\\n- Including em dashes in vault docs or paste boxes.\\r\\n- Writing paste boxes that span multiple code blocks or include surrounding prose inside the fenced block.\\r\\n- Inventing LSAT content. Joshua writes the curriculum.\\r\\n- Skipping the archive step when a brief supersedes prior work.\\r\\n- Asking multiple questions when Joshua has said \\"take autonomy\\" or \\"just do it.\\"\\r\\n- Putting the brief in `/mnt/user-data/outputs/` or anywhere outside the vault. Vault path is the source of truth.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Pointers to deeper sources\\r\\n\\r\\n- LSATU architecture rules: `C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/skills/lsat-u-architecture/SKILL.md`\\r\\n- LSATU repo: `~/ai-os-projects/lsatu/`\\r\\n- Abstract bootcamp vault: `~/ai-os/raw/sources/lsat-u/curriculum/Abstract/`\\r\\n- Skills strategy: `~/ai-os/raw/sources/lsat-u/skills-reference/00_skills-strategy.md`\\r\\n- Claude folder system: `~/ai-os-projects/`, `~/ai-os/raw/sources/lsat-u/prompts/`, `~/ai-os/raw/inbox/chat-exports/`, `~/ai-os/archive/`\\r\\n- Cowork prompts: `~/ai-os/raw/sources/lsat-u/prompts/`\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen in doubt about a convention, check the lsat-u-architecture skill or the Abstract vault for an existing example. Then conform.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n### chat-export\\r\\n---\\r\\nname: chat-export\\r\\ndescription: Save the current conversation as a structured markdown file and generate a handoff prompt for continuing in a new chat. Use this skill whenever the user says \\"export this chat\\", \\"save this conversation\\", \\"create a handoff\\", \\"save this for later\\", \\"export to my chat exports folder\\", or anything indicating they want to preserve a conversation and carry context into a new session. Always trigger this skill proactively when a conversation has been long, complex, or has produced decisions, files, or code — even if the user hasn\'t explicitly asked yet.\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Chat Export Skill\\r\\n\\r\\nSaves the current conversation as a structured markdown file to\\r\\n`C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/handoffs/` with a full transcript,\\r\\nsummary, and a paste-ready handoff prompt.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Output path\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\nC:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/handoffs/YYYY-MM-DD_short-title.md\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nUse today\'s date. Make the short title 3-5 words, kebab-case,\\r\\ndescribing what the chat was about.\\r\\n\\r\\nExample: `2026-04-28_claude-folder-cleanup-notebooklm.md`\\r\\n\\r\\n## Steps\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Reconstruct the full transcript** from the conversation in context.\\r\\n   Write every user message and every Claude reply verbatim under\\r\\n   `### User` and `### Claude` headings. Do not paraphrase or skip.\\r\\n\\r\\n2. **Write the structured summary** (see template below).\\r\\n\\r\\n3. **Write the handoff prompt** — a single paste-ready block the user\\r\\n   can drop into a new chat to resume instantly. It should:\\r\\n   - Point to the file: `Read C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/handoffs/<filename>.md`\\r\\n   - State what was done\\r\\n   - State what to do next (ask the user if unclear)\\r\\n\\r\\n4. **Save the file** using Desktop Commander\'s `write_file` to the\\r\\n   output path above.\\r\\n\\r\\n5. **Tell the user**:\\r\\n   - The filename\\r\\n   - The handoff prompt (show it in a code block so it\'s easy to copy)\\r\\n\\r\\n## File template\\r\\n\\r\\n```markdown\\r\\n# <Chat title>\\r\\n\\r\\n**Date:** YYYY-MM-DD\\r\\n**Topic:** one-line description\\r\\n**Tags:** (pick relevant: lsat-u | lsat-content | applications | tooling | drive-organizer | theology | general)\\r\\n**File:** C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/handoffs/<filename>.md\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## Summary\\r\\n\\r\\nOne paragraph. What was this chat about, what was the goal, what was accomplished.\\r\\n\\r\\n## What was decided / built\\r\\n\\r\\n- bullet list of concrete outcomes, decisions, files created, commands run\\r\\n\\r\\n## Open threads\\r\\n\\r\\n- things not finished\\r\\n- questions still unresolved\\r\\n- next steps\\r\\n\\r\\n## Handoff prompt\\r\\n\\r\\n> Paste this into a new chat to continue:\\r\\n>\\r\\n> ```\\r\\n> Read C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/handoffs/<filename>.md and continue\\r\\n> from where we left off. [Specific next thing to do goes here.]\\r\\n> ```\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## Full transcript\\r\\n\\r\\n### User\\r\\n[message]\\r\\n\\r\\n### Claude\\r\\n[reply]\\r\\n\\r\\n[...repeat for every message in the conversation...]\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n## Notes\\r\\n\\r\\n- Never truncate the transcript. If it\'s long, write all of it.\\r\\n- The \\"Open threads\\" section is the most important part for handoffs —\\r\\n  be precise about what is unfinished.\\r\\n- If the user specifies a subfolder (e.g. \\"save this under lsat-u\\"),\\r\\n  save to `C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/handoffs/lsat-u/<filename>.md`\\r\\n  and create the folder if it doesn\'t exist.\\r\\n- After saving, always show the handoff prompt in a fenced code block\\r\\n  so the user can copy it in one click.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n### code-review\\r\\n---\\r\\nname: code-review\\r\\ndescription: \\"Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes: Standards (does the code follow this repo\'s documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/spec asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to \\\\\\"review since X\\\\\\".\\"\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\nTwo-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Standards**: does the code conform to this repo\'s documented coding standards?\\r\\n- **Spec**: does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / spec?\\r\\n\\r\\nBoth axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don\'t pollute each other\'s context, then this skill aggregates their findings.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe issue tracker should have been provided to you. If not, tell the user to run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Process\\r\\n\\r\\n### 1. Pin the fixed point\\r\\n\\r\\nWhatever the user said is the fixed point (a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc.). If they didn\'t specify one, ask for it.\\r\\n\\r\\nCapture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here, not inside two parallel sub-agents.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 2. Identify the spec source\\r\\n\\r\\nLook for the originating spec, in this order:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.), fetched via the workflow in the tracker doc.\\r\\n2. A path the user passed as an argument.\\r\\n3. A spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.\\r\\n4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn\'t one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report \\"no spec available\\".\\r\\n\\r\\n### 3. Identify the standards sources\\r\\n\\r\\nSearch the repo for every file that documents how code should be written. When `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md` exists, it must be on the list.\\r\\n\\r\\nOn top of whatever the repo documents, the Standards axis always carries the **smell baseline** below: a fixed set of Fowler code smells (_Refactoring_, ch.3) that applies even when a repo documents nothing. Two rules bind it:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **The repo overrides.** A documented repo standard always wins; where it endorses something the baseline would flag, suppress the smell.\\r\\n- **Always a judgement call.** Each smell is a labelled heuristic (\\"possible Feature Envy\\"), never a hard violation. Like any standard here, skip anything tooling already enforces.\\r\\n\\r\\nEach smell reads *what it is* → *how to fix*; match it against the diff:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Mysterious Name**: a function, variable, or type whose name doesn\'t reveal what it does or holds. → rename it; if no honest name comes, the design\'s murky.\\r\\n- **Duplicated Code**: the same logic shape appears in more than one hunk or file in the change. → extract the shared shape, call it from both.\\r\\n- **Feature Envy**: a method that reaches into another object\'s data more than its own. → move the method onto the data it envies.\\r\\n- **Data Clumps**: the same few fields or params keep travelling together (a type wanting to be born). → bundle them into one type, pass that.\\r\\n- **Primitive Obsession**: a primitive or string standing in for a domain concept that deserves its own type. → give the concept its own small type.\\r\\n- **Repeated Switches**: the same `switch`/`if`-cascade on the same type recurs across the change. → replace with polymorphism, or one map both sites share.\\r\\n- **Shotgun Surgery**: one logical change forces scattered edits across many files in the diff. → gather what changes together into one module.\\r\\n- **Divergent Change**: one file or module is edited for several unrelated reasons. → split so each module changes for one reason.\\r\\n- **Speculative Generality**: abstraction, parameters, or hooks added for needs the spec doesn\'t have. → delete it; inline back until a real need shows.\\r\\n- **Message Chains**: long `a.b().c().d()` navigation the caller shouldn\'t depend on. → hide the walk behind one method on the first object.\\r\\n- **Middle Man**: a class or function that mostly just delegates onward. → cut it, call the real target direct.\\r\\n- **Refused Bequest**: a subclass or implementer that ignores or overrides most of what it inherits. → drop the inheritance, use composition.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel\\r\\n\\r\\nIssue both sub-agent calls together, in the foreground, and aggregate the reports they return.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Standards sub-agent prompt** should include:\\r\\n\\r\\n- The full diff command and commit list.\\r\\n- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3, **plus the smell baseline from step 3** pasted in full (the sub-agent has no other access to it).\\r\\n- The brief: \\"Report, per file/hunk where relevant, (a) every place the diff violates a documented standard: cite the standard (file + the rule); and (b) any baseline smell you spot: name it and quote the hunk. Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls: documented-standard breaches can be hard, but baseline smells are always judgement calls, and a documented repo standard overrides the baseline. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n**Spec sub-agent prompt** should include:\\r\\n\\r\\n- The diff command and commit list.\\r\\n- The path or fetched contents of the spec.\\r\\n- The brief: \\"Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn\'t asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\nIf the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 5. Aggregate\\r\\n\\r\\nPresent the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings, because the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).\\r\\n\\r\\nEnd with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don\'t pick a single winner across axes: that\'s the reranking the separation exists to prevent.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Why two axes\\r\\n\\r\\nA change can pass one axis and fail the other:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing → **Standards pass, Spec fail.**\\r\\n- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project\'s conventions → **Spec pass, Standards fail.**\\r\\n\\r\\nReporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n### requesting-code-review\\r\\n---\\r\\nname: requesting-code-review\\r\\ndescription: Use when completing tasks, implementing major features, or before merging to verify work meets requirements\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Requesting Code Review\\r\\n\\r\\nDispatch a code reviewer subagent to catch issues before they cascade. The reviewer gets precisely crafted context for evaluation — never your session\'s history. This keeps the reviewer focused o…7577 tokens truncated…ready manually tested all the edge cases\\"**\\r\\n\\r\\nManual testing is ad-hoc. You think you tested everything but:\\r\\n- No record of what you tested\\r\\n- Can\'t re-run when code changes\\r\\n- Easy to forget cases under pressure\\r\\n- \\"It worked when I tried it\\" ≠ comprehensive\\r\\n\\r\\nAutomated tests are systematic. They run the same way every time.\\r\\n\\r\\n**\\"Deleting X hours of work is wasteful\\"**\\r\\n\\r\\nSunk cost fallacy. The time is already gone. Your choice now:\\r\\n- Delete and rewrite with TDD (X more hours, high confidence)\\r\\n- Keep it and add tests after (30 min, low confidence, likely bugs)\\r\\n\\r\\nThe \\"waste\\" is keeping code you can\'t trust. Working code without real tests is technical debt.\\r\\n\\r\\n**\\"TDD is dogmatic, being pragmatic means adapting\\"**\\r\\n\\r\\nTDD IS pragmatic:\\r\\n- Finds bugs before commit (faster than debugging after)\\r\\n- Prevents regressions (tests catch breaks immediately)\\r\\n- Documents behavior (tests show how to use code)\\r\\n- Enables refactoring (change freely, tests catch breaks)\\r\\n\\r\\n\\"Pragmatic\\" shortcuts = debugging in production = slower.\\r\\n\\r\\n**\\"Tests after achieve the same goals - it\'s spirit not ritual\\"**\\r\\n\\r\\nNo. Tests-after answer \\"What does this do?\\" Tests-first answer \\"What should this do?\\"\\r\\n\\r\\nTests-after are biased by your implementation. You test what you built, not what\'s required. You verify remembered edge cases, not discovered ones.\\r\\n\\r\\nTests-first force edge case discovery before implementing. Tests-after verify you remembered everything (you didn\'t).\\r\\n\\r\\n30 minutes of tests after ≠ TDD. You get coverage, lose proof tests work.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Common Rationalizations\\r\\n\\r\\n| Excuse | Reality |\\r\\n|--------|---------|\\r\\n| \\"Too simple to test\\" | Simple code breaks. Test takes 30 seconds. |\\r\\n| \\"I\'ll test after\\" | Tests passing immediately prove nothing. |\\r\\n| \\"Tests after achieve same goals\\" | Tests-after = \\"what does this do?\\" Tests-first = \\"what should this do?\\" |\\r\\n| \\"Already manually tested\\" | Ad-hoc ≠ systematic. No record, can\'t re-run. |\\r\\n| \\"Deleting X hours is wasteful\\" | Sunk cost fallacy. Keeping unverified code is technical debt. |\\r\\n| \\"Keep as reference, write tests first\\" | You\'ll adapt it. That\'s testing after. Delete means delete. |\\r\\n| \\"Need to explore first\\" | Fine. Throw away exploration, start with TDD. |\\r\\n| \\"Test hard = design unclear\\" | Listen to test. Hard to test = hard to use. |\\r\\n| \\"TDD will slow me down\\" | TDD faster than debugging. Pragmatic = test-first. |\\r\\n| \\"Manual test faster\\" | Manual doesn\'t prove edge cases. You\'ll re-test every change. |\\r\\n| \\"Existing code has no tests\\" | You\'re improving it. Add tests for existing code. |\\r\\n\\r\\n## Red Flags - STOP and Start Over\\r\\n\\r\\n- Code before test\\r\\n- Test after implementation\\r\\n- Test passes immediately\\r\\n- Can\'t explain why test failed\\r\\n- Tests added \\"later\\"\\r\\n- Rationalizing \\"just this once\\"\\r\\n- \\"I already manually tested it\\"\\r\\n- \\"Tests after achieve the same purpose\\"\\r\\n- \\"It\'s about spirit not ritual\\"\\r\\n- \\"Keep as reference\\" or \\"adapt existing code\\"\\r\\n- \\"Already spent X hours, deleting is wasteful\\"\\r\\n- \\"TDD is dogmatic, I\'m being pragmatic\\"\\r\\n- \\"This is different because...\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n**All of these mean: Delete code. Start over with TDD.**\\r\\n\\r\\n## Example: Bug Fix\\r\\n\\r\\n**Bug:** Empty email accepted\\r\\n\\r\\n**RED**\\r\\n```typescript\\r\\ntest(\'rejects empty email\', async () => {\\r\\n  const result = await submitForm({ email: \'\' });\\r\\n  expect(result.error).toBe(\'Email required\');\\r\\n});\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**Verify RED**\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n$ npm test\\r\\nFAIL: expected \'Email required\', got undefined\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**GREEN**\\r\\n```typescript\\r\\nfunction submitForm(data: FormData) {\\r\\n  if (!data.email?.trim()) {\\r\\n    return { error: \'Email required\' };\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  // ...\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**Verify GREEN**\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n$ npm test\\r\\nPASS\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**REFACTOR**\\r\\nExtract validation for multiple fields if needed.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Verification Checklist\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore marking work complete:\\r\\n\\r\\n- [ ] Every new function/method has a test\\r\\n- [ ] Watched each test fail before implementing\\r\\n- [ ] Each test failed for expected reason (feature missing, not typo)\\r\\n- [ ] Wrote minimal code to pass each test\\r\\n- [ ] All tests pass\\r\\n- [ ] Output pristine (no errors, warnings)\\r\\n- [ ] Tests use real code (mocks only if unavoidable)\\r\\n- [ ] Edge cases and errors covered\\r\\n\\r\\nCan\'t check all boxes? You skipped TDD. Start over.\\r\\n\\r\\n## When Stuck\\r\\n\\r\\n| Problem | Solution |\\r\\n|---------|----------|\\r\\n| Don\'t know how to test | Write wished-for API. Write assertion first. Ask your human partner. |\\r\\n| Test too complicated | Design too complicated. Simplify interface. |\\r\\n| Must mock everything | Code too coupled. Use dependency injection. |\\r\\n| Test setup huge | Extract helpers. Still complex? Simplify design. |\\r\\n\\r\\n## Debugging Integration\\r\\n\\r\\nBug found? Write failing test reproducing it. Follow TDD cycle. Test proves fix and prevents regression.\\r\\n\\r\\nNever fix bugs without a test.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Testing Anti-Patterns\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen adding mocks or test utilities, read [testing-anti-patterns.md](testing-anti-patterns.md) to avoid common pitfalls:\\r\\n- Testing mock behavior instead of real behavior\\r\\n- Adding test-only methods to production classes\\r\\n- Mocking without understanding dependencies\\r\\n\\r\\n## Final Rule\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\nProduction code → test exists and failed first\\r\\nOtherwise → not TDD\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nNo exceptions without your human partner\'s permission.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n### resolving-merge-conflicts\\r\\n---\\r\\nname: resolving-merge-conflicts\\r\\ndescription: \\"Use when you need to resolve an in-progress git merge/rebase conflict.\\"\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **See the current state** of the merge/rebase. Check git history, and the conflicting files.\\r\\n\\r\\n2. **Find the primary sources** for each conflict. Understand deeply why each change was made, and what the original intent was. Read the commit messages, check the PRs, check original issues/tickets.\\r\\n\\r\\n3. **Resolve each hunk.** Preserve both intents where possible. Where incompatible, pick the one matching the merge\'s stated goal and note the trade-off. Do **not** invent new behaviour. Always resolve; never `--abort`.\\r\\n\\r\\n4. Discover the project\'s **automated checks** and run them, typically typecheck, then tests, then format. Fix anything the merge broke.\\r\\n\\r\\n5. **Finish the merge/rebase.** Stage everything and commit. If rebasing, continue the rebase process until all commits are rebased.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n### setup-pre-commit\\r\\n---\\r\\nname: setup-pre-commit\\r\\ndescription: Set up Husky pre-commit hooks with lint-staged (Prettier), type checking, and tests in the current repo. Use when user wants to add pre-commit hooks, set up Husky, configure lint-staged, or add commit-time formatting/typechecking/testing.\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Setup Pre-Commit Hooks\\r\\n\\r\\n## What This Sets Up\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Husky** pre-commit hook\\r\\n- **lint-staged** running Prettier on all staged files\\r\\n- **Prettier** config (if missing)\\r\\n- **typecheck** and **test** scripts in the pre-commit hook\\r\\n\\r\\n## Steps\\r\\n\\r\\n### 1. Detect package manager\\r\\n\\r\\nCheck for `package-lock.json` (npm), `pnpm-lock.yaml` (pnpm), `yarn.lock` (yarn), `bun.lockb` (bun). Use whichever is present. Default to npm if unclear.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 2. Install dependencies\\r\\n\\r\\nInstall as devDependencies:\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\nhusky lint-staged prettier\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n### 3. Initialize Husky\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\nnpx husky init\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThis creates `.husky/` dir and adds `prepare: \\"husky\\"` to package.json.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 4. Create `.husky/pre-commit`\\r\\n\\r\\nWrite this file (no shebang needed for Husky v9+):\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\nnpx lint-staged\\r\\nnpm run typecheck\\r\\nnpm run test\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**Adapt**: Replace `npm` with detected package manager. If repo has no `typecheck` or `test` script in package.json, omit those lines and tell the user.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 5. Create `.lintstagedrc`\\r\\n\\r\\n```json\\r\\n{\\r\\n  \\"*\\": \\"prettier --ignore-unknown --write\\"\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n### 6. Create `.prettierrc` (if missing)\\r\\n\\r\\nOnly create if no Prettier config exists. Use these defaults:\\r\\n\\r\\n```json\\r\\n{\\r\\n  \\"useTabs\\": false,\\r\\n  \\"tabWidth\\": 2,\\r\\n  \\"printWidth\\": 80,\\r\\n  \\"singleQuote\\": false,\\r\\n  \\"trailingComma\\": \\"es5\\",\\r\\n  \\"semi\\": true,\\r\\n  \\"arrowParens\\": \\"always\\"\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n### 7. Verify\\r\\n\\r\\n- [ ] `.husky/pre-commit` exists and is executable\\r\\n- [ ] `.lintstagedrc` exists\\r\\n- [ ] `prepare` script in package.json is `\\"husky\\"`\\r\\n- [ ] `prettier` config exists\\r\\n- [ ] Run `npx lint-staged` to verify it works\\r\\n\\r\\n### 8. Commit\\r\\n\\r\\nStage all changed/created files and commit with message: `Add pre-commit hooks (husky + lint-staged + prettier)`\\r\\n\\r\\nThis will run through the new pre-commit hooks: a good smoke test that everything works.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Notes\\r\\n\\r\\n- Husky v9+ doesn\'t need shebangs in hook files\\r\\n- `prettier --ignore-unknown` skips files Prettier can\'t parse (images, etc.)\\r\\n- The pre-commit runs lint-staged first (fast, staged-only), then full typecheck and tests\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n### bootcamp-parity-audit\\r\\n---\\r\\nname: bootcamp-parity-audit\\r\\ndescription: Audit a given LSAT U bootcamp\'s feature and visual parity against the flagship Intro to LR (Main Conclusion) bootcamp, anchored to the quality bar doc. Use when Joshua asks to \\"compare against Intro to LR\\", \\"check parity\\", \\"audit [bootcamp name]\\", or wants to know how far a bootcamp is from shipped quality. Trigger with /bootcamp-parity-audit <bootcamp-name>.\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\nAudit a named LSAT U bootcamp against the flagship Intro to LR (Main Conclusion) bootcamp and the shared quality bar, and report the delta.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Inputs\\r\\n\\r\\nThe bootcamp name/slug (e.g. \\"Abstract\\", \\"Flaw & Famous Flaws\\", \\"Causation Station\\"). If not given, ask which one, once, don\'t guess.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Anchor documents (read these first, in order)\\r\\n\\r\\n1. `~/ai-os/archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/synthesis/bootcamp-quality-bar.md` — the shared bar every bootcamp finishes to.\\r\\n2. `~/ai-os/archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/synthesis/intro-to-lr-product.md` — the flagship\'s product spec (what \\"done\\" looks like).\\r\\n3. `~/ai-os/archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/synthesis/intro-to-lr-design.md` — the flagship\'s design system (colors, type, role tokens).\\r\\n4. `~/ai-os/wiki/entities/intro-to-lr-bootcamp.md` if it exists, for entity-level facts.\\r\\n5. Whatever entity page exists for the target bootcamp under `wiki/entities/` — read it if present; if it doesn\'t exist yet, say so, that itself is a gap.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Codebase pass\\r\\n\\r\\nIn the LSATU repo (`C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu/`):\\r\\n1. Find the target bootcamp\'s source directory under `src/bootcamps/`.\\r\\n2. Compare its file/component structure against the Intro to LR structure (`src/bootcamps/main-conclusion/`) at a glance, don\'t read every file, sample the key surfaces (overview, drill/practice flow, data layer).\\r\\n3. Note any surface Intro to LR has that the target bootcamp lacks entirely.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Output: 5-point delta report\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua\'s preferred format (per past sessions):\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\n1. Feature parity: <what Intro to LR has that this bootcamp is missing>\\r\\n2. Visual parity: <design-system deviations from intro-to-lr-design.md — colors, type, spacing, tokens>\\r\\n3. Content/pedagogy parity: <coaching tone, teach-first-calibrate-after gaps vs teaching-voice.md>\\r\\n4. Data/quality-bar gaps: <fake data, dead buttons, error handling — anything bootcamp-quality-bar.md flags>\\r\\n5. Verdict: <shipped-quality / in-progress / needs-work, one line, plus the single highest-leverage next fix>\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n## Rules\\r\\n\\r\\n- No em dashes. Terse per finding, don\'t pad with rationale unless asked.\\r\\n- Diagnosis only unless Joshua asks you to fix something. Report the delta, don\'t start building.\\r\\n- Don\'t re-read the full 216-file Intro to LR tree each time; use the entity/synthesis docs as the source of truth for what \\"done\\" means, and spot-check the codebase only where the docs are ambiguous.\\r\\n- If the target bootcamp has no entity page in `wiki/entities/`, flag that as a documentation gap in point 5 rather than silently skipping it.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n### decision-mapping\\r\\n---\\r\\nname: decision-mapping\\r\\ndescription: Turn a loose idea into a sequenced map of investigation tickets, then drive them to resolution one at a time.\\r\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\nThis skill is invoked when a loose idea requires more than one agent session to turn into a plan. It creates a stateful decision map in a markdown file, and drives the user through a sequence of tickets to resolve the open questions - which may require either prototyping, research or discussion.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The Decision Map\\r\\n\\r\\nThe decision map is a single compact Markdown file, one per planning effort, git-tracked alongside the project. It is the canonical artifact — the **whole map is loaded as context into every session**, so it must stay compact.\\r\\n\\r\\nAssets created during tickets should be linked to from the map, not duplicated within it.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Structure\\r\\n\\r\\nNumbered entries (\\"tickets\\"), each its own section keyed by its number:\\r\\n\\r\\n```markdown\\r\\n## #1: Relational Or Non-Relational Database?\\r\\n\\r\\nBlocked by: #<ticket-number>, #<ticket-number>\\r\\nType: Research | Prototype | Discuss\\r\\n\\r\\n### Question\\r\\n\\r\\n<question-here>\\r\\n\\r\\n### Answer\\r\\n\\r\\n<answer-here>\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nEach ticket must be sized to one 100K token agent session.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Ticket Types\\r\\n\\r\\nThere are three types of tickets:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Research**: Reading documentation, third-party API\'s, or local resources like knowledge bases. Creates a markdown summary as an asset. Use this when knowledge outside the current working directory is required.\\r\\n- **Prototype**: Writing UI or logic code to test a hypothesis, or to explore a design space. Uses the /prototype skill. Creates a prototype as an asset. Use this when \\"how should it look\\" or \\"how should it behave\\" is the key question.\\r\\n- **Discuss**: Conversation with the agent. Uses the /grilling and /domain-modelling skills. The default case.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Fog of war\\r\\n\\r\\nThe map is _deliberately_ incomplete beyond the frontier. Your job is to investigate the frontier, and to resolve tickets in order to push the frontier forward. Push back the fog of war, one node at a time.\\r\\n\\r\\nAt some point, the fog of war should have been pushed back far enough that the path to the finish line is clear. At that point, no more tickets will be required and the decision map can be considered \'done\'.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Invocation\\r\\n\\r\\nThere are two ways this skill can be invoked: **bootstrap** and **resume**.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Bootstrap\\r\\n\\r\\nUser invokes with a loose idea.\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Run a /grilling and /domain-modelling session to surface the open decisions.\\r\\n2. Write a new decision map — mostly fog, frontier identified, trivially-decidable entries resolved inline.\\r\\n3. Stop. Map-building is one session\'s work; do not also resolve tickets.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Resume\\r\\n\\r\\nUser invokes with a path to an existing map and a ticket number.\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Load the **whole map** as context.\\r\\n2. Run a session to resolve the ticket, invoking skills as needed. If in doubt, use `/grilling` and `/domain-modelling`.\\r\\n3. Record what the session resolved in the ticket\'s body.\\r\\n4. Add newly-discovered tickets (with correct `blocked_by` edges).\\r\\n5. Stop.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf the decisions made invalidate other parts of the map, update or delete those nodes.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Parallelism\\r\\n\\r\\nThe user may choose to run tickets in parallel, so expect other agents to make changes to the map.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Skipping The Decision Map\\r\\n\\r\\nMany times, the initial grilling will result in no fog of war. No unresolved tickets. Nothing to do, except implement.\\r\\n\\r\\nIn those situations, you should offer the user the chance to skip the decision map - since the decision map is only needed if multi-session decisions need to be made.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf they skip it, you should recommend either implementing directly or using `/to-prd` to schedule a multi-session implementation.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n### research-deep\\r\\n---\\r\\nname: research-deep\\r\\ndescription: Read research outline, launch independent agent for each item for deep research. Disable task output.\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Research Deep - Deep Research\\r\\n\\r\\n## Trigger\\r\\n`/research-deep`\\r\\n\\r\\n## Workflow\\r\\n\\r\\n### Step 1: Auto-locate Outline\\r\\nFind `*/outline.yaml` file in current working directory, read items list, execution config (including items_per_agent).\\r\\n\\r\\n### Step 2: Resume Check\\r\\n- Check completed JSON files in output_dir\\r\\n- Skip completed items\\r\\n\\r\\n### Step 3: Batch Execution\\r\\n- Batch by batch_size (need user approval before next batch)\\r\\n- Each agent handles items_per_agent items\\r\\n- Launch web-search-agent (background parallel, disable task output)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Parameter Retrieval**:\\r\\n- `{topic}`: topic field from outline.yaml\\r\\n- `{item_name}`: item\'s name field\\r\\n- `{item_related_info}`: item\'s complete yaml content (name + category + description etc.)\\r\\n- `{output_dir}`: execution.output_dir from outline.yaml (default: ./results)\\r\\n- `{fields_path}`: absolute path to {topic}/fields.yaml\\r\\n- `{output_path}`: absolute path to {output_dir}/{item_name_slug}.json (slugify item_name: replace spaces with _, remove special chars)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Hard Constraint**: The following prompt must be strictly reproduced, only replacing variables in {xxx}, do not modify structure or wording.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Prompt Template**:\\r\\n```python\\r\\nprompt = f\\"\\"\\"## Task\\r\\nResearch {item_related_info}, output structured JSON to {output_path}\\r\\n\\r\\n## Field Definitions\\r\\nRead {fields_path} to get all field definitions\\r\\n\\r\\n## Output Requirements\\r\\n1. Output JSON according to fields defined in fields.yaml\\r\\n2. Mark uncertain field values with [uncertain]\\r\\n3. Add uncertain array at the end of JSON, listing all uncertain field names\\r\\n4. All field values must be in English\\r\\n\\r\\n## Output Path\\r\\n{output_path}\\r\\n\\r\\n## Validation\\r\\nAfter completing JSON output, run validation script to ensure complete field coverage:\\r\\npython ~/ai-os/skills/claude/research/validate_json.py -f {fields_path} -j {output_path}\\r\\nTask is complete only after validation passes.\\r\\n\\"\\"\\"\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**One-shot Example** (assuming researching GitHub Copilot):\\r\\n```\\r\\n## Task\\r\\nResearch name: GitHub Copilot\\r\\ncategory: International Product\\r\\ndescription: Developed by Microsoft/GitHub, first mainstream AI coding assistant, ~40% market share, output structured JSON to {project_dir}/results/GitHub_Copilot.json\\r\\n\\r\\n## Field Definitions\\r\\nRead {project_dir}/fields.yaml to get all field definitions\\r\\n\\r\\n## Output Requirements\\r\\n1. Output JSON according to fields defined in fields.yaml\\r\\n2. Mark uncertain field values with [uncertain]\\r\\n3. Add uncertain array at the end of JSON, listing all uncertain field names\\r\\n4. All field values must be in English\\r\\n\\r\\n## Output Path\\r\\n{project_dir}/results/GitHub_Copilot.json\\r\\n\\r\\n## Validation\\r\\nAfter completing JSON output, run validation script to ensure complete field coverage:\\r\\npython ~/ai-os/skills/claude/research/validate_json.py -f {project_dir}/fields.yaml -j {project_dir}/results/GitHub_Copilot.json\\r\\nTask is complete only after validation passes.\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n### Step 4: Wait and Monitor\\r\\n- Wait for current batch to complete\\r\\n- Launch next batch\\r\\n- Display progress\\r\\n\\r\\n### Step 5: Summary Report\\r\\nAfter all complete, output:\\r\\n- Completion count\\r\\n- Failed/uncertain marked items\\r\\n- Output directory\\r\\n\\r\\n## Agent Config\\r\\n- Background execution: Yes\\r\\n- Task Output: Disabled (agent has explicit output file when complete)\\r\\n- Resume support: Yes\\r\\n\\r\\n"}'}]

'{"chunk_id":"e79a66","wall_time_seconds":0.6177571,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":4426,"output":"\\r\\n### requesting-code-review\\r\\n---\\r\\nname: requesting-code-review\\r\\ndescription: Use when completing tasks, implementing major features, or before merging to verify work meets requirements\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Requesting Code Review\\r\\n\\r\\nDispatch a code reviewer subagent to catch issues before they cascade. The reviewer gets precisely crafted context for evaluation — never your session\'s history. This keeps the reviewer focused on the work product, not your thought process, and preserves your own context for continued work.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Core principle:** Review early, review often.\\r\\n\\r\\n## When to Request Review\\r\\n\\r\\n**Mandatory:**\\r\\n- After each task in subagent-driven development\\r\\n- After completing major feature\\r\\n- Before merge to main\\r\\n\\r\\n**Optional but valuable:**\\r\\n- When stuck (fresh perspective)\\r\\n- Before refactoring (baseline check)\\r\\n- After fixing complex bug\\r\\n\\r\\n## How to Request\\r\\n\\r\\n**1. Get git SHAs:**\\r\\n```bash\\r\\nBASE_SHA=$(git rev-parse HEAD~1)  # or origin/main\\r\\nHEAD_SHA=$(git rev-parse HEAD)\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**2. Dispatch code reviewer subagent:**\\r\\n\\r\\nDispatch a `general-purpose` subagent, filling the template at [code-reviewer.md](code-reviewer.md)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Placeholders:**\\r\\n- `{DESCRIPTION}` - Brief summary of what you built\\r\\n- `{PLAN_OR_REQUIREMENTS}` - What it should do\\r\\n- `{BASE_SHA}` - Starting commit\\r\\n- `{HEAD_SHA}` - Ending commit\\r\\n\\r\\n**3. Act on feedback:**\\r\\n- Fix Critical issues immediately\\r\\n- Fix Important issues before proceeding\\r\\n- Note Minor issues for later\\r\\n- Push back if reviewer is wrong (with reasoning)\\r\\n\\r\\n## Example\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\n[Just completed Task 2: Add verification function]\\r\\n\\r\\nYou: Let me request code review before proceeding.\\r\\n\\r\\nBASE_SHA=$(git log --oneline | grep \\"Task 1\\" | head -1 | awk \'{print $1}\')\\r\\nHEAD_SHA=$(git rev-parse HEAD)\\r\\n\\r\\n[Dispatch code reviewer subagent]\\r\\n  DESCRIPTION: Added verifyIndex() and repairIndex() with 4 issue types\\r\\n  PLAN_OR_REQUIREMENTS: Task 2 from docs/superpowers/plans/deployment-plan.md\\r\\n  BASE_SHA: a7981ec\\r\\n  HEAD_SHA: 3df7661\\r\\n\\r\\n[Subagent returns]:\\r\\n  Strengths: Clean architecture, real tests\\r\\n  Issues:\\r\\n    Important: Missing progress indicators\\r\\n    Minor: Magic number (100) for reporting interval\\r\\n  Assessment: Ready to proceed\\r\\n\\r\\nYou: [Fix progress indicators]\\r\\n[Continue to Task 3]\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n## Integration with Workflows\\r\\n\\r\\n**Subagent-Driven Development:**\\r\\n- Review after EACH task\\r\\n- Catch issues before they compound\\r\\n- Fix before moving to next task\\r\\n\\r\\n**Executing Plans:**\\r\\n- Review after each task or at natural checkpoints\\r\\n- Get feedback, apply, continue\\r\\n\\r\\n**Ad-Hoc Development:**\\r\\n- Review before merge\\r\\n- Review when stuck\\r\\n\\r\\n## Red Flags\\r\\n\\r\\n**Never:**\\r\\n- Skip review because \\"it\'s simple\\"\\r\\n- Ignore Critical issues\\r\\n- Proceed with unfixed Important issues\\r\\n- Argue with valid technical feedback\\r\\n\\r\\n**If reviewer wrong:**\\r\\n- Push back with technical reasoning\\r\\n- Show code/tests that prove it works\\r\\n- Request clarification\\r\\n\\r\\nSee template at: [code-reviewer.md](code-reviewer.md)\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n### guided-drill-assignment\\r\\n---\\r\\nname: guided-drill-assignment\\r\\ndescription: Use this whenever Joshua asks to look at a student\'s transcript, choose an appropriate LSAT U guided drill, verify the drill works, assign it through the platform, or confirm that the student received a clear notification. This skill covers the full workflow from transcript review to guided drill recommendation, platform/browser QA, assignment, and notification verification.\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Guided Drill Assignment\\r\\n\\r\\nUse this skill to turn student performance context into a verified guided drill assignment in LSAT U.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe operating standard is: do not merely recommend a drill. Choose the right drill from the student\'s recent transcript/context, verify the drill actually launches and is wired correctly, assign it through the platform, then verify the student\'s notification surface.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Inputs\\r\\n\\r\\nExpect one or more of:\\r\\n\\r\\n- A student name or shorthand, such as `C`, `C. Alexander`, or another current student.\\r\\n- A request to inspect a recent transcript, chat export, meeting note, student page, or platform record.\\r\\n- A request to find an appropriate guided drill.\\r\\n- A request to assign a drill and make sure the student is notified.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf the student identity is ambiguous and cannot be safely inferred from local context, ask one concise clarifying question before assigning anything.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Required Context\\r\\n\\r\\nFor LSAT U vault work, follow the vault operating order before making a recommendation:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Read `wiki/hot.md`.\\r\\n2. Read `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/README.md`.\\r\\n3. Read `wiki/dashboard.md`.\\r\\n4. Read the relevant student page under `students/`.\\r\\n5. Read the student\'s `assignment-log.md` if it exists so you do not repeat assigned work unless Joshua explicitly asks for a repeat.\\r\\n6. Read `wiki/guided-drill-verification-log.md` so already-verified drills are not re-QA\'d unnecessarily.\\r\\n7. Search all available transcripts, transcript downloads, chat exports, and meeting notes for that student.\\r\\n8. Sort candidate transcript/context files by the actual meeting date, not file modification time, and use the most up-to-date transcript as the primary source of truth.\\r\\n\\r\\nPrefer local sources first. Do not invent student weaknesses or assignment history. If an older transcript conflicts with a newer one, follow the newer transcript and mention the older file only as background.\\r\\n\\r\\nUseful searches:\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\nrg -n \\"student-name-or-shorthand|student full name|student email\\" wiki raw students projects\\r\\nfind students -path \\"*transcript*\\" -type f | rg -i \\"student-slug|student-name\\"\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n## Drill Selection\\r\\n\\r\\nChoose the drill by matching the student\'s most recent need to the drill\'s pedagogical purpose.\\r\\n\\r\\nEvaluate:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Newest transcript evidence by meeting date: missed concepts, confusion, pacing, task avoidance, question type weakness, or reasoning pattern weakness.\\r\\n- Student readiness: assign teach-first drills when the student needs instruction, not a cold test.\\r\\n- Drill metadata: title, slug, week/module, question type, tags, pool size, question count, estimated time.\\r\\n- Platform constraints: student must have classroom and drill access.\\r\\n- Assignment history: avoid assigning a drill already present in the student\'s `assignment-log.md` unless Joshua explicitly wants a repeat or the log says it should be repeated.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen the best drill is not obvious, produce a short ranked recommendation before assigning:\\r\\n\\r\\n```markdown\\r\\nRecommended: <drill title>\\r\\nReason: <one sentence tied to transcript evidence>\\r\\nBackup: <second drill title, only if meaningfully close>\\r\\nDo not assign yet because: <only include if blocked>\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nIf the user has already asked to assign and the best choice is clear, proceed without stopping.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Repo Checks\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore using a drill, verify that it exists in the app data and is wired to the platform.\\r\\n\\r\\nCommon files to inspect:\\r\\n\\r\\n- `data/guided-drills/_seed.json`\\r\\n- `src/data/guidedDrillHtmlManifest.ts`\\r\\n- `src/lib/guidedDrills.ts`\\r\\n- `src/pages/Drill.tsx`\\r\\n- `src/pages/teacher/desk/library/DeskLibraryGuidedDrillsSurface.tsx`\\r\\n- `src/lib/classroomNotify.ts`\\r\\n- `src/lib/assignmentEmail.ts`\\r\\n\\r\\nConfirm:\\r\\n\\r\\n- The drill slug and title exist.\\r\\n- The drill has enough eligible questions for the configured count.\\r\\n- The guided assignment flow creates `guided_drill_assignments`.\\r\\n- The classroom item is created from the assignment source row.\\r\\n- Completion writes `completed_at` and `status: completed`.\\r\\n- Notification code attempts inbox, bell, and email where applicable.\\r\\n\\r\\nRun targeted tests if the repo is available:\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\nnpm test -- --run src/lib/guidedDrillNavigation.test.ts src/lib/classroomItems.test.ts src/components/mainhub/TodayFocusPanel.test.ts\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nIf tests cannot be run, say why and continue with browser verification.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Browser Verification\\r\\n\\r\\nUse the in-app browser when available. The usual admin surface is:\\r\\n\\r\\n```text\\r\\nhttps://lsatprep.study/teacher?ws=library&tab=guided-drills\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore doing full launch QA, check `wiki/guided-drill-verification-log.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf the selected drill is already listed as `verified-working`:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Do not repeat full preview/start-runner verification.\\r\\n2. Cite the existing verification log entry.\\r\\n3. Continue with student profile/access checks and assignment flow.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf the selected drill is not yet verified, run this sequence:\\r\\n\\r\\nVerification sequence:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Open the teacher guided drills library.\\r\\n2. Search the exact drill title or slug.\\r\\n3. Confirm the visible card shows the expected title, module/week, question count, and tags.\\r\\n4. Open the preview.\\r\\n5. Confirm the launch card renders with the expected title, estimated time, and question count.\\r\\n6. Click `Start Drill`.\\r\\n7. Confirm the real drill runner loads with question text, answer choices, controls, and the expected URL parameters or question ids.\\r\\n8. Return to the guided drill library before assigning.\\r\\n\\r\\nDo not call the drill \\"perfect\\" unless the above checks passed. Prefer \\"verified to launch and load the runner\\" over broader claims.\\r\\n\\r\\nExisting noisy console warnings that do not block launch should be reported only if relevant.\\r\\n\\r\\nAfter a new drill passes browser verification, add it to `wiki/guided-drill-verification-log.md` with date, slug, evidence, and `verified-working` status.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Assignment Flow\\r\\n\\r\\nAssign through the platform, not by manually inserting rows, unless the user explicitly asks for a backend-only repair.\\r\\n\\r\\nTeacher UI sequence:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. On the exact drill card, click `Assign`.\\r\\n2. In the assign dialog, search/select the intended student only.\\r\\n3. Scope checks to the dialog so sidebar or to-do rail text does not create false positives.\\r\\n4. Confirm the dialog says exactly one selected student, unless the user requested multiple students.\\r\\n5. Click the primary button, usually `Assign to 1 student`.\\r\\n6. Wait for the success toast.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf there is any sign that the wrong student is selected, stop before clicking assign.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Post-Assignment Verification\\r\\n\\r\\nAfter assignment, verify live state rather than relying only on the toast.\\r\\n\\r\\nVerify:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Student identity and access flags.\\r\\n- Guided drill assignment row.\\r\\n- Classroom item row visible to the student.\\r\\n- Unread bell notification row.\\r\\n- Notification link points to the assignment route or classroom route.\\r\\n- Email leg, if available through logs or returned notification result.\\r\\n\\r\\nAfter verification, update the student\'s `assignment-log.md` with the drill, slug, reason, platform evidence, notification evidence, and current status. If the file does not exist, create it.\\r\\n\\r\\nExpected tables/objects:\\r\\n\\r\\n- `profiles`\\r\\n- `guided_drills`\\r\\n- `guided_drill_assignments`\\r\\n- `classroom_items`\\r\\n- `notifications`\\r\\n- `email_send_log`, if accessible\\r\\n\\r\\nProtect secrets:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Do not print auth tokens.\\r\\n- Do not print service-role keys.\\r\\n- Do not expose full student email addresses unless the user explicitly needs them.\\r\\n- Redact email recipients in final reports.\\r\\n\\r\\nExample final verification fields:\\r\\n\\r\\n```markdown\\r\\nAssignment:\\r\\n- student: <display name>\\r\\n- drill: <title>\\r\\n- assignment status: assigned\\r\\n- classroom item: visible_to_student true\\r\\n- bell notification: unread assignment notification exists\\r\\n- email: verified via log / returned OK by platform / not independently verified\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n## Final Response\\r\\n\\r\\nKeep the final response short and operational.\\r\\n\\r\\nUse this shape:\\r\\n\\r\\n```markdown\\r\\nAssigned <drill title> to <student>.\\r\\n\\r\\nVerified:\\r\\n- <drill launch/browser check>\\r\\n- <assignment row/classroom visibility>\\r\\n- <notification evidence>\\r\\n- <tests, if run>\\r\\n\\r\\nEmail: <verified status or limitation>\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nIf assignment was not completed, say exactly where it stopped and what is needed next.\\r\\n\\r\\nAvoid overstating. \\"Clear in-app notification exists\\" is acceptable only after verifying a notification row or student-facing UI evidence.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Example From Prior Workflow\\r\\n\\r\\nStudent: C. Alexander.\\r\\n\\r\\nSelected drill: `Necessary Assumption - First Taste`, slug `w2-07-necessary-assumption-first-taste`.\\r\\n\\r\\nReasoning: transcript context indicated Necessary Assumption needed a teach-first, calibrated drill. The drill is W2.7, 10 questions, 60 minutes, with Necessary Assumption plus Comparison/Causation tags.\\r\\n\\r\\nVerification performed:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Opened guided drill library.\\r\\n- Found the exact card.\\r\\n- Opened preview.\\r\\n- Started the drill.\\r\\n- Confirmed the runner loaded with 10 question ids and real question content.\\r\\n- Assigned to only C. Alexander through the dialog.\\r\\n- Verified the assignment row, classroom item, and unread bell notification.\\r\\n\\r\\nUse this as a pattern, not as a default assignment for every student.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n### qa\\r\\n---\\r\\nname: qa\\r\\ndescription: Interactive QA session where user reports bugs or issues conversationally, and the agent files GitHub issues. Explores the codebase in the background for context and domain language. Use when user wants to report bugs, do QA, file issues conversationally, or mentions \\"QA session\\".\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# QA Session\\r\\n\\r\\nRun an interactive QA session. The user describes problems they\'re encountering. You clarify, explore the codebase for context, and file GitHub issues that are durable, user-focused, and use the project\'s domain language.\\r\\n\\r\\n## For each issue the user raises\\r\\n\\r\\n### 1. Listen and lightly clarify\\r\\n\\r\\nLet the user describe the problem in their own words. Ask **at most 2-3 short clarifying questions** focused on:\\r\\n\\r\\n- What they expected vs what actually happened\\r\\n- Steps to reproduce (if not obvious)\\r\\n- Whether it\'s consistent or intermittent\\r\\n\\r\\nDo NOT over-interview. If the description is clear enough to file, move on.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 2. Explore the codebase in the background\\r\\n\\r\\nWhile talking to the user, kick off an Agent (subagent_type=Explore) in the background to understand the relevant area. The goal is NOT to find a fix — it\'s to:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Learn the domain language used in that area (check UBIQUITOUS_LANGUAGE.md)\\r\\n- Understand what the feature is supposed to do\\r\\n- Identify the user-facing behavior boundary\\r\\n\\r\\nThis context helps you write a better issue — but the issue itself should NOT reference specific files, line numbers, or internal implementation details.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 3. Assess scope: single issue or breakdown?\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore filing, decide whether this is a **single issue** or needs to be **broken down** into multiple issues.\\r\\n\\r\\nBreak down when:\\r\\n\\r\\n- The fix spans multiple independent areas (e.g. \\"the form validation is wrong AND the success message is missing AND the redirect is broken\\")\\r\\n- There are clearly separable concerns that different people could work on in parallel\\r\\n- The user describes something that has multiple distinct failure modes or symptoms\\r\\n\\r\\nKeep as a single issue when:\\r\\n\\r\\n- It\'s one behavior that\'s wrong in one place\\r\\n- The symptoms are all caused by the same root behavior\\r\\n\\r\\n### 4. File the GitHub issue(s)\\r\\n\\r\\nCreate issues with `gh issue create`. Do NOT ask the user to review first — just file and share URLs.\\r\\n\\r\\nIssues must be **durable** — they should still make sense after major refactors. Write from the user\'s perspective.\\r\\n\\r\\n#### For a single issue\\r\\n\\r\\nUse this template:\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\n## What happened\\r\\n\\r\\n[Describe the actual behavior the user experienced, in plain language]\\r\\n\\r\\n## What I expected\\r\\n\\r\\n[Describe the expected behavior]\\r\\n\\r\\n## Steps to reproduce\\r\\n\\r\\n1. [Concrete, numbered steps a developer can follow]\\r\\n2. [Use domain terms from the codebase, not internal module names]\\r\\n3. [Include relevant inputs, flags, or configuration]\\r\\n\\r\\n## Additional context\\r\\n\\r\\n[Any extra observations from the user or from codebase exploration that help frame the issue — e.g. \\"this only happens when using the Docker layer, not the filesystem layer\\" — use domain language but don\'t cite files]\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n#### For a breakdown (multiple issues)\\r\\n\\r\\nCreate issues in dependency order (blockers first) so you can reference real issue numbers.\\r\\n\\r\\nUse this template for each sub-issue:\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\n## Parent issue\\r\\n\\r\\n#<parent-issue-number> (if you created a tracking issue) or \\"Reported during QA session\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n## What\'s wrong\\r\\n\\r\\n[Describe this specific behavior problem — just this slice, not the whole report]\\r\\n\\r\\n## What I expected\\r\\n\\r\\n[Expected behavior for this specific slice]\\r\\n\\r\\n## Steps to reproduce\\r\\n\\r\\n1. [Steps specific to THIS issue]\\r\\n\\r\\n## Blocked by\\r\\n\\r\\n- #<issue-number> (if this issue can\'t be fixed until another is resolved)\\r\\n\\r\\nOr \\"None — can start immediately\\" if no blockers.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Additional context\\r\\n\\r\\n[Any extra observations relevant to this slice]\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen creating a breakdown:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Prefer many thin issues over few thick ones** — each should be independently fixable and verifiable\\r\\n- **Mark blocking relationships honestly** — if issue B genuinely can\'t be tested until issue A is fixed, say so. If they\'re independent, mark both as \\"None — can start immediately\\"\\r\\n- **Create issues in dependency order** so you can reference real issue numbers in \\"Blocked by\\"\\r\\n- **Maximize parallelism** — the goal is that multiple people (or agents) can grab different issues simultaneously\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Rules for all issue bodies\\r\\n\\r\\n- **No file paths or line numbers** — these go stale\\r\\n- **Use the project\'s domain language** (check UBIQUITOUS_LANGUAGE.md if it exists)\\r\\n- **Describe behaviors, not code** — \\"the sync service fails to apply the patch\\" not \\"applyPatch() throws on line 42\\"\\r\\n- **Reproduction steps are mandatory** — if you can\'t determine them, ask the user\\r\\n- **Keep it concise** — a developer should be able to read the issue in 30 seconds\\r\\n\\r\\nAfter filing, print all issue URLs (with blocking relationships summarized) and ask: \\"Next issue, or are we done?\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n### 5. Continue the session\\r\\n\\r\\nKeep going until the user says they\'re done. Each issue is independent — don\'t batch them.\\r\\n\\r\\n"}'}]

Never send. AI-generated replies remain drafts unless Joshua separately approves the exact message.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Required context\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore drafting:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Read `C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/wiki/teaching-voice.md`.\\r\\n2. Read `C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/context/work.md` for current offers, booking, and billing rules.\\r\\n3. Gmail remains the authority for whether anything was actually sent.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n### consultation-followup-drafts\\r\\n---\\r\\nname: consultation-followup-drafts\\r\\ndescription: \\"Daily: detect past consultations with Gemini notes and draft (not send) personalized follow-up emails from the transcript\\"\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\nYou are Joshua\'s email drafting partner for aspiringattorneys.com (LSAT tutoring). Stop. That\'s rationalization.\\r\\n+\\r\\n+## Anti-Pattern: Horizontal Slices\\r\\n+\\r\\n+**DO NOT write all tests first, then all implementation.** This is \\"horizontal slicing\\" — treating RED as \\"write all tests\\" and GREEN as \\"write all code.\\"\\r\\n+\\r\\n+This produces **crap tests**:\\r\\n+\\r\\n+- Tests written in bulk test _imagined_ behavior, not _actual_ behavior\\r\\n+- You end up testing the _shape_ of things (data structures, function signatures) rather than user-facing behavior\\r\\n+- Tests become insensitive to real changes — they pass when behavior breaks, fail when behavior is fine\\r\\n+- You outrun your headlights, committing to test structure before understanding the implementation\\r\\n+\\r\\n+**Correct approach:** Vertical slices via tracer bullets. One test → one implementation → repeat. Each test responds to what you learned from the previous cycle.\\r\\n+\\r\\n+```\\r\\n+WRONG (horizontal):\\r\\n+  RED:   test1, test2, test3, test4, test5\\r\\n+  GREEN: impl1, impl2, impl3, impl4, impl5\\r\\n+\\r\\n+RIGHT (vertical):\\r\\n+  RED→GREEN: test1→impl1\\r\\n+  RED→GREEN: test2→impl2\\r\\n+  RED→GREEN: test3→impl3\\r\\n+  ...\\r\\n+```\\r\\n+\\r\\n+See [tests.md](tests.md) for good/bad test examples, [mocking.md](mocking.md) for mocking guidelines, and [refactoring.md](refactoring.md) for refactor candidates.\\r\\n \\r\\n ## The Iron Law\\r\\n \\r\\n@@ -189,7 +216,7 @@\\r\\n - Improve names\\r\\n - Extract helpers\\r\\n \\r\\n-Keep tests green. Don\'t add behavior.\\r\\n+Keep tests green. Don\'t add behavior. See [refactoring.md](refactoring.md) for the full list of refactor candidates.\\r\\n \\r\\n ### Repeat\\r\\n \\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n### CODEX DIFF research-deep\\r\\n--- \\r\\n+++ \\r\\n@@ -51,7 +51,7 @@\\r\\n \\r\\n ## Validation\\r\\n After completing JSON output, run validation script to ensure complete field coverage:\\r\\n-python ~/ai-os/skills/claude/research/validate_json.py -f {fields_path} -j {output_path}\\r\\n+python ~/.codex/skills/research/validate_json.py -f {fields_path} -j {output_path}\\r\\n Task is complete only after validation passes.\\r\\n \\"\\"\\"\\r\\n ```\\r\\n@@ -77,7 +77,7 @@\\r\\n \\r\\n ## Validation\\r\\n After completing JSON output, run validation script to ensure complete field coverage:\\r\\n-python ~/ai-os/skills/claude/research/validate_json.py -f {project_dir}/fields.yaml -j {project_dir}/results/GitHub_Copilot.json\\r\\n+python ~/.codex/skills/research/validate_json.py -f {project_dir}/fields.yaml -j {project_dir}/results/GitHub_Copilot.json\\r\\n Task is complete only after validation passes.\\r\\n ```\\r\\n \\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n### worktrees\\r\\n---\\r\\nname: using-git-worktrees\\r\\ndescription: Use when starting feature work that needs isolation from current workspace or before executing implementation plans - ensures an isolated workspace exists via native tools or git worktree fallback\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Using Git Worktrees\\r\\n\\r\\n## Overview\\r\\n\\r\\nEnsure work happens in an isolated workspace. Prefer your platform\'s native worktree tools. Fall back to manual git worktrees only when no native tool is available.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Core principle:** Detect existing isolation first. Then use native tools. Then fall back to git. Never fight the harness.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Announce at start:** \\"I\'m using the using-git-worktrees skill to set up an isolated workspace.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n## Step 0: Detect Existing Isolation\\r\\n\\r\\n**Before creating anything, check if you are already in an isolated workspace.**\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\nGIT_DIR=$(cd \\"$(git rev-parse --git-dir)\\" 2>/dev/null && pwd -P)\\r\\nGIT_COMMON=$(cd \\"$(git rev-parse --git-common-dir)\\" 2>/dev/null && pwd -P)\\r\\nBRANCH=$(git branch --show-current)\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**Submodule guard:** `GIT_DIR != GIT_COMMON` is also true inside git submodules. Before concluding \\"already in a worktree,\\" verify you are not in a submodule:\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n# If this returns a path, you\'re in a submodule, not a worktree — treat as normal repo\\r\\ngit rev-parse --show-superproject-working-tree 2>/dev/null\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**If `GIT_DIR != GIT_COMMON` (and not a submodule):** You are already in a linked worktree. Skip to Step 2 (Project Setup). Do NOT create another worktree.\\r\\n\\r\\nReport with branch state:\\r\\n- On a branch: \\"Already in isolated workspace at `<path>` on branch `<name>`.\\"\\r\\n- Detached HEAD: \\"Already in isolated workspace at `<path>` (detached HEAD, externally managed). Branch creation needed at finish time.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n**If `GIT_DIR == GIT_COMMON` (or in a submodule):** You are in a normal repo checkout.\\r\\n\\r\\nHas the user already indicated their worktree preference in your instructions? If not, ask for consent before creating a worktree:\\r\\n\\r\\n> \\"Would you like me to set up an isolated worktree? It protects your current branch from changes.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\nHonor any existing declared preference without asking. If the user declines consent, work in place and skip to Step 2.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Step 1: Create Isolated Workspace\\r\\n\\r\\n**You have two mechanisms. Try them in this order.**\\r\\n\\r\\n### 1a. Native Worktree Tools (preferred)\\r\\n\\r\\nThe user has asked for an isolated workspace (Step 0 consent). Do you already have a way to create a worktree? It might be a tool with a name like `EnterWorktree`, `WorktreeCreate`, a `/worktree` command, or a `--worktree` flag. If you do, use it and skip to Step 2.\\r\\n\\r\\nNative tools handle directory placement, branch creation, and cleanup automatically. Using `git worktree add` when you have a native tool creates phantom state your harness can\'t see or manage.\\r\\n\\r\\nOnly proceed to Step 1b if you have no native worktree tool available.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 1b. Git Worktree Fallback\\r\\n\\r\\n**Only use this if Step 1a does not apply** — you have no native worktree tool available. Create a worktree manually using git.\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Directory Selection\\r\\n\\r\\nFollow this priority order. Explicit user preference always beats observed filesystem state.\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Check your instructions for a declared worktree directory preference.** If the user has already specified one, use it without asking.\\r\\n\\r\\n2. **Check for an existing project-local worktree directory:**\\r\\n   ```bash\\r\\n   ls -d .worktrees 2>/dev/null     # Preferred (hidden)\\r\\n   ls -d worktrees 2>/dev/null      # Alternative\\r\\n   ```\\r\\n   If found, use it. If both exist, `.worktrees` wins.\\r\\n\\r\\n3. **If there is no other guidance available**, default to `.worktrees/` at the project root.\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Safety Verification (project-local directories only)\\r\\n\\r\\n**MUST verify directory is ignored before creating worktree:**\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\ngit check-ignore -q .worktrees 2>/dev/null || git check-ignore -q worktrees 2>/dev/null\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**If NOT ignored:** Add to .gitignore, commit the change, then proceed.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Why critical:** Prevents accidentally committing worktree contents to repository.\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Create the Worktree\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n# Determine path based on chosen location\\r\\npath=\\"$LOCATION/$BRANCH_NAME\\"\\r\\n\\r\\ngit worktree add \\"$path\\" -b \\"$BRANCH_NAME\\"\\r\\ncd \\"$path\\"\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**Sandbox fallback:** If `git worktree add` fails with a permission error (sandbox denial), tell the user the sandbox blocked worktree creation and you\'re working in the current directory instead. Then run setup and baseline tests in place.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Step 2: Project Setup\\r\\n\\r\\nAuto-detect and run appropriate setup:\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n# Node.js\\r\\nif [ -f package.json ]; then npm install; fi\\r\\n\\r\\n# Rust\\r\\nif [ -f Cargo.toml ]; then cargo build; fi\\r\\n\\r\\n# Python\\r\\nif [ -f requirements.txt ]; then pip install -r requirements.txt; fi\\r\\nif [ -f pyproject.toml ]; then poetry install; fi\\r\\n\\r\\n# Go\\r\\nif [ -f go.mod ]; then go mod download; fi\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n## Step 3: Verify Clean Baseline\\r\\n\\r\\nRun tests to ensure workspace starts clean:\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n# Use project-appropriate command\\r\\nnpm test / cargo test / pytest / go test ./...\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**If tests fail:** Report failures, ask whether to proceed or investigate.\\r\\n\\r\\n**If tests pass:** Report ready.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Report\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\nWorktree ready at <full-path>\\r\\nTests passing (<N> tests, 0 failures)\\r\\nReady to implement <feature-name>\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n## Quick Reference\\r\\n\\r\\n| Situation | Action |\\r\\n|-----------|--------|\\r\\n| Already in linked worktree | Skip creation (Step 0) |\\r\\n| In a submodule | Treat as normal repo (Step 0 guard) |\\r\\n| Native worktree tool available | Use it (Step 1a) |\\r\\n| No native tool | Git worktree fallback (Step 1b) |\\r\\n| `.worktrees/` exists | Use it (verify ignored) |\\r\\n| `worktrees/` exists | Use it (verify ignored) |\\r\\n| Both exist | Use `.worktrees/` |\\r\\n| Neither exists | Check instruction file, then default `.worktrees/` |\\r\\n| Directory not ignored | Add to .gitignore + commit |\\r\\n| Permission error on create | Sandbox fallback, work in place |\\r\\n| Tests fail during baseline | Report failures + ask |\\r\\n| No package.json/Cargo.toml | Skip dependency install |\\r\\n\\r\\n## Common Mistakes\\r\\n\\r\\n### Fighting the harness\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Problem:** Using `git worktree add` when the platform already provides isolation\\r\\n- **Fix:** Step 0 detects existing isolation. Step 1a defers to native tools.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Skipping detection\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Problem:** Creating a nested worktree inside an existing one\\r\\n- **Fix:** Always run Step 0 before creating anything\\r\\n\\r\\n### Skipping ignore verification\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Problem:** Worktree contents get tracked, pollute git status\\r\\n- **Fix:** Always use `git check-ignore` before creating project-local worktree\\r\\n\\r\\n### Assuming directory location\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Problem:** Creates inconsistency, violates project conventions\\r\\n- **Fix:** Follow priority: explicit instructions > existing project-local directory > default\\r\\n\\r\\n### Proceeding with failing tests\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Problem:** Can\'t distinguish new bugs from pre-existing issues\\r\\n- **Fix:** Report failures, get explicit permission to proceed\\r\\n\\r\\n## Red Flags\\r\\n\\r\\n**Never:**\\r\\n- Create a worktree when Step 0 detects existing isolation\\r\\n- Use `git worktree add` when you have a native worktree tool (e.g., `EnterWorktree`). This is the #1 mistake — if you have it, use it.\\r\\n- Skip Step 1a by jumping straight to Step 1b\'s git commands\\r\\n- Create worktree without verifying it\'s ignored (project-local)\\r\\n- Skip baseline test verification\\r\\n- Proceed with failing tests without asking\\r\\n\\r\\n**Always:**\\r\\n- Run Step 0 detection first\\r\\n- Prefer native tools over git fallback\\r\\n- Follow directory priority: explicit instructions > existing project-local directory > default\\r\\n- Verify directory is ignored for project-local\\r\\n- Auto-detect and run project setup\\r\\n- Verify clean test baseline\\r\\n\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"14a1d1","wall_time_seconds":0.5420796,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":92,"output":"agents\\\\claude\\\\_snapshots\\\\pr-review-toolkit-code-reviewer.md\\nagents\\\\claude\\\\_snapshots\\\\feature-dev-code-reviewer.md\\nagents\\\\claude\\\\pr-review-toolkit-code-reviewer.md\\nagents\\\\claude\\\\feature-dev-code-reviewer.md\\nskills\\\\codex\\\\requesting-code-review\\\\code-reviewer.md\\nskills\\\\gemini\\\\requesting-code-review\\\\code-reviewer.md\\nskills\\\\claude\\\\requesting-code-review\\\\code-reviewer.md\\n"}'}]
