[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 16.4 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"d6c91f","wall_time_seconds":0.6350141,"exit_code":1,"original_token_count":0,"output":""}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"bcc9b7","wall_time_seconds":0.4677338,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1094,"output":"---\\r\\nname: test-driven-development\\r\\ndescription: Use when implementing any feature or bugfix, before writing implementation code\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Test-Driven Development (TDD)\\r\\n\\r\\n## Overview\\r\\n\\r\\nWrite the test first. Watch it fail. Write minimal code to pass.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Core principle:** If you didn\'t watch the test fail, you don\'t know if it tests the right thing.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Violating the letter of the rules is violating the spirit of the rules.**\\r\\n\\r\\n## When to Use\\r\\n\\r\\n**Always:**\\r\\n- New features\\r\\n- Bug fixes\\r\\n- Refactoring\\r\\n- Behavior changes\\r\\n\\r\\n**Exceptions (ask your human partner):**\\r\\n- Throwaway prototypes\\r\\n- Generated code\\r\\n- Configuration files\\r\\n\\r\\nThinking \\"skip TDD just this once\\"? Stop. That\'s rationalization.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The Iron Law\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\nNO PRODUCTION CODE WITHOUT A FAILING TEST FIRST\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nWrite code before the test? Delete it. Start over.\\r\\n\\r\\n**No exceptions:**\\r\\n- Don\'t keep it as \\"reference\\"\\r\\n- Don\'t \\"adapt\\" it while writing tests\\r\\n- Don\'t look at it\\r\\n- Delete means delete\\r\\n\\r\\nImplement fresh from tests. Period.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Red-Green-Refactor\\r\\n\\r\\n```dot\\r\\ndigraph tdd_cycle {\\r\\n    rankdir=LR;\\r\\n    red [label=\\"RED\\\\nWrite failing test\\", shape=box, style=filled, fillcolor=\\"#ffcccc\\"];\\r\\n    verify_red [label=\\"Verify fails\\\\ncorrectly\\", shape=diamond];\\r\\n    green [label=\\"GREEN\\\\nMinimal code\\", shape=box, style=filled, fillcolor=\\"#ccffcc\\"];\\r\\n    verify_green [label=\\"Verify passes\\\\nAll green\\", shape=diamond];\\r\\n    refactor [label=\\"REFACTOR\\\\nClean up\\", shape=box, style=filled, fillcolor=\\"#ccccff\\"];\\r\\n    next [label=\\"Next\\", shape=ellipse];\\r\\n\\r\\n    red -> verify_red;\\r\\n    verify_red -> green [label=\\"yes\\"];\\r\\n    verify_red -> red [label=\\"wrong\\\\nfailure\\"];\\r\\n    green -> verify_green;\\r\\n    verify_green -> refactor [label=\\"yes\\"];\\r\\n    verify_green -> green [label=\\"no\\"];\\r\\n    refactor -> verify_green [label=\\"stay\\\\ngreen\\"];\\r\\n    verify_green -> next;\\r\\n    next -> red;\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n### RED - Write Failing Test\\r\\n\\r\\nWrite one minimal test showing what should happen.\\r\\n\\r\\n<Good>\\r\\n```typescript\\r\\ntest(\'retries failed operations 3 times\', async () => {\\r\\n  let attempts = 0;\\r\\n  const operation = () => {\\r\\n    attempts++;\\r\\n    if (attempts < 3) throw new Error(\'fail\');\\r\\n    return \'success\';\\r\\n  };\\r\\n\\r\\n  const result = await retryOperation(operation);\\r\\n\\r\\n  expect(result).toBe(\'success\');\\r\\n  expect(attempts).toBe(3);\\r\\n});\\r\\n```\\r\\nClear name, tests real behavior, one thing\\r\\n</Good>\\r\\n\\r\\n<Bad>\\r\\n```typescript\\r\\ntest(\'retry works\', async () => {\\r\\n  const mock = jest.fn()\\r\\n    .mockRejectedValueOnce(new Error())\\r\\n    .mockRejectedValueOnce(new Error())\\r\\n    .mockResolvedValueOnce(\'success\');\\r\\n  await retryOperation(mock);\\r\\n  expect(mock).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(3);\\r\\n});\\r\\n```\\r\\nVague name, tests mock not code\\r\\n</Bad>\\r\\n\\r\\n**Requirements:**\\r\\n- One behavior\\r\\n- Clear name\\r\\n- Real code (no mocks unless unavoidable)\\r\\n\\r\\n### Verify RED - Watch It Fail\\r\\n\\r\\n**MANDATORY. Never skip.**\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\nnpm test path/to/test.test.ts\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nConfirm:\\r\\n- Test fails (not errors)\\r\\n- Failure message is expected\\r\\n- Fails because feature missing (not typos)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Test passes?** You\'re testing existing behavior. Fix test.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Test errors?** Fix error, re-run until it fails correctly.\\r\\n\\r\\n### GREEN - Minimal Code\\r\\n\\r\\nWrite simplest code to pass the test.\\r\\n\\r\\n<Good>\\r\\n```typescript\\r\\nasync function retryOperation<T>(fn: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {\\r\\n  for (let i = 0; i < 3; i++) {\\r\\n    try {\\r\\n      return await fn();\\r\\n    } catch (e) {\\r\\n      if (i === 2) throw e;\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  throw new Error(\'unreachable\');\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\nJust enough to pass\\r\\n</Good>\\r\\n\\r\\n<Bad>\\r\\n```typescript\\r\\nasync function retryOperation<T>(\\r\\n  fn: () => Promise<T>,\\r\\n  options?: {\\r\\n    maxRetries?: number;\\r\\n    backoff?: \'linear\' | \'exponential\';\\r\\n    onRetry?: (attempt: number) => void;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n): Promise<T> {\\r\\n  // YAGNI\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\nOver-engineered\\r\\n</Bad>\\r\\n\\r\\nDon\'t add features, refactor other code, or \\"improve\\" beyond the test.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Verify GREEN - Watch It Pass\\r\\n\\r\\n**MANDATORY.**\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\nnpm test path/to/test.test.ts\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nConfirm:\\r\\n- Test passes\\r\\n- Other tests still pass\\r\\n- Output pristine (no errors, warnings)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Test fails?** Fix code, not test.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Other tests fail?** Fix now.\\r\\n\\r\\n### REFACTOR - Clean Up\\r\\n\\r\\nAfter green only:\\r\\n- Remove duplication\\r\\n- Improve names\\r\\n- Extract helpers\\r\\n\\r\\nKeep tests green. Don\'t add behavior.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Repeat\\r\\n\\r\\nNext failing test for next feature.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Good Tests\\r\\n\\r\\n| Quality | Good | Bad |\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"e4bb93","wall_time_seconds":0.486755,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1304,"output":"# Code Reviewer Prompt Template\\n\\nUse this template when dispatching a code reviewer subagent.\\n\\n**Purpose:** Review completed work against requirements and code quality standards before it cascades into more work.\\n\\n```\\nSubagent (general-purpose):\\n  description: \\"Review code changes\\"\\n  prompt: |\\n    You are a Senior Code Reviewer with expertise in software architecture,\\n    design patterns, and best practices. Your job is to review completed work\\n    against its plan or requirements and identify issues before they cascade.\\n\\n    ## What Was Implemented\\n\\n    [DESCRIPTION]\\n\\n    ## Requirements / Plan\\n\\n    [PLAN_OR_REQUIREMENTS]\\n\\n    ## Git Range to Review\\n\\n    **Base:** [BASE_SHA]\\n    **Head:** [HEAD_SHA]\\n\\n    ```bash\\n    git diff --stat [BASE_SHA]..[HEAD_SHA]\\n    git diff [BASE_SHA]..[HEAD_SHA]\\n    ```\\n\\n    ## Read-Only Review\\n\\n    Your review is read-only on this checkout. Do not mutate the working tree, the index, HEAD, or branch state in any way. Use tools like `git show`, `git diff`, and `git log` to inspect history. If you need a working copy of a different revision, check it out into a separate temporary directory (e.g. `git worktree add /tmp/review-[SHA] [SHA]`) — never move HEAD on this checkout.\\n\\n    ## What to Check\\n\\n    **Plan alignment:**\\n    - Does the implementation match the plan / requirements?\\n    - Are deviations justified improvements, or problematic departures?\\n    - Is all planned functionality present?\\n\\n    **Code quality:**\\n    - Clean separation of concerns?\\n    - Proper error handling?\\n    - Type safety where applicable?\\n    - DRY without premature abstraction?\\n    - Edge cases handled?\\n\\n    **Architecture:**\\n    - Sound design decisions?\\n    - Reasonable scalability and performance?\\n    - Security concerns?\\n    - Integrates cleanly with surrounding code?\\n\\n    **Testing:**\\n    - Tests verify real behavior, not mocks?\\n    - Edge cases covered?\\n    - Integration tests where they matter?\\n    - All tests passing?\\n\\n    **Production readiness:**\\n    - Migration strategy if schema changed?\\n    - Backward compatibility considered?\\n    - Documentation complete?\\n    - No obvious bugs?\\n\\n    ## Calibration\\n\\n    Categorize issues by actual severity. Not everything is Critical.\\n    Acknowledge what was done well before listing issues — accurate praise\\n    helps the implementer trust the rest of the feedback.\\n\\n    If you find significant deviations from the plan, flag them specifically\\n    so the implementer can confirm whether the deviation was intentional.\\n    If you find issues with the plan itself rather than the implementation,\\n    say so.\\n\\n    ## Output Format\\n\\n    ### Strengths\\n    [What\'s well done? Be specific.]\\n\\n    ### Issues\\n\\n    #### Critical (Must Fix)\\n    [Bugs, security issues, data loss risks, broken functionality]\\n\\n    #### Important (Should Fix)\\n    [Architecture problems, missing features, poor error handling, test gaps]\\n\\n    #### Minor (Nice to Have)\\n    [Code style, optimization opportunities, documentation polish]\\n\\n    For each issue:\\n    - File:line reference\\n    - What\'s wrong\\n    - Why it matters\\n    - How to fix (if not obvious)\\n\\n    ### Recommendations\\n    [Improvements for code quality, architecture, or process]\\n\\n    ### Assessment\\n\\n    **Ready to merge?** [Yes | No | With fixes]\\n\\n    **Reasoning:** [1-2 sentence technical assessment]\\n\\n    ## Critical Rules\\n\\n    **DO:**\\n    - Categorize by actual severity\\n    - Be specific (file:line, not vague)\\n    - Explain WHY each issue matters\\n    - Acknowledge strengths\\n    - Give a clear verdict\\n\\n    **DON\'T:**\\n    - Say \\"looks good\\" without checking\\n    - Mark nitpicks as Critical\\n    - Give feedback on code you didn\'t actually read\\n    - Be vague (\\"improve error handling\\")\\n    - Avoid giving a clear verdict\\n```\\n\\n**Placeholders:**\\n- `[DESCRIPTION]` — brief summary of what was built\\n- `[PLAN_OR_REQUIREMENTS]` — what it should do (plan file path, task text, or requirements)\\n- `[BASE_SHA]` — starting commit\\n- `[HEAD_SHA]` — ending commit\\n\\n**Reviewer returns:** Strengths, Issues (Critical / Important / Minor), Recommendations, Assessment\\n\\n## Example Output\\n\\n```\\n### Strengths\\n- Clean database schema with proper migrations (db.ts:15-42)\\n- Comprehensive test coverage (18 tests, all edge cases)\\n- Good error handling with fallbacks (summarizer.ts:85-92)\\n\\n### Issues\\n\\n#### Important\\n1. **Missing help text in CLI wrapper**\\n   - File: index-conversations:1-31\\n   - Issue: No --help flag, users won\'t discover --concurrency\\n   - Fix: Add --help case with usage examples\\n\\n2. **Date validation missing**\\n   - File: search.ts:25-27\\n   - Issue: Invalid dates silently return no results\\n   - Fix: Validate ISO format, throw error with example\\n\\n#### Minor\\n1. **Progress indicators**\\n   - File: indexer.ts:130\\n   - Issue: No \\"X of Y\\" counter for long operations\\n   - Impact: Users don\'t know how long to wait\\n\\n### Recommendations\\n- Add progress reporting for user experience\\n- Consider config file for excluded projects (portability)\\n\\n### Assessment\\n\\n**Ready to merge: With fixes**\\n\\n**Reasoning:** Core implementation is solid with good architecture and tests. Important issues (help text, date validation) are easily fixed and don\'t affect core functionality.\\n```\\n\\r\\n"}'}]

text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"@'\nfrom pathlib import Path\nimport hashlib,json,re\nroot=Path.cwd(); report=root/'audits/skills-2026-10-10-repairs'; backup=report/'backups/canonical-core'; changes=[]\nnames='lsatu-cc-handoff chat-export code-review requesting-code-review guided-drill-assignment consultation-inquiry-drafts consultation-followup-drafts qa test-driven-development resolving-merge-conflicts setup-pre-commit bootcamp-parity-audit decision-mapping research-deep using-git-worktrees'.split()\ndef save(p,text):\n old=p.read_bytes(); new=text.encode('utf-8');\n if old==new:return\n dst=backup/p.relative_to(root);dst.parent.mkdir(parents=True,exist_ok=True)\n if not dst.exists():dst.write_bytes(old)\n assert p.read_bytes()==old\n p.write_bytes(new);changes.append({'path':str(p.relative_to(root)),'before':hashlib.sha256(old).hexdigest(),'after':hashlib.sha256(new).hexdigest()})\ndef rep(s,a,b):\n assert a in s, 'missing '+a[:80]\n return s.replace(a,b)\nfor rt in ['claude','codex','gemini']:\n for name in names:\n  p=root/'skills'/rt/name/'SKILL.md'\n  if not p.exists():continue\n  s=p.read_text(encoding='utf-8')\n  if name=='lsatu-cc-handoff':\n   s=re.sub(r'description: .*\\n','description: Create an LSAT U execution brief for Claude Code when Joshua explicitly asks for a CC handoff, paste box, or execution prompt. A screenshot or bug complaint alone requests diagnosis, not this handoff. Preserve source content, current project conventions, and the task\\'s Git authorization.\\n',s,count=1)\n   s=s.replace('`~/ai-os-projects/lsatu/`','`../ai-os-projects/lsatu/`').replace('`~/ai-os/raw/sources/lsat-u/curriculum/<topic>/`','`raw/sources/lsat-u/curriculum/<topic>/`')\n   s=rep(s,'Existing briefs that contradict the new direction must be archived (move to `_archive/`).','Read sources without modifying or moving them. Record supersession in the new operational brief; preserve contradictions explicitly.')\n   s=rep(s,'File path convention: `~/ai-os/raw/sources/lsat-u/curriculum/<topic>/<BRIEF_NAME>.md`','File path convention, relative to the current AI OS root: `handoffs/lsat-u/<topic>/<BRIEF_NAME>.md`. Never write, move or archive operational briefs under `raw/sources/`.')\n   s=s.replace('`For: Claude Code`, `Direct commits to main. Push after each step.`','`For: Claude Code`, plus the current checkout/branch and the actual commit/push authorization. Do not assume main or permission to publish.')\n   s=s.replace('and `~/ai-os/CLAUDE.md`.','and the current root `CLAUDE.md`. Follow the application\\'s nearest instructions.')\n   s=s.replace('   - Direct commits to main. Push at the end of each step.','   - Preserve unrelated changes. Commit/push only when authorized by the task; stage only owned paths on the verified branch.')\n   s=s.replace('commit message.','proposed commit message when a commit is authorized.')\n   s=rep(s,'**Archive superseded briefs.** When a new brief replaces a prior plan in the same domain, move the old one to `_archive/<NAME>_superseded.md`. The vault stays clean.','**Record supersession.** Link the old brief and explain the replacement in the new handoff. Do not move or delete source evidence. Preserve old operational briefs unless Joshua explicitly authorizes relocation.')\n   s=s.replace('Reference the brief by full vault path with `~/Documents/Obsidian\\\\ Vault/...` (escape the space).','Reference the saved brief by its verified absolute path on this machine, quoted for the receiving shell.')\n   s=s.replace('Tell CC to \"execute end to end. Commit and push after each step.\"','Tell CC to execute the authorized scope end to end and state the actual Git/publication permissions.')\n   s=s.replace('Skipping the archive step when a brief supersedes prior work.','Failing to record supersession or moving immutable evidence.')\n   s=s.replace('C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/skills/lsat-u-architecture/SKILL.md','skills/claude/lsat-u-architecture/SKILL.md')\n   s=s.replace('~/ai-os/','').replace('~/ai-os-projects/','../ai-os-projects/')\n   s=s.replace('Brutalist preservation is non-negotiable','Preservation of the current project design system is required').replace('Brutalist palette preserved. Same `brutal-*` tokens. Same `.topic-abstract` wrapper.','Preserve the verified current design system; retain `brutal-*` tokens and `.topic-abstract` only where the owning module uses them.')\n  elif name=='chat-export':\n   s=re.sub(r'description: .*\\n','description: Export a conversation into a local Markdown record when explicitly requested. Retrieve original logs when available, label visible-context-only exports honestly, and include a handoff summary. Context-reset advice alone belongs to session-handoff.\\n',s,count=1)\n   s=s.replace('with a full transcript,','with a provenance-labeled transcript,')\n   s=rep(s,'1. **Reconstruct the full transcript** from the conversation in context.\\n   Write every user message and every Claude reply verbatim under\\n   `### User` and `### Claude` headings. Do not paraphrase or skip.','1. **Retrieve transcript evidence.** Prefer the host\\'s original conversation export/log for this exact session. Record source path, session identifier, available range and exclusions (tools/attachments). If unavailable, export only messages actually visible in context and label it `Visible-context export, incomplete history`. After compaction, a summary is not verbatim evidence: list unknown/missing ranges and never invent them. Use `### User` and `### Assistant` headings; preserve available message text exactly.')\n   s=s.replace(\"Save the file** using Desktop Commander's `write_file` to the\",\"Save the file** using an available local file-writing tool to the\")\n   s=s.replace('## Full transcript','## Transcript coverage\\n\\nSource: <verified log/export or visible context>\\nAvailable range: <known range>\\nMissing sections: <none proven / explicit gaps / unknown earlier history>\\n\\n## Available transcript').replace('### Claude','### Assistant')\n   s=s.replace('Never truncate the transcript. If it\\'s long, write all of it.','Preserve all available verified messages, chunking file writes when needed. Do not call the export complete without original-history coverage evidence.')\n  elif name=='code-review':\n   s=rep(s,'Whatever the user said is the fixed point (a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc.). If they didn\\'t specify one, ask for it.','Determine scope first: committed branch/PR, current work in progress, or both. Use a supplied fixed point; for branch/PR review resolve its actual base. A request to review uncommitted work needs no invented base or extra confirmation.')\n   s=rep(s,'Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.','Record `git status --short` first. For committed scope use `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` and `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`. Pass the same captured scope to both reviewers; if files change during review, refresh the affected scope.')\n   s=s.replace('The full diff command and commit list.','The complete captured review scope: committed diff (if requested), staged/unstaged diffs, in-scope untracked contents, exact paths and commit list.')\n  elif name=='requesting-code-review':\n   s=s.replace('Dispatch a `general-purpose` subagent, filling the template at [code-reviewer.md](code-reviewer.md)','Use the available host reviewer role/tool, filling the template at [code-reviewer.md](code-reviewer.md). `general-purpose` is a Claude host name, not a required Codex tool. If delegation is unavailable, perform the same read-only review and disclose that limitation.')\n   s=s.replace('**1. Get git SHAs:**','**1. Capture review scope before dispatch:**\\n\\nRead `git status --short`. Use the actual task baseline for committed changes; do not assume `HEAD~1` is the start. For WIP capture `git diff --cached`, `git diff`, and `git ls-files --others --exclude-standard`; read each requested untracked file. Pass these paths/content with the Git range to the reviewer. An empty range is not proof no work exists. Preserve the index/worktree.\\n\\nExample committed scope only:')\n  elif name=='guided-drill-assignment':\n   s=re.sub(r'description: .*\\n','description: Select and verify an LSAT U guided drill when Joshua asks for a drill recommendation or guided drill assignment. Transcript retrieval belongs to transcript-intake; transcript analysis alone does not trigger assignment. Inspection/recommendation are read-only; assign only when requested, through the current platform executor.\\n',s,count=1)\n   s=rep(s,'The operating standard is: do not merely recommend a drill. Choose the right drill from the student\\'s recent transcript/context, verify the drill actually launches and is wired correctly, assign it through the platform, then verify the student\\'s notification surface.','Choose the mode from the request:\\n\\n- **Inspect:** analyze the student evidence only; no assignment, notifications or new student run.\\n- **Recommend:** select and explain a drill, with available metadata/access evidence; stop before assignment.\\n- **Assign:** when Joshua has requested assignment, use the current `assign-lsatu-work` / `lsat-u-platform-actions` executor and its catalog, identity, duplicate, authorization and read-after-write contract. This skill supplies selection/verification knowledge, not a competing executor.\\n\\nOpening a drill runner can create progress/run records; do that only within authorized QA/assignment scope. A read-only request uses existing evidence and reports untested launch status.')\n   s=s.replace('## Assignment Flow\\n','## Assignment Flow\\n\\nThis section applies only in assign mode. Resolve and read the current platform executor before a live mutation; defer to its approved UI/API transport and duplicate checks. The UI sequence below is a fallback reference when that executor permits it.\\n')\n   s=s.replace('Assign through the platform, not by manually inserting rows, unless the user explicitly asks for a backend-only repair.','Use the executor\\'s approved platform transport. Do not insert rows manually unless the task explicitly authorizes a backend repair.')\n   s=s.replace('Use this shape:','In inspect/recommend mode, report findings/recommendation and state `Not assigned`. In assign mode use this shape:')\n  elif name=='consultation-inquiry-drafts':\n   s=rep(s,'Create the draft through `gws`.','Before creating, list and inspect existing Gmail drafts through `gws`, following pagination and matching the inquiry thread ID + normalized recipient + workflow (`consultation-inquiry`). Reuse the matching unsent draft ID; update only that draft if the current request authorizes revision. Multiple matches are a conflict: report them without deletion. A failed/partial draft lookup blocks creation, rather than assuming none. Repeated sweeps must leave one matching draft. Record draft ID, thread ID and workflow key in the ledger, then verify the draft by ID after the write.\\n\\nCreate the draft through `gws`.')\n  elif name=='consultation-followup-drafts':\n   s=(root/'skills/claude/consultation-followup-drafts/SKILL.md').read_text(encoding='utf-8') if rt=='codex' else s\n   s=rep(s,'STEP 5 — Draft using','Before STEP 5, inspect all existing drafts through `gws` (paginate). Match the stable consultation event ID + normalized recipient + workflow (`consultation-followup`); corroborate with thread ID and the ledger. Reuse a matching unsent draft ID rather than creating another. Update only that draft when revision is authorized; multiple matches require a conflict report, not deletion. Failed lookup blocks creation. Record event ID, thread ID, draft ID and workflow in `wiki/send-ledger.md`, and read the draft back after a write. A repeat sweep must retain one matching draft.\\n\\nSTEP 5 — Draft using')\n  elif name=='qa':\n   s=re.sub(r'description: .*\\n','description: Investigate and document conversational bug reports or QA findings. Default to a local report; create GitHub issues only when Joshua explicitly requests issue filing/publication. Use for QA sessions, reproduction and bug triage.\\n',s,count=1)\n   s=s.replace('Create issues with `gh issue create`. Do NOT ask the user to review first — just file and share URLs.','Default to a local issue-ready report. Otherwise show the concrete drafts. Before authorized creation check for the same existing issue, use `--body-file` for exact Markdown, and read back each created issue. Never publish solely because the user asked for QA.')\n   s=s.replace('After filing, print all issue URLs','After authorized filing, print all issue URLs')\n  elif name=='test-driven-development':\n   s=re.sub(r'\\*\\*Always:\\*\\*.*?## Red-Green-Refactor','''**Use for meaningful behavioral changes:** new behavior, bug fixes and risky refactors. Choose tests that distinguish correct behavior from the failure, not tests mirroring implementation. Existing project/user testing requirements win.\n\nFor reversible low-impact configuration/docs or generated code, run appropriate validation instead of ceremonial tests. Use existing authorization; no extra permission is needed for that routine choice.\n\n## Preserve existing work\n\nNever delete or restart existing implementation because tests were written later. Keep user and unrelated edits. For an existing bug, add a focused regression test, demonstrate its expected failure on the broken behavior in isolation where safe, make the smallest fix and rerun. If a test already passes, determine whether it proves existing behavior or misses the target failure; do not manufacture a production bug.\n\nFor new behavior, prefer one vertical red-green slice at a time. A missing initial red run is an evidence limitation to report and remedy with an appropriate counterexample, not permission to delete work.\n\n## Red-Green-Refactor''',s,flags=re.S,count=1)\n   start=s.index('## Common Rationalizations') if '## Common Rationalizations' in s else -1\n   earlier=s.find('**\"Deleting X hours of work is wasteful\"**')\n   if earlier>=0:\n    end=s.index('## Example: Bug Fix',earlier)\n    s=s[:earlier]+'''## Avoid ritual over evidence\n\nPreserve existing code and confirm meaningful behavior with regression/reference checks. A passing test alone does not prove it catches the reported failure; inspect its assertions and use a safe counterexample when needed. Rebase continuation remains bounded to the authorized operation.')\n  elif name=='setup-pre-commit':\n   s=s.replace('### 1. Detect package manager','### 1. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 12.0 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"e0b055","wall_time_seconds":0.5499152,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":2578,"output":" }\\r\\n  // ...\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**Verify GREEN**\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n$ npm test\\r\\nPASS\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**REFACTOR**\\r\\nExtract validation for multiple fields if needed.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Verification Checklist\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore marking work complete:\\r\\n\\r\\n- [ ] Every new function/method has a test\\r\\n- [ ] Watched each test fail before implementing\\r\\n- [ ] Each test failed for expected reason (feature missing, not typo)\\r\\n- [ ] Wrote minimal code to pass each test\\r\\n- [ ] All tests pass\\r\\n- [ ] Output pristine (no errors, warnings)\\r\\n- [ ] Tests use real code (mocks only if unavoidable)\\r\\n- [ ] Edge cases and errors covered\\r\\n\\r\\nReport missing evidence and perform the relevant check; preserve existing work.\\r\\n\\r\\n## When Stuck\\r\\n\\r\\n| Problem | Solution |\\r\\n|---------|----------|\\r\\n| Don\'t know how to test | Write wished-for API. Write assertion first. Ask your human partner. |\\r\\n| Test too complicated | Design too complicated. Simplify interface. |\\r\\n| Must mock everything | Code too coupled. Use dependency injection. |\\r\\n| Test setup huge | Extract helpers. Still complex? Simplify design. |\\r\\n\\r\\n## Debugging Integration\\r\\n\\r\\nBug found? Write failing test reproducing it. Follow TDD cycle. Test proves fix and prevents regression.\\r\\n\\r\\nFor behavioral bugs, use a focused regression test when practical; report any constraint and the alternative verification used.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Testing Anti-Patterns\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen adding mocks or test utilities, read [testing-anti-patterns.md](testing-anti-patterns.md) to avoid common pitfalls:\\r\\n- Testing mock behavior instead of real behavior\\r\\n- Adding test-only methods to production classes\\r\\n- Mocking without understanding dependencies\\r\\n\\r\\n## Final Rule\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\nProduction code → test exists and failed first\\r\\nOtherwise → not TDD\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nFollow the project and user testing scope. Preserve work and disclose verification limitations.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\nHANDOFF\\r\\n---\\r\\nname: lsatu-cc-handoff\\r\\ndescription: Create an LSAT U execution brief for Claude Code when Joshua explicitly asks for a CC handoff, paste box, or execution prompt. A screenshot or bug complaint alone requests diagnosis, not this handoff. Preserve source content, current project conventions, and the task\'s Git authorization.\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# LSAT U Build Loop: Claude Code Handoff\\r\\n\\r\\nThis is the operational skill for translating Joshua\'s input into a Claude Code execution prompt. Use it every time the loop runs.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe loop: input -> analyze -> write brief to vault -> hand paste box back to Joshua -> CC executes -> Joshua returns with results.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Phase 1: Analyze before writing\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore writing a single line of brief, do all of the following:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Read the actual current code.** Do not write recommendations from memory. The LSATU repo is at `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/`. The topic modules live in `src/topics/<topic>/`. Read the relevant view files, data files, and store files before recommending changes.\\r\\n\\r\\n2. **Read the existing vault docs.** Curriculum work lives in `raw/sources/lsat-u/curriculum/<topic>/`. Look for `_BUILD_SPEC*.md`, `MODULE_PLAN.md`, `_HANDOFF/`, `_source/`, `_archive/`. Read sources without modifying or moving them. Record supersession in the new operational brief; preserve contradictions explicitly.\\r\\n\\r\\n3. **Verify content authenticity.** Joshua authors the LSAT curriculum. Claude does not invent LSAT content. When auditing quiz files, drill banks, or lesson body content, check whether items are verbatim from `_source/` files or from git history (e.g. `git show <sha>:<path>`), or if they were fabricated by a prior Claude Code run. Flag fabrications to a `_CONTENT_AUDIT.md` rather than silently rewriting.\\r\\n\\r\\n4. **If a screenshot was provided.** Describe what is visible. List specific visual problems with concrete CSS-class-level fixes. Cross-reference against the rendering component (LessonBody.tsx, ModuleView.tsx, the per-quiz renderers) to identify the root cause.\\r\\n\\r\\n5. **Push back where appropriate.** If Joshua\'s stated direction will introduce complexity (adding features, more sidebar entries, more drill types, more chrome), say so. The bootcamp default is simpler-not-more. Preservation of the current project design system is required but everything else should reduce cognitive load.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Phase 2: Write the build brief to vault\\r\\n\\r\\nFile path convention, relative to the current AI OS root: `handoffs/lsat-u/<topic>/<BRIEF_NAME>.md`. Never write, move or archive operational briefs under `raw/sources/`.\\r\\n\\r\\nName patterns Joshua uses:\\r\\n- `MODULE_PLAN.md` (architecture-defining)\\r\\n- `<DOMAIN>_<ACTION>.md` like `LESSON_READABILITY_FIX.md`, `MODULE_QUALITY_UPGRADE.md`, `MODULE_CLOSEOUT.md`\\r\\n- `_AUDIT_<DATE>.md` for audit findings\\r\\n- `_HANDOFF/<NN>_<NAME>_BRIEF.md` only if Joshua already has that subfolder convention going\\r\\n\\r\\n**Required sections in every brief:**\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Frontmatter line: `For: Claude Code`, plus the current checkout/branch and the actual commit/push authorization. Do not assume main or permission to publish.\\r\\n2. Goal: one or two sentences.\\r\\n3. Required reading: explicit list of files CC must read before writing code. Always includes the brief itself, the relevant view files, the architecture spec (`_BUILD_SPEC_v2.0.md` or `MODULE_PLAN.md`), and the current root `CLAUDE.md`. Follow the application\'s nearest instructions.\\r\\n4. Hard rules. Always include:\\r\\n   - No em dashes. `grep -rn \'—\' src/topics/<topic>/` must return zero before push.\\r\\n   - Verbatim curriculum content. Do not paraphrase walkthroughs, vocab callouts, worked examples, or LSAT stimuli.\\r\\n   - Preserve the verified current design system; retain `brutal-*` tokens and `.topic-abstract` only where the owning module uses them.\\r\\n   - Every Supabase mutation returns `{ ok, error }`. No silent errors. Errors surface as toasts.\\r\\n   - No `(supabase as any)`. Regenerate types if schema changes.\\r\\n   - Preserve unrelated changes. Commit/push only when authorized by the task; stage only owned paths on the verified branch.\\r\\n   - Do not invent LSAT content. Stop and ask if a source is missing.\\r\\n5. Numbered steps. Always called \\"Step 1, Step 2, ...\\" not \\"Phase 1, Phase 2.\\" Joshua dislikes the word \\"phase.\\"\\r\\n6. Per step: target files, what changes, expected outcome, proposed commit message when a commit is authorized.\\r\\n7. Verification block: explicit checklist for the final push (em dash check, build, dev walk, mobile viewport, reduced motion, keyboard-only when relevant).\\r\\n8. Done-state report template using fenced code block with `DONE:` header, commits list, verification checks, open issues.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Brief length target:** 100 to 250 lines. Tight. Concrete. No filler.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Record supersession.** Link the old brief and explain the replacement in the new handoff. Do not move or delete source evidence. Preserve old operational briefs unless Joshua explicitly authorizes relocation.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Phase 3: Hand the paste box back to Joshua\\r\\n\\r\\nAfter the brief is saved, give Joshua a paste-box prompt as a markdown code block. The prompt must:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Reference the saved brief by its verified absolute path on this machine, quoted for the receiving shell.\\r\\n2. Tell CC to execute the authorized scope end to end and state the actual Git/publication permissions.\\r\\n3. Restate the hard rules in one short paragraph.\\r\\n4. List the steps in one-line summaries.\\r\\n5. Note any migrations or external actions required (e.g., \\"Apply migration via Supabase dashboard if CLI not linked\\").\\r\\n6. End with: \\"If a step blocks, stop and post status. Otherwise run all N steps and post the done-state report. Go.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n**The paste-box format is the deliverable Joshua actually uses.** It must be inside a single fenced code block so he can copy with one click. No surrounding commentary inside the code block.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Phase 4: Voice and tone rules when writing to Joshua\\r\\n\\r\\n- No em dashes anywhere. Use colons, periods, or restructure.\\r\\n- Terse. Direct. Strong opinionated recommendations, not hedged suggestions.\\r\\n- Push back when Joshua\'s direction would introduce complexity or break existing rules.\\r\\n- Skills-check rule: before creating, modifying, or recommending any skill, read `raw/sources/lsat-u/skills-reference/00_skills-strategy.md` and `01_skills-detailed-evaluation.md` first. Conform to documented patterns.\\r\\n- Modules not phases.\\r\\n- Don\'t repeat what already shipped. Just acknowledge and move on.\\r\\n- Don\'t ask multiple-choice questions when Joshua has given clear autonomy. Make the call, document it, move.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Common brief types\\r\\n\\r\\n**Audit brief** — finding gaps in shipped work. Output: `_AUDIT_<DATE>.md` plus a follow-up fix brief.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Build brief** — implementing new feature or major restructure. Output: `<DOMAIN>_PLAN.md` or `<DOMAIN>_<FEATURE>.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Fix brief** — closing specific bugs or visual issues. Output: `<DOMAIN>_FIX.md` or `<DOMAIN>_READABILITY_FIX.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Closeout brief** — finishing items left open by a prior pass. Output: `<DOMAIN>_CLOSEOUT.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Cleanup brief** — removing zombies, archiving superseded files, regenerating types. A committed range alone omits these. Reconcile status with the captured path list; report omitted/unavailable files instead of implying a complete review.\\n\\n    ## Read-Only Review')\n s=s.replace('check it out into a separate temporary directory (e.g. `git worktree add /tmp/review-[SHA] [SHA]`)','use a host-approved separate temporary checkout outside this vault, preserving existing worktree state')\n s=s.replace('- `[BASE_SHA]`','- `[REVIEW_SCOPE]` / `[REVIEW_PATHS]` - requested change classes and exact captured paths/content\\n- `[BASE_SHA]`')\n save(p,s);ap=root/act/'requesting-code-review/code-reviewer.md'\n if ap.exists():save(ap,s)\n for name in ['test-driven-development','lsatu-cc-handoff','requesting-code-review']:\n  p=root/'skills'/rt/name/'SKILL.md'\n  if not p.exists():continue\n  s=p.read_text(encoding='utf-8')\n  if name=='test-driven-development':\n   s=s.replace('- [ ] Every new function/method has a test','- [ ] Meaningful changed behaviors have focused tests or a justified relevant validation').replace('- [ ] Watched each test fail before implementing','- [ ] For new behavior/regressions, observed the expected failure where practical; recorded existing-code exceptions').replace('Production code → test exists and failed first\\nOtherwise → not TDD','New behavioral slice -> expected failure, minimal fix, green verification\\nExisting implementation -> preserve work, regression/reference verification')\n  elif name=='lsatu-cc-handoff':\n   s=s.replace('## Phase ','## Step ').replace('Flag fabrications to a `_CONTENT_AUDIT.md`','Flag fabrications in an operational `_CONTENT_AUDIT.md` under `handoffs/lsat-u/<topic>/`')\n   s=s.replace('read `raw/sources/lsat-u/skills-reference/00_skills-strategy.md` and `01_skills-detailed-evaluation.md` first. Follow current authority over historical advice.')\n   s=s.replace('- Cowork prompts: `raw/sources/lsat-u/prompts/`','- Historical prompt evidence (read-only): `raw/sources/lsat-u/prompts/`; new operational prompts: `handoffs/lsat-u/`')\n   s=s.replace('`raw/inbox/chat-exports/`','`handoffs/`')\n  elif name=='requesting-code-review':\n   s=s.replace('- `{BASE_SHA}` - Starting commit','- `{REVIEW_SCOPE}` / `{REVIEW_PATHS}` - committed/WIP scope and exact path/content inventory\\n- `{BASE_SHA}` - Starting commit for requested committed scope')\n  save(p,s);ap=root/act/name/'SKILL.md'\n  if ap.exists():save(ap,s)\nrows=[]\nfor b in backup.rglob('*'):\n if b.is_file():\n  p=root/b.relative_to(backup);rows.append({'path':str(p.relative_to(root)),'before':hashlib.sha256(b.read_bytes()).hexdigest(),'after':hashlib.sha256(p.read_bytes()).hexdigest(),'backup':str(b.relative_to(root))})\n(report/'CORE-receipts.json').write_text(json.dumps(rows,indent=2),encoding='utf-8');print('Backed-up edited files:',len(rows))\n'@ | python -",max_output_tokens:1200}));

tempfile,subprocess,json,hashlib\nroot=Path.cwd();report=root/'audits/skills-2026-10-10-repairs';rows=json.loads((report/'CORE-receipts.json').read_text(encoding='utf-8'));checks={};errors=[]\nfor r in rows:\n p=root/r['path'];b=root/r['backup']\n if hashlib.sha256(p.read_bytes()).hexdigest()!=r['after']:errors.append('hash '+r['path'])\n if hashlib.sha256(b.read_bytes()).hexdigest()!=r['before']:errors.append('backup '+r['path'])\n if p.name=='SKILL.md' and not p.read_text(encoding='utf-8').startswith('---\\n'):errors.append('frontmatter '+r['path'])\nnames='lsatu-cc-handoff chat-export code-review requesting-code-review guided-drill-assignment consultation-inquiry-drafts consultation-followup-drafts qa test-driven-development resolving-merge-conflicts setup-pre-commit bootcamp-parity-audit decision-mapping research-deep using-git-worktrees'.split();pairs=[]\nfor rt,act in [('claude','.claude/skills'),('codex','.agents/skills'),('gemini','.gemini/skills')]:\n for n in names:\n  p=root/act/n/'SKILL.md';c=root/'skills'/rt/n/'SKILL.md'\n  if p.exists() and c.exists():\n   ok=p.read_bytes()==c.read_bytes();pairs.append({'runtime':rt,'name':n,'equal':ok})\n   if not ok:errors.append('activation '+str(p))\nchecks['activation_pairs']=pairs;checks['hash_errors']=errors\n# Prediction: committed HEAD range misses one edit, WIP diffs and untracked inventory expose all three classes.\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix='ai-os-core-contract-') as td:\n d=Path(td)\n def git(*args):return subprocess.run(['git',*args],cwd=d,capture_output=True,text=True,check=True).stdout\n git('init');(d/'tracked.txt').write_text('baseline\\n');git('add','tracked.txt');git('-c','user.name=Fixture','-c','user.email=fixture@example.invalid','commit','-m','baseline')\n (d/'tracked.txt').write_text('staged\\n');git('add','tracked.txt');(d/'tracked.txt').write_text('unstaged\\n');(d/'untracked.txt').write_text('new\\n')\n checks['wip_fixture']={'committed_range_empty':git('diff','HEAD...HEAD')=='','staged_visible':'staged' in git('diff','--cached'),'unstaged_visible':'unstaged' in git('diff'),'untracked_visible':'untracked.txt' in git('ls-files','--others','--exclude-standard')}\n assert all(checks['wip_fixture'].values())\n (d/'.gitignore').write_text('worktrees/\\n');(d/'worktrees').mkdir();(d/'.worktrees').mkdir()\n old=subprocess.run(['git','check-ignore','-q','worktrees'],cwd=d).returncode==0\n actual=subprocess.run(['git','check-ignore','-q','--','.worktrees'],cwd=d).returncode==0\n with (d/'.gitignore').open('a') as f:f.write('.worktrees/\\n')\n fixed=subprocess.run(['git','check-ignore','-q','--','.worktrees'],cwd=d).returncode==0\n checks['ignore_fixture']={'old_or_accepts_wrong_location':old and not actual,'exact_location_rejects':not actual,'after_scoped_rule_passes':fixed};assert all(checks['ignore_fixture'].values())\nassert not errors\n(report/'CORE-checks.json').write_text(json.dumps(checks,indent=2),encoding='utf-8');print(json.dumps({'edited_files':len(rows),'activation_pairs':len(pairs),'hash_errors':errors,'wip_fixture':checks['wip_fixture'],'ignore_fixture':checks['ignore_fixture']},indent=2))\n'@ text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"@'\nfrom pathlib import Path\np=Path('audits/skills-2026-10-10-repairs/CORE.md')\np.write_text('''# Core workflow repairs preserve source evidence and user work\n\nCompleted 2026-10-10. Ownership: 15 named skill routes in canonical Claude/Codex/Gemini catalogs, existing corresponding project activations, and requesting-code-review's local reviewer template. No global entry, frozen snapshot, application code, live student record, Gmail draft or Git commit was changed.\n\n## Resolved instruction defects\n\n| Route | Repair | Check and limit |\n|---|---|---|\n| lsatu-cc-handoff | Explicit handoff trigger; briefs/content audits in handoffs/lsat-u; immutable sources remain read-only; actual branch/commit/push authorization; verified machine paths; current design-system authority. | Current bodies no longer require raw-source writes, moving source briefs, main or automatic push. Historical absent skills evaluation no longer blocks. |\n| chat-export | Explicit export trigger; original-session log preferred; visible-context exports labeled incomplete; missing history recorded; available file writer rather than hardcoded Desktop Commander. | Transcript template includes coverage/source/missing ranges and Assistant role. No claim missing history recovered. |\n| code-review, requesting-code-review | Distinguish committed/WIP/combined scope; staged, unstaged and untracked evidence and exact reviewed paths; host-supported reviewer; support template carries same contract. | Isolated Git fixture proves committed range empty while all three WIP classes are visible. |\n| guided-drill-assignment | Inspect/recommend/assign modes; transcript-only request makes no assignment or runner record; selection preserved, current assignment executor owns writes. | Static contract review; no actual student assignment made. |\n| consultation-inquiry-drafts | Stable inquiry thread/recipient/workflow key; paginate draft lookup; reuse matching draft ID; lookup failure blocks creation; read-after-write and ledger identifiers. | No real Gmail read/write performed. |\n| consultation-followup-drafts | Same idempotent event/recipient/workflow draft contract; stale Codex connector/archive/template instructions aligned with current Claude GWS/current template. | Repeated sweep contract preserves one unsent draft; live repeat acceptance untested. |\n| qa | Report by default; issue publication only when authorized; check existing issues and preserve exact body via body-file. | No GitHub issue created. |\n| test-driven-development | Removed delete/restart ceremony, blanket no-exception claims and destructive rationalization table; preserve code; meaningful regression checks; proportional configuration/docs validation. | Codex vertical-slice supplement retained; no existing implementation deleted. |\n| resolving-merge-conflicts | Baseline/index preservation; stage resolved paths only; finish bounded to authorized operation; unresolved conflicts remain explicit. | No real merge/rebase/commit performed. |\n| setup-pre-commit | Inspect/merge existing hooks/config/scripts; preserve prepare behavior; scoped staging and authorized commit; smoke test isolated or explicitly scoped. | No hook installation or actual index mutation performed. |\n| using-git-worktrees | Ignore check uses selected location only; scoped ignore adjustment and no automatic commit. | Isolated fixture demonstrates old OR condition accepts wrong directory; exact check rejects then passes after correct rule. |\n| decision-mapping | Correct domain-modeling route; actual installed host entry points; optional prototype/to-prd discovery; superseded nodes retained. | Exact misspelling removed; no planning files deleted. |\n| bootcamp-parity-audit | Discover authoritative current anchors; record paths/revisions; absent benchmark means limited observations, no fabricated full parity verdict. | All three legacy benchmark filenames absent in searched archive/wiki/references; authoritative benchmark recovery remains a dependency limit. |\n| research-deep | Resolve activated package/helper before dispatch; verified validator_path parameter; unavailable helper cannot produce a validation claim; use actual host researcher roles. | No validate_json.py found in skills, including hidden/unignored. Validator dependency remains explicitly blocked rather than silently passed. |\n\n## Receipts and behavioral evidence\n\n[CORE-receipts.json](CORE-receipts.json) records original and repaired SHA-256 values plus path-preserving backups for 60 files. [CORE-checks.json](CORE-checks.json) records 14 existing activation pairs matching canonical bytes, zero hash/backup/frontmatter mismatches, and both isolated Git behavioral counterexamples.\n\nDirect truncation writes to one file returned Windows OSError 22. ACL/read checks succeeded; guarded sibling-temp atomic replacement worked. No sync service was stopped and no permissions were changed. Backups remain intact. Python was executed inline; no Python source file was created or edited, so the edited-file Python execution gate is not applicable.\n\n## Seven method grades\n\nFollowing [hot](../../wiki/karpathy/hot.md) and [rules](../../wiki/karpathy/rules.md): mechanism reconstruction pass (request -> mode -> scope -> output), small baseline pass (one tracked file), prediction/run/comparison pass (committed miss versus complete WIP inventory), counterexample/fix pass (selected ignore location failure then rule correction), relevant execution pass within local static/fixture scope (hashes, activation equality, Git fixtures), explicit assumptions pass (no native selector/live-service certification), justified simplicity pass (targeted contracts and preserved runtime supplement). Original source artifacts remain untouched.\n\n## What I ran\n\nCurrent bodies, runtime differences, owned support template, existing nested instruction discovery, audit row reasons, scoped path checks, 60-file hash reconciliation, 14 activation comparisons and isolated WIP/ignore Git fixtures.\n\n## What came out\n\nConfirmed instruction defects repaired, originals preserved, current activation copies matching their own runtime catalogs. Missing authoritative benchmark anchors and research validator remain explicit dependency limits; no fabricated replacement or validation success.\n\n## What I changed\n\n60 owned catalog/activation/template files and local repair reports/receipts. No deletion, global mutation, frozen snapshot change, live write, commit or push.\n''',encoding='utf-8')\nprint('Saved CORE.md')\n'@ | python -",max_output_tokens:1000}));
