Add to Claude Desktop or any MCP-compatible agent orchestrator so other agents can query the graph live.\\r\\n\\r\\nTo configure in Claude Desktop, add to `claude_desktop_config.json`:\\r\\n```json\\r\\n{\\r\\n  \\"mcpServers\\": {\\r\\n    \\"graphify\\": {\\r\\n      \\"command\\": \\"python3\\",\\r\\n      \\"args\\": [\\"-m\\", \\"graphify.serve\\", \\"/absolute/path/to/graphify-out/graph.json\\"]\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n### Step 8 - Token reduction benchmark (only if total_words > 5000)\\r\\n\\r\\nIf `total_words` from `graphify-out/.graphify_detect.json` is greater than 5,000, run:\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n$(cat graphify-out/.graphify_python) -c \\"\\r\\nimport json\\r\\nfrom graphify.benchmark import run_benchmark, print_benchmark\\r\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\r\\n\\r\\ndetection = json.loads(Path(\'graphify-out/.graphify_detect.json\').read_text())\\r\\nresult = run_benchmark(\'graphify-out/graph.json\', corpus_words=detection[\'total_words\'])\\r\\nprint_benchmark(result)\\r\\n\\"\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nPrint the output directly in chat. If `total_words <= 5000`, skip silently - the graph value is structural clarity, not token compression, for small corpora.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n### Step 9 - Save manifest, update cost tracker, clean up, and report\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n$(cat graphify-out/.graphify_python) -c \\"\\r\\nimport json\\r\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\r\\nfrom datetime import datetime, timezone\\r\\nfrom graphify.detect import save_manifest\\r\\n\\r\\n# Save manifest for --update\\r\\ndetect = json.loads(Path(\'graphify-out/.graphify_detect.json\').read_text())\\r\\nsave_manifest(detect[\'files\'])\\r\\n\\r\\n# Update cumulative cost tracker\\r\\nextract = json.loads(Path(\'graphify-out/.graphify_extract.json\').read_text())\\r\\ninput_tok = extract.get(\'input_tokens\', 0)\\r\\noutput_tok = extract.get(\'output_tokens\', 0)\\r\\n\\r\\ncost_path = Path(\'graphify-out/cost.json\')\\r\\nif cost_path.exists():\\r\\n    cost = json.loads(cost_path.read_text())\\r\\nelse:\\r\\n    cost = {\'runs\': [], \'total_input_tokens\': 0, \'total_output_tokens\': 0}\\r\\n\\r\\ncost[\'runs\'].append({\\r\\n    \'date\': datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(),\\r\\n    \'input_tokens\': input_tok,\\r\\n    \'output_tokens\': output_tok,\\r\\n    \'files\': detect.get(\'total_files\', 0),\\r\\n})\\r\\ncost[\'total_input_tokens\'] += input_tok\\r\\ncost[\'total_output_tokens\'] += output_tok\\r\\ncost_path.write_text(json.dumps(cost, indent=2))\\r\\n\\r\\nprint(f\'This run: {input_tok:,} input tokens, {output_tok:,} output tokens\')\\r\\nprint(f\'All time: {cost[\\\\\\"total_input_tokens\\\\\\"]:,} input, {cost[\\\\\\"total_output_tokens\\\\\\"]:,} output ({len(cost[\\\\\\"runs\\\\\\"])} runs)\')\\r\\n\\"\\r\\nRetain intermediate captures for verification; remove only after explicit authorization.\\r\\nRetain intermediate captures for verification; remove only after explicit authorization.\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nTell the user (omit the obsidian line unless --obsidian was given):\\r\\n```\\r\\nGraph complete. Outputs in PATH_TO_DIR/graphify-out/\\r\\n\\r\\n  graph.html            - interactive graph, open in browser\\r\\n  GRAPH_REPORT.md       - audit report\\r\\n  graph.json            - raw graph data\\r\\n  obsidian/             - Obsidian vault (only if --obsidian was given)\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nIf graphify saved you time, consider supporting it: https://github.com/sponsors/safishamsi\\r\\n\\r\\nReplace PATH_TO_DIR with the actual absolute path of the directory that was processed.\\r\\n\\r\\nThen paste these sections from GRAPH_REPORT.md directly into the chat:\\r\\n- God Nodes\\r\\n- Surprising Connections\\r\\n- Suggested Questions\\r\\n\\r\\nDo NOT paste the full report - just those three sections. Keep it concise.\\r\\n\\r\\nThen immediately offer to explore. Pick the single most interesting suggested question from the report - the one that crosses the most community boundaries or has the most surprising bridge node - and ask:\\r\\n\\r\\n> \\"The most interesting question this graph can answer: **[question]**. Want me to trace it?\\"\\r\\n\\r\\nIf the user says yes, run `/graphify query \\"[question]\\"` on the graph and walk them through the answer using the graph structure - which nodes connect, which community boundaries get crossed, what the path reveals. Keep going as long as they want to explore. Each answer should end with a natural follow-up (\\"this connects to X - want to go deeper?\\") so the session feels like navigation, not a one-shot report.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe graph is the map. Your job after the pipeline is to be the guide.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## Interpreter guard for subcommands\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore running any subcommand below (`--update`, `--cluster-only`, `query`, `path`, `explain`, `add`), check that `.graphify_python` exists. If it\'s missing (e.g. user deleted `graphify-out/`), re-resolve the interpreter first:\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\nif [ ! -f graphify-out/.graphify_python ]; then\\r\\n    GRAPHIFY_BIN=$(which graphify 2>/dev/null)\\r\\n    if [ -n \\"$GRAPHIFY_BIN\\" ]; then\\r\\n        PYTHON=$(head -1 \\"$GRAPHIFY_BIN\\" | tr -d \'#!\')\\r\\n        case \\"$PYTHON\\" in *[!a-zA-Z0-9/_.-]*) PYTHON=\\"python3\\" ;; esac\\r\\n    else\\r\\n        PYTHON=\\"python3\\"\\r\\n    fi\\r\\n    mkdir -p graphify-out\\r\\n    \\"$PYTHON\\" -c \\"import sys; open(\'graphify-out/.graphify_python\', \'w\').write(sys.executable)\\"\\r\\nfi\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n## For --update (incremental re-extraction)\\r\\n\\r\\nFor changed/deleted inputs, perform a fresh full rebuild until incremental pruning is verified; the incremental code below is reference-only and must not run in that mode.\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n$(cat graphify-out/.graphify_python) -c \\"\\r\\nimport sys, json\\r\\nfrom graphify.detect import detect_incremental, save_manifest\\r\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\r\\n\\r\\nresult = detect_incremental(Path(\'INPUT_PATH\'))\\r\\nnew_total = result.get(\'new_total\', 0)\\r\\nprint(json.dumps(result, indent=2))\\r\\nPath(\'graphify-out/.graphify_incremental.json\').write_text(json.dumps(result))\\r\\nif new_total == 0:\\r\\n    print(\'No files changed since last run. Nothing to update.\')\\r\\n    raise SystemExit(0)\\r\\nprint(f\'{new_total} new/changed file(s) to re-extract.\')\\r\\n\\"\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nIf new files exist, first check whether all changed files are code files:\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n$(cat graphify-out/.graphify_python) -c \\"\\r\\nimport json\\r\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\r\\n\\r\\nresult = json.loads(open(\'graphify-out/.graphify_incremental.json\').read()) if Path(\'graphify-out/.graphify_incremental.json\').exists() else {}\\r\\ncode_exts = {\'.py\',\'.ts\',\'.js\',\'.go\',\'.rs\',\'.java\',\'.cpp\',\'.c\',\'.rb\',\'.swift\',\'.kt\',\'.cs\',\'.scala\',\'.php\',\'.cc\',\'.cxx\',\'.hpp\',\'.h\',\'.kts\',\'.lua\',\'.toc\'}\\r\\nnew_files = result.get(\'new_files\', {})\\r\\nall_changed = [f for files in new_files.values() for f in files]\\r\\ncode_only = all(Path(f).suffix.lower() in code_exts for f in all_changed)\\r\\nprint(\'code_only:\', code_only)\\r\\n\\"\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nIf `code_only` is True: print `[graphify update] Code-only changes detected - skipping semantic extraction (no LLM needed)`, run only Step 3A (AST) on the changed files, skip Step 3B entirely (no subagents), then go straight to merge and Steps 4–8.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf `code_only` is False (any changed file is a doc/paper/image): run the full Steps 3A–3C pipeline as normal.\\r\\n\\r\\nThen:\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n$(cat graphify-out/.graphify_python) -c \\"\\r\\nimport sys, json\\r\\nfrom graphify.build import build_from_json\\r\\nfrom graphify.export import to_json\\r\\nfrom networkx.readwrite import json_graph\\r\\nimport networkx as nx\\r\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\r\\n\\r\\n# Load existing graph\\r\\nexisting_data = json.loads(Path(\'graphify-out/graph.json\').read_text())\\r\\nG_existing = json_graph.node_link_graph(existing_data, edges=\'links\')\\r\\n\\r\\n# Load new extraction\\r\\nnew_extraction = json.loads(Path(\'graphify-out/.graphify_extract.json\').read_text())\\r\\nG_new = build_from_json(new_extraction)\\r\\n\\r\\n# Prune nodes from deleted files\\r\\nincremental = json.loads(Path(\'graphify-out/.graphify_incremental.json\').read_text())\\r\\ndeleted = set(incremental.get(\'deleted_files\', []))\\r\\nif deleted:\\r\\n    to_remove = [n for n, d in G_existing.nodes(data=True) if d.get(\'source_file\') in deleted]\\r\\n    G_existing.remove_nodes_from(to_remove)\\r\\n    print(f\'Pruned {len(to_remove)} ghost nodes from {len(deleted)} deleted file(s)\')\\r\\n\\r\\n# Merge: new nodes/edges into existing graph\\r\\nG_existing.update(G_new)\\r\\nprint(f\'Merged: {G_existing.number_of_nodes()} nodes, {G_existing.number_of_edges()} edges\')\\r\\n\\r\\n# Write merged result back to .graphify_extract.json so Step 4 sees the full graph\\r\\nmerged_out = {\\r\\n    \'nodes\': [{\'id\': n, **d} for n, d in G_existing.nodes(data=True)],\\r\\n    \'edges\': [{\'source\': u, \'target\': v, **d} for u, v, d in G_existing.edges(data=True)],\\r\\n    \'hyperedges\': new_extraction.get(\'hyperedges\', []),\\r\\n    \'input_tokens\': new_extraction.get(\'input_tokens\', 0),\\r\\n    \'output_tokens\': new_extraction.get(\'output_tokens\', 0),\\r\\n}\\r\\nPath(\'graphify-out/.graphify_extract.json\').write_text(json.dumps(merged_out))\\r\\nprint(f\'[graphify update] Merged extraction written ({len(merged_out[\\\\\\"nodes\\\\\\"])} nodes, {len(merged_out[\\\\\\"edges\\\\\\"])} edges)\')\\r\\n\\" \\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThen run Steps 4–8 on the merged graph as normal.\\r\\n\\r\\nAfter Step 4, show the graph diff:\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n$(cat graphify-out/.graphify_python) -c \\"\\r\\nimport json\\r\\nfrom graphify.analyze import graph_diff\\r\\nfrom graphify.build import build_from_json\\r\\nfrom networkx.readwrite import json_graph\\r\\nimport networkx as nx\\r\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\r\\n\\r\\n# Load old graph (before update) from backup written before merge\\r\\nold_data = json.loads(Path(\'graphify-out/.graphify_old.json\').read_text()) if Path(\'graphify-out/.graphify_old.json\').exists() else None\\r\\nnew_extract = json.loads(Path(\'graphify-out/.graphify_extract.json\').read_text())\\r\\nG_new = build_from_json(new_extract)\\r\\n\\r\\nif old_data:\\r\\n    G_old = json_graph.node_link_graph(old_data, edges=\'links\')\\r\\n    diff = graph_diff(G_old, G_new)\\r\\n    print(diff[\'summary\'])\\r\\n    if diff[\'new_nodes\']:\\r\\n        print(\'New nodes:\', \', \'.join(n[\'label\'] for n in diff[\'new_nodes\'][:5]))\\r\\n    if diff[\'new_edges\']:\\r\\n        print(\'New edges:\', len(diff[\'new_edges\']))\\r\\n\\"\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore the merge step, save the old graph: `cp graphify-out/graph.json graphify-out/.graphify_old.json`\\r\\nRetain intermediate captures for verification; remove only after explicit authorization.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## For --cluster-only\\r\\n\\r\\nSkip Steps 1–3. Load the existing graph from `graphify-out/graph.json` and re-run clustering:\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n$(cat graphify-out/.graphify_python) -c \\"\\r\\nimport sys, json\\r\\nfrom graphify.cluster import cluster, score_all\\r\\nfrom graphify.analyze import god_nodes, surprising_connections\\r\\nfrom graphify.report import generate\\r\\nfrom graphify.export import to_json\\r\\nfrom networkx.readwrite import json_graph\\r\\nimport networkx as nx\\r\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\r\\n\\r\\ndata = json.loads(Path(\'graphify-out/graph.json\').read_text())\\r\\nG = json_graph.node_link_graph(data, edges=\'links\')\\r\\n\\r\\ndetection = {\'total_files\': 0, \'total_words\': 99999, \'needs_graph\': True, \'warning\': None,\\r\\n             \'files\': {\'code\': [], \'document\': [], \'paper\': []}}\\r\\ntokens = {\'input\': 0, \'output\': 0}\\r\\n\\r\\ncommunities = cluster(G)\\r\\ncohesion = score_all(G, communities)\\r\\ngods = god_nodes(G)\\r\\nsurprises = surprising_connections(G, communities)\\r\\nlabels = {cid: \'Community \' + str(cid) for cid in communities}\\r\\n\\r\\nreport = generate(G, communities, cohesion, labels, gods, surprises, detection, tokens, \'.\')\\r\\nPath(\'graphify-out/GRAPH_REPORT.md\').write_text(report)\\r\\nto_json(G, communities, \'graphify-out/graph.json\')\\r\\n\\r\\nanalysis = {\\r\\n    \'communities\': {str(k): v for k, v in communities.items()},\\r\\n    \'cohesion\': {str(k): v for k, v in cohesion.items()},\\r\\n    \'gods\': gods,\\r\\n    \'surprises\': surprises,\\r\\n}\\r\\nPath(\'graphify-out/.graphify_analysis.json\').write_text(json.dumps(analysis, indent=2))\\r\\nprint(f\'Re-clustered: {len(communities)} communities\')\\r\\n\\"\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThen run Steps 5–9 as normal (label communities, generate viz, benchmark, clean up, report).\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## For /graphify query\\r\\n\\r\\nTwo traversal modes - choose based on the question:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Mode | Flag | Best for |\\r\\n|------|------|----------|\\r\\n| BFS (default) | _(none)_ | \\"What is X connected to?\\" - broad context, nearest neighbors first |\\r\\n| DFS | `--dfs` | \\"How does X reach Y?\\" - trace a specific chain or dependency path |\\r\\n\\r\\nFirst check the graph exists:\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n$(cat graphify-out/.graphify_python) -c \\"\\r\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\r\\nif not Path(\'graphify-out/graph.json\').exists():\\r\\n    print(\'ERROR: No graph found. Run /graphify <path> first to build the graph.\')\\r\\n    raise SystemExit(1)\\r\\n\\"\\r\\n```\\r\\nIf it fails, stop and tell the user to run `/graphify <path>` first.\\r\\n\\r\\nLoad `graphify-out/graph.json`, then:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Find the 1-3 nodes whose label best matches key terms in the question.\\r\\n2. Run the appropriate traversal from each starting node.\\r\\n3. Read the subgraph - node labels, edge relations, confidence tags, source locations.\\r\\n4. Answer using **only** what the graph contains. Quote `source_location` when citing a specific fact.\\r\\n5. If the graph lacks enough information, say so - do not hallucinate edges.\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n"}'}]

## Tool: exec

text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"Get-Content skills/claude/graphify/SKILL.md | Select-Object -Skip 1050",max_output_tokens:8500}));
text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"Get-Content skills/claude/teach/SKILL.md -Raw",max_output_tokens:4500}));

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 14.5 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"2ba33e","wall_time_seconds":0.8217804,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":2990,"output":"$(cat graphify-out/.graphify_python) -c \\"\\r\\nimport sys, json\\r\\nfrom networkx.readwrite import json_graph\\r\\nimport networkx as nx\\r\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\r\\n\\r\\ndata = json.loads(Path(\'graphify-out/graph.json\').read_text())\\r\\nG = json_graph.node_link_graph(data, edges=\'links\')\\r\\n\\r\\nquestion = \'QUESTION\'\\r\\nmode = \'MODE\'  # \'bfs\' or \'dfs\'\\r\\nterms = [t.lower() for t in question.split() if len(t) > 3]\\r\\n\\r\\n# Find best-matching start nodes\\r\\nscored = []\\r\\nfor nid, ndata in G.nodes(data=True):\\r\\n    label = ndata.get(\'label\', \'\').lower()\\r\\n    score = sum(1 for t in terms if t in label)\\r\\n    if score > 0:\\r\\n        scored.append((score, nid))\\r\\nscored.sort(reverse=True)\\r\\nstart_nodes = [nid for _, nid in scored[:3]]\\r\\n\\r\\nif not start_nodes:\\r\\n    print(\'No matching nodes found for query terms:\', terms)\\r\\n    sys.exit(0)\\r\\n\\r\\nsubgraph_nodes = set()\\r\\nsubgraph_edges = []\\r\\n\\r\\nif mode == \'dfs\':\\r\\n    # DFS: follow one path as deep as possible before backtracking.\\r\\n    # Depth-limited to 6 to avoid traversing the whole graph.\\r\\n    visited = set()\\r\\n    stack = [(n, 0) for n in reversed(start_nodes)]\\r\\n    while stack:\\r\\n        node, depth = stack.pop()\\r\\n        if node in visited or depth > 6:\\r\\n            continue\\r\\n        visited.add(node)\\r\\n        subgraph_nodes.add(node)\\r\\n        for neighbor in G.neighbors(node):\\r\\n            if neighbor not in visited:\\r\\n                stack.append((neighbor, depth + 1))\\r\\n                subgraph_edges.append((node, neighbor))\\r\\nelse:\\r\\n    # BFS: explore all neighbors layer by layer up to depth 3.\\r\\n    frontier = set(start_nodes)\\r\\n    subgraph_nodes = set(start_nodes)\\r\\n    for _ in range(3):\\r\\n        next_frontier = set()\\r\\n        for n in frontier:\\r\\n            for neighbor in G.neighbors(n):\\r\\n                if neighbor not in subgraph_nodes:\\r\\n                    next_frontier.add(neighbor)\\r\\n                    subgraph_edges.append((n, neighbor))\\r\\n        subgraph_nodes.update(next_frontier)\\r\\n        frontier = next_frontier\\r\\n\\r\\n# Token-budget aware output: rank by relevance, cut at budget (~4 chars/token)\\r\\ntoken_budget = BUDGET  # default 2000\\r\\nchar_budget = token_budget * 4\\r\\n\\r\\n# Score each node by term overlap for ranked output\\r\\ndef relevance(nid):\\r\\n    label = G.nodes[nid].get(\'label\', \'\').lower()\\r\\n    return sum(1 for t in terms if t in label)\\r\\n\\r\\nranked_nodes = sorted(subgraph_nodes, key=relevance, reverse=True)\\r\\n\\r\\nlines = [f\'Traversal: {mode.upper()} | Start: {[G.nodes[n].get(\\\\\\"label\\\\\\",n) for n in start_nodes]} | {len(subgraph_nodes)} nodes\']\\r\\nfor nid in ranked_nodes:\\r\\n    d = G.nodes[nid]\\r\\n    lines.append(f\'  NODE {d.get(\\\\\\"label\\\\\\", nid)} [src={d.get(\\\\\\"source_file\\\\\\",\\\\\\"\\\\\\")} loc={d.get(\\\\\\"source_location\\\\\\",\\\\\\"\\\\\\")}]\')\\r\\nfor u, v in subgraph_edges:\\r\\n    if u in subgraph_nodes and v in subgraph_nodes:\\r\\n        d = G.edges[u, v]\\r\\n        lines.append(f\'  EDGE {G.nodes[u].get(\\\\\\"label\\\\\\",u)} --{d.get(\\\\\\"relation\\\\\\",\\\\\\"\\\\\\")} [{d.get(\\\\\\"confidence\\\\\\",\\\\\\"\\\\\\")}]--> {G.nodes[v].get(\\\\\\"label\\\\\\",v)}\')\\r\\n\\r\\noutput = \'\\\\n\'.join(lines)\\r\\nif len(output) > char_budget:\\r\\n    output = output[:char_budget] + f\'\\\\n... (truncated at ~{token_budget} token budget - use --budget N for more)\'\\r\\nprint(output)\\r\\n\\"\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nReplace `QUESTION` with the user\'s actual question, `MODE` with `bfs` or `dfs`, and `BUDGET` with the token budget (default `2000`, or whatever `--budget N` specifies). Then answer based on the subgraph output above.\\r\\n\\r\\nAfter writing the answer, save it back into the graph so it improves future queries:\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n$(cat graphify-out/.graphify_python) -m graphify save-result --question \\"QUESTION\\" --answer \\"ANSWER\\" --type query --nodes NODE1 NODE2\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nReplace `QUESTION` with the question, `ANSWER` with your full answer text, `SOURCE_NODES` with the list of node labels you cited. This closes the feedback loop: the next `--update` will extract this Q&A as a node in the graph.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## For /graphify path\\r\\n\\r\\nFind the shortest path between two named concepts in the graph.\\r\\n\\r\\nFirst check the graph exists:\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n$(cat graphify-out/.graphify_python) -c \\"\\r\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\r\\nif not Path(\'graphify-out/graph.json\').exists():\\r\\n    print(\'ERROR: No graph found. Run /graphify <path> first to build the graph.\')\\r\\n    raise SystemExit(1)\\r\\n\\"\\r\\n```\\r\\nIf it fails, stop and tell the user to run `/graphify <path>` first.\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n$(cat graphify-out/.graphify_python) -c \\"\\r\\nimport json, sys\\r\\nimport networkx as nx\\r\\nfrom networkx.readwrite import json_graph\\r\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\r\\n\\r\\ndata = json.loads(Path(\'graphify-out/graph.json\').read_text())\\r\\nG = json_graph.node_link_graph(data, edges=\'links\')\\r\\n\\r\\na_term = \'NODE_A\'\\r\\nb_term = \'NODE_B\'\\r\\n\\r\\ndef find_node(term):\\r\\n    term = term.lower()\\r\\n    scored = sorted(\\r\\n        [(sum(1 for w in term.split() if w in G.nodes[n].get(\'label\',\'\').lower()), n)\\r\\n         for n in G.nodes()],\\r\\n        reverse=True\\r\\n    )\\r\\n    return scored[0][1] if scored and scored[0][0] > 0 else None\\r\\n\\r\\nsrc = find_node(a_term)\\r\\ntgt = find_node(b_term)\\r\\n\\r\\nif not src or not tgt:\\r\\n    print(f\'Could not find nodes matching: {a_term!r} or {b_term!r}\')\\r\\n    sys.exit(0)\\r\\n\\r\\ntry:\\r\\n    path = nx.shortest_path(G, src, tgt)\\r\\n    print(f\'Shortest path ({len(path)-1} hops):\')\\r\\n    for i, nid in enumerate(path):\\r\\n        label = G.nodes[nid].get(\'label\', nid)\\r\\n        if i < len(path) - 1:\\r\\n            edge = G.edges[nid, path[i+1]]\\r\\n            rel = edge.get(\'relation\', \'\')\\r\\n            conf = edge.get(\'confidence\', \'\')\\r\\n            print(f\'  {label} --{rel}--> [{conf}]\')\\r\\n        else:\\r\\n            print(f\'  {label}\')\\r\\nexcept nx.NetworkXNoPath:\\r\\n    print(f\'No path found between {a_term!r} and {b_term!r}\')\\r\\nexcept nx.NodeNotFound as e:\\r\\n    print(f\'Node not found: {e}\')\\r\\n\\"\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nReplace `NODE_A` and `NODE_B` with the actual concept names from the user. Then explain the path in plain language - what each hop means, why it\'s significant.\\r\\n\\r\\nAfter writing the explanation, save it back:\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n$(cat graphify-out/.graphify_python) -m graphify save-result --question \\"Path from NODE_A to NODE_B\\" --answer \\"ANSWER\\" --type path_query --nodes NODE_A NODE_B\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## For /graphify explain\\r\\n\\r\\nGive a plain-language explanation of a single node - everything connected to it.\\r\\n\\r\\nFirst check the graph exists:\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n$(cat graphify-out/.graphify_python) -c \\"\\r\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\r\\nif not Path(\'graphify-out/graph.json\').exists():\\r\\n    print(\'ERROR: No graph found. Run /graphify <path> first to build the graph.\')\\r\\n    raise SystemExit(1)\\r\\n\\"\\r\\n```\\r\\nIf it fails, stop and tell the user to run `/graphify <path>` first.\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n$(cat graphify-out/.graphify_python) -c \\"\\r\\nimport json, sys\\r\\nimport networkx as nx\\r\\nfrom networkx.readwrite import json_graph\\r\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\r\\n\\r\\ndata = json.loads(Path(\'graphify-out/graph.json\').read_text())\\r\\nG = json_graph.node_link_graph(data, edges=\'links\')\\r\\n\\r\\nterm = \'NODE_NAME\'\\r\\nterm_lower = term.lower()\\r\\n\\r\\n# Find best matching node\\r\\nscored = sorted(\\r\\n    [(sum(1 for w in term_lower.split() if w in G.nodes[n].get(\'label\',\'\').lower()), n)\\r\\n     for n in G.nodes()],\\r\\n    reverse=True\\r\\n)\\r\\nif not scored or scored[0][0] == 0:\\r\\n    print(f\'No node matching {term!r}\')\\r\\n    sys.exit(0)\\r\\n\\r\\nnid = scored[0][1]\\r\\ndata_n = G.nodes[nid]\\r\\nprint(f\'NODE: {data_n.get(\\\\\\"label\\\\\\", nid)}\')\\r\\nprint(f\'  source: {data_n.get(\\\\\\"source_file\\\\\\",\\\\\\"unknown\\\\\\")}\')\\r\\nprint(f\'  type: {data_n.get(\\\\\\"file_type\\\\\\",\\\\\\"unknown\\\\\\")}\')\\r\\nprint(f\'  degree: {G.degree(nid)}\')\\r\\nprint()\\r\\nprint(\'CONNECTIONS:\')\\r\\nfor neighbor in G.neighbors(nid):\\r\\n    edge = G.edges[nid, neighbor]\\r\\n    nlabel = G.nodes[neighbor].get(\'label\', neighbor)\\r\\n    rel = edge.get(\'relation\', \'\')\\r\\n    conf = edge.get(\'confidence\', \'\')\\r\\n    src_file = G.nodes[neighbor].get(\'source_file\', \'\')\\r\\n    print(f\'  --{rel}--> {nlabel} [{conf}] ({src_file})\')\\r\\n\\"\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nReplace `NODE_NAME` with the concept the user asked about. Then write a 3-5 sentence explanation of what this node is, what it connects to, and why those connections are significant. Use the source locations as citations.\\r\\n\\r\\nAfter writing the explanation, save it back:\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n$(cat graphify-out/.graphify_python) -m graphify save-result --question \\"Explain NODE_NAME\\" --answer \\"ANSWER\\" --type explain --nodes NODE_NAME\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## For /graphify add\\r\\n\\r\\nFetch a URL and add it to the corpus, then update the graph.\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n$(cat graphify-out/.graphify_python) -c \\"\\r\\nimport sys\\r\\nfrom graphify.ingest import ingest\\r\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\r\\n\\r\\ntry:\\r\\n    out = ingest(\'URL\', Path(\'./raw\'), author=\'AUTHOR\', contributor=\'CONTRIBUTOR\')\\r\\n    print(f\'Saved to {out}\')\\r\\nexcept ValueError as e:\\r\\n    print(f\'error: {e}\', file=sys.stderr)\\r\\n    sys.exit(1)\\r\\nexcept RuntimeError as e:\\r\\n    print(f\'error: {e}\', file=sys.stderr)\\r\\n    sys.exit(1)\\r\\n\\"\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nReplace `URL` with the actual URL, `AUTHOR` with the user\'s name if provided, `CONTRIBUTOR` likewise. If the command exits with an error, tell the user what went wrong - do not silently continue. After a successful save, automatically run the `--update` pipeline on `./raw` to merge the new file into the existing graph.\\r\\n\\r\\nSupported URL types (auto-detected):\\r\\n- YouTube / any video URL → audio downloaded via yt-dlp, transcribed to `.txt` on next run (requires `pip install \'graphifyy[video]\'`)\\r\\n- Twitter/X → fetched via oEmbed, saved as `.md` with tweet text and author\\r\\n- arXiv → abstract + metadata saved as `.md`\\r\\n- PDF → downloaded as `.pdf`\\r\\n- Images (.png/.jpg/.webp) → downloaded, Claude vision extracts on next run\\r\\n- Any webpage → converted to markdown via html2text\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## For --watch\\r\\n\\r\\nStart a background watcher that monitors a folder and auto-updates the graph when files change.\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\npython3 -m graphify.watch INPUT_PATH --debounce 3\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nReplace INPUT_PATH with the folder to watch. Behavior depends on what changed:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Code files only (.py, .ts, .go, etc.):** re-runs AST extraction + rebuild + cluster immediately, no LLM needed. `graph.json` and `GRAPH_REPORT.md` are updated automatically.\\r\\n- **Docs, papers, or images:** writes a `graphify-out/needs_update` flag and prints a notification to run `/graphify --update` (LLM semantic re-extraction required).\\r\\n\\r\\nDebounce (default 3s): waits until file activity stops before triggering, so a wave of parallel agent writes doesn\'t trigger a rebuild per file.\\r\\n\\r\\nPress Ctrl+C to stop.\\r\\n\\r\\nFor agentic workflows: run `--watch` in a background terminal. Code changes from agent waves are picked up automatically between waves. If agents are also writing docs or notes, you\'ll need a manual `/graphify --update` after those waves.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## For git commit hook\\r\\n\\r\\nInstall a post-commit hook that auto-rebuilds the graph after every commit. No background process needed - triggers once per commit, works with any editor.\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\ngraphify hook install    # install\\r\\ngraphify hook uninstall  # remove\\r\\ngraphify hook status     # check\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nAfter every `git commit`, the hook detects which code files changed (via `git diff HEAD~1`), re-runs AST extraction on those files, and rebuilds `graph.json` and `GRAPH_REPORT.md`. Doc/image changes are ignored by the hook - run `/graphify --update` manually for those.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf a post-commit hook already exists, graphify appends to it rather than replacing it.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## For native CLAUDE.md integration\\r\\n\\r\\nRun once per project to make graphify always-on in Claude Code sessions:\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\ngraphify claude install\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThis writes a `## graphify` section to the local `CLAUDE.md` that instructs Claude to check the graph before answering codebase questions and rebuild it after code changes. No manual `/graphify` needed in future sessions.\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\ngraphify claude uninstall  # remove the section\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## Honesty Rules\\r\\n\\r\\n- Never invent an edge. If unsure, use AMBIGUOUS.\\r\\n- Never skip the corpus check warning.\\r\\n- Always show token cost in the report.\\r\\n- Never hide cohesion scores behind symbols - show the raw number.\\r\\n- Never run HTML viz on a graph with more than 5,000 nodes without warning the user.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"713de6","wall_time_seconds":0.629001,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":2502,"output":"---\\nname: teach\\ndescription: Teach the user a new skill or concept, within this workspace.\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\nargument-hint: \\"What would you like to learn about?\\"\\n---\\n\\n## Current AI OS execution contract\\n\\nChoose a dedicated sibling learning project before creating MISSION, lessons, assets or dependencies. Do not initialize an executable lesson workspace in the Obsidian vault. Assessment quality does not require identical word counts.\\n\\nThe user has asked you to teach them something. This is a stateful request - they intend to learn the topic over multiple sessions.\\n\\n## Teaching Workspace\\n\\nTreat the current directory as a teaching workspace. Workspace paths (`./lessons/` and the rest) resolve from the directory `/teach` was run in; only the `*-FORMAT.md` links resolve from this skill\'s folder. The state of their learning is captured in this directory in several files:\\n\\n- `MISSION.md`: A document capturing the _reason_ the user is interested in the topic. This should be used to ground all teaching. Use the format in [MISSION-FORMAT.md](./MISSION-FORMAT.md).\\n- `./reference/*.html`: A directory of reference materials. These are the compressed learnings from the lessons - cheat sheets, reference algorithms, syntax, yoga poses, glossaries. They are the raw units of learning. They should be beautiful documents which print out well, and are designed for quick reference.\\n- `RESOURCES.md`: A list of resources which can be explored to ground your teaching in contextual knowledge, or to acquire knowledge and wisdom. Use the format in [RESOURCES-FORMAT.md](./RESOURCES-FORMAT.md).\\n- `./learning-records/*.md`: A directory of learning records, which capture what the user has learned. These are loosely equivalent to architectural decision records in software development - they capture non-obvious lessons and key insights that may need to be revised later, or drive future sessions. These should be used to calculate the zone of proximal development. They are titled `0001-<dash-case-name>.md`, where the number increments each time. Use the format in [LEARNING-RECORD-FORMAT.md](./LEARNING-RECORD-FORMAT.md).\\n- `./lessons/*.html`: A directory of lessons. A **lesson** is a single, self-contained HTML output that teaches one tightly-scoped thing tied to the mission. This is the primary unit of teaching in this workspace.\\n- `./assets/*`: Reusable **components** shared across lessons. See [Assets](#assets).\\n- `NOTES.md`: A scratchpad for you to jot down user preferences, or working notes.\\n\\n## Philosophy\\n\\nTo learn at a deep level, the user needs three things:\\n\\n- **Knowledge**, captured from high-quality, high-trust resources\\n- **Skills**, acquired through highly-relevant interactive lessons devised by you, based on the knowledge\\n- **Wisdom**, which comes from interacting with other learners and practitioners\\n\\nBefore the `RESOURCES.md` is well-populated, your focus should be to find high-quality resources which will help the user acquire knowledge. Never trust your parametric knowledge.\\n\\nSome topics may require more skills than knowledge. Learning more about theoretical physics might be more knowledge-based. For yoga, more skills-based.\\n\\n### Fluency vs Storage Strength\\n\\nYou should be careful to split between two types of learning:\\n\\n- **Fluency strength**: in-the-moment retrieval of knowledge\\n- **Storage strength**: long-term retention of knowledge\\n\\nFluency can give the user an illusory sense of mastery, but storage strength is the real goal. Try to design lessons which build long-term retention by desirable difficulty:\\n\\n- Using retrieval practice (recall from memory)\\n- Spacing (distributing practice over time)\\n- Interleaving (mixing up different but related topics in practice - for skills practice only)\\n\\n## Lessons\\n\\nA lesson is the main thing you produce: the unit in which knowledge and skills reach the user. Each lesson is one self-contained HTML file, saved to `./lessons/` and titled `0001-<dash-case-name>.html` where the number increments each time.\\n\\nA lesson should be **beautiful**, with clean, readable typography and layout, since the user will return to these later to review. Think Tufte.\\n\\nThe lesson should be short, and completable very quickly. Learners\' working memory is very small, and we need to stay within it. But each lesson should give the user a single tangible win that they can build on. It should be directly tied to the mission, and should be in the user\'s zone of proximal development.\\n\\nIf possible, open the lesson file for the user by running a CLI command.\\n\\nEach lesson should link via HTML anchors to other lessons and reference documents.\\n\\nEach lesson should recommend a primary source for the user to read or watch. This should be the most high-quality, high-trust resource you found on the topic.\\n\\nEach lesson should contain a reminder to ask followup questions to the agent. The agent is their teacher, and can assist with anything that\'s unclear.\\n\\n## Assets\\n\\nLessons are built from reusable **components**, stored in `./assets/`: stylesheets, quiz widgets, simulators, diagram helpers, and anything else a second lesson could reuse.\\n\\nReuse is the default, not the exception. Before authoring a lesson, read `./assets/` and build from the components already there. When a lesson needs something new and reusable, write it as a component in `./assets/` and link to it; never inline code a future lesson would duplicate.\\n\\nA shared stylesheet is the first component every workspace earns: every lesson links it, so the lessons look like one consistent course rather than a pile of one-offs. As the workspace grows, so should the component library.\\n\\n## The Mission\\n\\nEvery lesson should be tied into the mission - the reason that the user is interested in learning about the topic.\\n\\nIf the user is unclear about the mission, or the `MISSION.md` is not populated, your first job should be to question the user on why they want to learn this.\\n\\nFailing to understand the mission will mean knowledge acquisition is not grounded in real-world goals. Lessons will feel too abstract. You will have no way of judging what the user should do next.\\n\\nMissions may change as the user develops more skills and knowledge. This is normal - make sure to update the `MISSION.md` and add a learning record to capture the change. Confirm with the user before changing the mission.\\n\\n## Zone Of Proximal Development\\n\\nEach lesson, the user should always feel as if they are being challenged \'just enough\'.\\n\\nThe user may specify an exact thing they want to learn. If they don\'t, figure out their zone of proximal development by:\\n\\n- Reading their `learning-records`\\n- Figuring out the right thing to teach them based on their mission\\n- Teach the most relevant thing that fits in their zone of proximal development\\n\\n## Knowledge\\n\\nLessons should be designed around a skill the user is going to learn. The knowledge in the lesson should be only what\'s required to acquire that skill. You teach the knowledge first, then get the user to practice the skills via an interactive feedback loop.\\n\\nKnowledge should first be gathered from trusted resources. Use `RESOURCES.md` to keep track of them. Lessons should be littered with citations - links to external resources to back up any claim made. This increases the trustworthiness of the lesson.\\n\\nFor acquiring knowledge, difficulty is the enemy. It eats working memory you need for understanding.\\n\\n## Skills\\n\\nIf knowledge is all about acquisition, skills are about durability and flexibility. Make the knowledge stick.\\n\\nFor skill acquisition, difficulty is the tool. Effortful retrieval is what builds storage strength. Skills should be taught through interactive lessons. There are several tools at your disposal:\\n\\n- Interactive lessons, using quizzes and light in-browser tasks\\n- Lessons which guide the user through a list of real-world steps to take (for instance, yoga poses)\\n\\nEach of these should be based on a **feedback loop**, where the user receives feedback on their performance. This feedback loop should be as tight as possible, giving feedback immediately - and ideally automatically.\\n\\nFor quizzes, each answer should be exactly the same number of words (and characters, if possible). Vary which position holds the correct answer across questions. Don\'t give the user any clues about the answer through formatting or order.\\n\\n## Acquiring Wisdom\\n\\nWisdom comes from true real-world interaction - testing your skills outside the learning environment.\\n\\nWhen the user asks a question that appears to require wisdom, your default posture should be to attempt to answer - but to ultimately delegate to a **community**.\\n\\nA community is a place (online or offline) where the user can test their skills in the real world. This might be a forum, a subreddit, a real-world class (budget permitting) or a local interest group.\\n\\nYou should attempt to find high-reputation communities the user can join. If the user expresses a preference that they don\'t want to join a community, respect it.\\n\\n## Reference Documents\\n\\nWhile creating lessons, you should also create reference documents. Lessons can reference these documents - they are useful for tracking raw units of knowledge useful across lessons.\\n\\nLessons will rarely be revisited later - reference documents will be. They should be the compressed essence of the lesson, in a format designed for quick reference.\\n\\nSome learning topics lend themselves to reference:\\n\\n- Syntax and code snippets for programming\\n- Algorithms and flowcharts for processes\\n- Yoga poses and sequences for yoga\\n- Exercises and routines for fitness\\n- Glossaries for any topic with its own nomenclature\\n\\nGlossaries, in particular, are an essential reference. Once one is created, it should be adhered to in every lesson.\\n\\n## `NOTES.md`\\n\\nThe user will sometimes express preferences of how they want to be taught, or things you should keep in mind. This is the place to record those preferences, so you can refer back to them when designing lessons or working with the user.\\n\\r\\n"}'}]

text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"@'\nfrom pathlib import Path\nimport re,json,hashlib\nroot=Path.cwd();out=root/'audits/skills-2026-10-10-repairs';backup=out/'backups/canonical-directives';rows=[]\nnames='brainstorming using-superpowers discord-configure writing-skills wayfinder watch graphify redesign-existing-projects web-artifacts-builder teach'.split()\ndef save(p,s):\n old=p.read_bytes();new=s.encode('utf-8')\n if new==old:return\n bp=backup/p.relative_to(root);bp.parent.mkdir(parents=True,exist_ok=True)\n if not bp.exists():bp.write_bytes(old)\n t=p.with_suffix('.directives-tmp');t.write_bytes(new);assert p.read_bytes()==old;t.replace(p)\n rows.append({'path':str(p.relative_to(root)),'before':hashlib.sha256(old).hexdigest(),'after':hashlib.sha256(new).hexdigest(),'backup':str(bp.relative_to(root))})\nfor rt,act in [('claude','.claude/skills'),('codex','.agents/skills'),('gemini','.gemini/skills')]:\n for n in names:\n  p=root/'skills'/rt/n/'SKILL.md'\n  if not p.exists():continue\n  s=p.read_text(encoding='utf-8')\n  if n=='brainstorming':\n   s=s.replace('You MUST create a task for each of these items and complete them in order:','For unresolved design only, use these checkpoints proportionately; reuse existing requirements and approval rather than repeating settled decisions:')\n   s=s.replace('get user approval after each section','get approval for new unresolved consequential choices').replace('**User reviews written spec** — ask user to review the spec file before proceeding','**Review written spec** — request review only for choices not already approved')\n   s=s.replace('**The terminal state is invoking writing-plans.** Do NOT invoke frontend-design, mcp-builder, or any other implementation skill. The ONLY skill you invoke after brainstorming is writing-plans.','When this task is design-only, finish with the approved design or use writing-plans for a substantial implementation. When implementation is already authorized and requirements are settled, proceed through the appropriate implementation workflow; do not force an extra planning skill.')\n   s=s.replace('Ask after each section whether it looks right so far','Ask about unresolved consequential choices; bundle settled sections for review without repeated approval')\n   a=s.index('**User Review Gate:**');b=s.index('## Key Principles',a)\n   s=s[:a]+'''**User Review Gate:**\nAsk the user to review new consequential choices when approval is still missing. A saved spec documenting already approved decisions does not require another permission gate. State the path and outstanding decisions plainly; wait only for those required answers.\n\n**Implementation:**\nUse writing-plans when work benefits from a detailed plan. For small approved changes, implement directly and verify the relevant behavior.\n\n'''+s[b:]\n   s=s.replace('Always propose 2-3 approaches before settling','Compare alternatives when there is a real unresolved choice').replace('skills/brainstorming/visual-companion.md','`visual-companion.md` relative to this skill directory')\n   s=s.replace('The first time that happens, offer it then, as its own message:','If a separate companion server would materially help, offer it then; ordinary inline/file visuals already within scope need no new permission:')\n  elif n=='using-superpowers':\n   a=s.index('## The Rule');b=s.index('## Platform Adaptation',a)\n   s=s[:a]+'''## Select skills by task\n\nFor an explicit Superpowers request, read the relevant installed entry points before applying them. Announce the skill and its purpose. Use actual available host tools; a Claude Skill tool is not presumed in Codex.\n\nChoose process guidance when it improves an unresolved design or diagnosis. Already approved mechanical work proceeds directly. Simple questions and read-only file checks do not require blanket workflow loading. Use checklists proportionately to the task; no mandatory todo/tool call exists merely because a skill has a checklist.\n\n'''+s[b:]\n   s=s.replace('Only skip skill workflows or instructions when your human partner has explicitly told you to.','Apply only the relevant portions within the authorized task; higher-priority instructions and current host capability always govern.')\n  elif n=='discord-configure':\n   s=s.replace('asks \"how do I set this up\" or \"who can reach me,\"','asks those questions specifically about their Discord channel,')\n   s=s.replace('Read both state files and give the user a complete picture:','Inspect non-secret channel state and check credential presence without reading a secret value into tool output:')\n   s=s.replace('check `the central private .env` for\\n   `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN`.','use a presence-only credential loader/check for `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` in the central private `.env`.')\n   s=s.replace('*\"Run `/discord:configure <token>` with your bot token from\\n     the Developer Portal → Bot → Reset Token.\"*','*\"Enter DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN privately in the central .env using the editor, then rerun Discord status. Do not put a token in chat or a command argument.\"*')\n   s=s.replace('### `<token>` — save it','### Private token entry')\n   s=s.replace('1. Request private credential entry into the central .env; do not accept credential arguments in chat.\\n   long base64-ish strings, typically starting `MT` or `Nz`. Generated from\\n   Developer Portal → Bot → Reset Token; only shown once.\\n2. `mkdir -p ~/.claude/channels/discord`\\n3. Read existing `.env` if present; update/add the `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN=` line,\\n   preserve other keys. Write back, no quotes around the value.','1. Direct private credential entry into the central `.env` editor; never accept token arguments or values in chat.\\n2. REQUIRED: Use [other-skill-name] for workflow.','Use authorized delegation when it helps; read the installed [other-skill-name] entry point through available host tools.')\n  elif n=='wayfinder':\n   s=s.replace('## The Map','## Publication scope\\n\\nPlanning defaults to a local Markdown map. A missing API key is not a blocker to free captions or frames, and does not authorize an installer or a second credential home. Do not run setup.py's credential scaffold. Apply only the relevant portions within the authorized task; higher-priority instructions and current host capability always govern.\\r\\ndiscord-configure 48 **Push toward lockdown — always.** The goal for every setup is `allowlist`\\r\\nwriting-skills 9 Explicit pressure-testing/reference workflow, not automatic primary creator. Preserve unrelated implementation; never delete existing user code solely because a test-first ceremony was absent.\\r\\nwriting-skills 23 **REQUIRED BACKGROUND:** You MUST understand superpowers:test-driven-development before using this skill. This skill adapts TDD to documentation.\\r\\nwriting-skills 289 - ✅ Good: `**REQUIRED BACKGROUND:** You MUST understand superpowers:systematic-debugging`\\r\\nwriting-skills 381 Keep the existing skill, support files and user edits. Use bounded evaluation proportional to the risk and available tools. Missing behavioral evidence is a stated limit, not a mandate to rewrite or delete useful knowledge.\\r\\nwriting-skills 440 | \\"Testing is overkill\\" | Untested skills have issues. Always. 15 min testing saves hours. |\\r\\nwriting-skills 514 2. **Always include a no-guidance control.** If the control doesn\'t exhibit the failure, there is nothing to fix — stop, don\'t author the guidance.\\r\\nwriting-skills 599 - [ ] Commit/push only when explicitly authorized, and stage only owned paths\\r\\nwayfinder 9 Resolve actual host tools rather than literal Skill calls. Creating/assigning/commenting/closing remote issues, adding labels or pushing research branches requires the task\'s explicit remote-write authorization. An explicit request to create/update a tracker map authorizes that named map and its needed tickets, not unrelated publication. Without it, preserve issue-ready drafts locally. Use actual host skill entry points rather than assuming a native Skill tool.\\r\\nwayfinder 87 - **Grilling** (HITL): Conversation. The default case. Always read the installed grilling and domain-modeling entry points.\\r\\nwayfinder 123 5. **Fire the research subagents.** For each `research` ticket you just created, spin up a subagent that reads the installed research skill through the available host tool to resolve it in parallel, capturing its findings on a throwaway `research/<name>` branch with a context pointer from the ticket. Push the branch only if explicitly authorized; otherwise keep local evidence and report the path.\\r\\nwayfinder 134 5. Add newly-surfaced tickets (create-then-wire); graduate any fog the answer has made specifiable, clearing each graduated patch from **Not yet specified** so it lives only as its new ticket. If the answer reveals that a ticket (this one or another) sits beyond the destination, **rule it out of scope** rather than resolving it on the route. If the decision invalidates other parts of the map, update within the authorized scope; delete only with explicit deletion authorization.\\r\\nwatch 15 For transcript-only YouTube requests use the existing free get_youtube_transcript.py collector first. For visual frame inspection check binaries without requiring an API key, then run watch.py with --no-whisper. Credentials stay in the central .env; do not create ~/.config/watch/.env. Optional external audio transcription requires explicit bounded provider/cost authorization and an approved loader. Preserve output until deletion is expressly authorized.\\r\\nwatch 25 For visual questions, inspect the bundled watch script and run it with `--no-whisper` by default. A missing API key is not a blocker to free captions or frames, and does not authorize an installer or a second credential home. Do not run setup.py\'s credential scaffold. API fallback is optional only after explicit upload/spend authorization, through privately entered central `.env` credentials loaded without printing values. If the installed script cannot use that loader, report the integration gap; do not put keys in chat, arguments or `~/.config/watch/.env`.\\r\\nwatch 51 python3 \\"<verified-skill-directory>/scripts/watch.py\\" \\"<source>\\" --no-whisper --no-whisper\\r\\nwatch 60 - `--whisper groq|openai` — force a specific Whisper backend (default: prefer Groq if both keys exist)\\r\\nwatch 61 - `--no-whisper` — disable the Whisper fallback entirely (frames-only if no captions)\\r\\nwatch 96 - **Transcript** — what\'s said at each timestamp. The report\'s header shows the source (`captions` = yt-dlp pulled native subs; `whisper (groq)` or `whisper (openai)` = transcribed by API).\\r\\nwatch 100 **Step 5 — retain captures.** Preserve the working directory for verification and follow-ups. Delete only after explicit authorization.\\r\\nwatch 108    - **Groq** — `whisper-large-v3`. Preferred default: cheaper, faster. Get a key at console.groq.com/keys.\\r\\nwatch 109    - **OpenAI** — `whisper-1`. Fallback. Get a key at platform.openai.com/api-keys.\\r\\nwatch 111 Approved keys live in the central .env; this AI OS does not use the vendor alternate secret home. The script prefers Groq when both are set; override with `--whisper openai` to force OpenAI. Use `--no-whisper` to skip the fallback entirely.\\r\\nwatch 136 - Sends the extracted audio clip to OpenAI\'s audio transcription API (`api.openai.com/v1/audio/transcriptions`) when `OPENAI_API_KEY` is set and Groq is not, or when `--whisper openai` is forced\\r\\nwatch 141 - Does not upload the video itself to any API — only the extracted audio goes out, and only when native captions are missing AND Whisper is not disabled with `--no-whisper`\\r\\nwatch 147 **Bundled scripts:** `scripts/watch.py` (entry point), `scripts/download.py` (yt-dlp wrapper), `scripts/frames.py` (ffmpeg frame extraction), `scripts/transcribe.py` (caption selection + Whisper orchestration), `scripts/whisper.py` (Groq / OpenAI clients), `scripts/setup.py` (preflight + installer)\\r\\ngraphify 9 Graphify is optional derived analysis, not an intake pipeline. Use existing ingest for new sources. Workers must write valid JSON to the exact assigned .graphify_chunk_NN.json path; validate disk content before counting success. Current basename AST IDs are not collision-safe: check duplicate IDs across source_file paths and stop with the collisions rather than silently merge. Until a shared AST/semantic path-ID migration is verified, use a fresh full rebuild for changed/deleted inputs, not stale incremental merge. Missing chunks prevent a completeness claim. Preserve intermediates unless removal is explicitly authorized.\\r\\ngraphify 26 /graphify <path> --whisper-model medium                # use a larger Whisper model for better transcription accuracy\\r\\ngraphify 33 /graphify <path> --neo4j-push bolt://localhost:7687   # push directly to Neo4j\\r\\ngraphify 180 GRAPHIFY_WHISPER_MODEL=base  # or whatever --whisper-model the user passed\\r\\ngraphify 201 **Whisper model:** Default is `base`. If the user passed `--whisper-model <name>`, set `GRAPHIFY_WHISPER_MODEL=<name>` in the environment before running the command above.\\r\\ngraphify 337 - EXTRACTED edges: confidence_score = 1.0 always\\r\\ngraphify 344 Node ID format: lowercase, only `[a-z0-9_]`, no dots or slashes. Format: `{stem}_{entity}` where stem is the filename without extension and entity is the symbol name, both normalized (lowercase, non-alphanumeric chars replaced with `_`). Example: `src/auth/session.py` + `ValidateToken` → `session_validatetoken`. This must match the ID the AST extractor generates so cross-references between code and semantic nodes connect correctly. CRITICAL: never append chunk numbers, sequence numbers, or any suffix to an ID (no `_c1`, `_c2`, `_chunk2`, etc.). IDs must be deterministic from the label alone — the same entity must always produce the same ID regardless of which chunk processes it.\\r\\ngraphify 403 Retain intermediate captures for verification; remove only after explicit authorization.\\r\\ngraphify 533 **Generate HTML always** (unless `--no-viz`). **Obsidian vault only if `--obsidian` was explicitly given** — skip it otherwise, it generates one file per node.\\r\\ngraphify 570 Generate the HTML graph (always, unless `--no-viz`):\\r\\ngraphify 647 ### Step 7 - Neo4j export (only if --neo4j or --neo4j-push flag)\\r\\ngraphify 664 **If `--neo4j-push <uri>`** - push directly to a running Neo4j instance. Verify explicit target/write authorization, then use credentials privately entered in the central `.env` through the approved loader:\\r\\ngraphify 671 from graphify.export import push_to_neo4j\\r\\ngraphify 681 result = push_to_neo4j(G, uri=os.environ[\'NEO4J_URI\'], user=os.environ[\'NEO4J_USER\'], password=[REDACTED], communities=communities)\\r\\ngraphify 682 print(f\'Pushed to Neo4j: {result[\\\\\\"nodes\\\\\\"]} nodes, {result[\\\\\\"edges\\\\\\"]} edges\')\\r\\ngraphify 843 Before running any subcommand below (`--update`, `--cluster-only`, `query`, `path`, `explain`, `add`), check that `.graphify_python` exists. If it\'s missing (e.g. user deleted `graphify-out/`), re-resolve the interpreter first:\\r\\ngraphify 861 For changed/deleted inputs, perform a fresh full rebuild until incremental pruning is verified; the incremental code below is reference-only and must not run in that mode.\\r\\ngraphify 919 # Prune nodes from deleted files\\r\\ngraphify 921 deleted = set(incremental.get(\'deleted_files\', []))\\r\\ngraphify 922 if deleted:\\r\\ngraphify 923     to_remove = [n for n, d in G_existing.nodes(data=True) if d.get(\'source_file\') in deleted]\\r\\ngraphify 925     print(f\'Pruned {len(to_remove)} ghost nodes from {len(deleted)} deleted file(s)\')\\r\\ngraphify 974 Retain intermediate captures for verification; remove only after explicit authorization.\\r\\ngraphify 1336 Reference only: install always-on integration only when explicitly requested. Preserve hand-owned instructions; in AI OS shared rules require deliberate changes to both AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md, not an automatic vendor installer:\\r\\ngraphify 1354 - Always show token cost in the report.\\r\\nredesign-existing-projects 59 - **Symmetrical vertical padding.** Top and bottom padding are always identical. Adjust optically — bottom padding often needs to be slightly larger.\\r\\nredesign-existing-projects 60 - **Dashboard always has a left sidebar.** Try top navigation, a floating command menu, or a collapsible panel instead.\\r\\nredesign-existing-projects 65 - **Mathematical alignment that looks optically wrong.** Centering by the math doesn\'t always look centered to the eye. Icons next to text, play buttons in circles, or text in buttons often need 1-2px optical adjustments to feel right.\\r\\nredesign-existing-projects 98 - **Always one filled button + one ghost button.** Add text links or tertiary styles to reduce visual noise.\\r\\nredesign-existing-projects 105 - **Light/dark toggle always a sun/moon switch.** Use a dropdown, system preference detection, or integrate it into settings.\\r\\nredesign-existing-projects 113 - **Missing favicon.** Always include a branded favicon.\\r\\nteach 87 Each lesson, the user should always feel as if they are being challenged \'just enough\'.\\r\\n"}'}]

text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"@'\nfrom pathlib import Path\nimport json,hashlib,re\nroot=Path.cwd();out=root/'audits/skills-2026-10-10-repairs';backup=out/'backups/canonical-directives';rows=json.loads((out/'DIRECTIVES-receipts.json').read_text(encoding='utf-8'))\ndef save(p,s):\n old=p.read_bytes();new=s.encode('utf-8')\n if old==new:return\n bp=backup/p.relative_to(root);bp.parent.mkdir(parents=True,exist_ok=True)\n if not bp.exists():bp.write_bytes(old)\n t=p.with_suffix('.directives-tmp');t.write_bytes(new);assert p.read_bytes()==old;t.replace(p)\n prior=next((x for x in rows if x['path']==str(p.relative_to(root))),None)\n if prior:prior['after']=hashlib.sha256(new).hexdigest()\n else:rows.append({'path':str(p.relative_to(root)),'before':hashlib.sha256(old).hexdigest(),'after':hashlib.sha256(new).hexdigest(),'backup':str(bp.relative_to(root))})\nfor rt,act in [('claude','.claude/skills'),('codex','.agents/skills'),('gemini','.gemini/skills')]:\n for n in ['graphify','watch']:\n  p=root/'skills'/rt/n/'SKILL.md';s=p.read_text(encoding='utf-8')\n  if n=='watch':\n   s=s.replace('--no-whisper --no-whisper','--no-whisper')\n   s=re.sub(r'(python3 \"\\$\\{CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR\\}/scripts/watch.py\"[^\\n]*)(?=\\n)',lambda m:m.group(1)+' --no-whisper' if '--no-whisper' not in m.group(1) else m.group(1),s)\n   s=s.replace('Approved keys live in the central .env; this AI OS does not use the vendor alternate secret home. The script prefers Groq when both are set; override with `--whisper openai` to force OpenAI. Use `--no-whisper` to skip the fallback entirely.','Approved keys live in the central private `.env`; no alternate credential home. Default `--no-whisper` disables paid/upload fallback. Configured keys do not authorize spending or upload; verify the central loader and explicit task/provider limits before an optional fallback.')\n  else:\n   guard=\"\"\"# Stop cross-file basename-ID collisions before any deduplication.\nidentity = {}\nfor node in all_nodes:\n    nid = node['id']\n    source = node.get('source_file')\n    if nid in identity and identity[nid] != source:\n        raise ValueError(f'Node ID collision: {nid}: {identity[nid]} vs {source}')\n    identity[nid] = source\n\"\"\"\n   s=s.replace('seen = set()\\ndeduped = []',guard+'seen = set()\\ndeduped = []')\n   guard2=guard.replace('all_nodes','ast[\\'nodes\\'] + sem[\\'nodes\\']')\n   s=s.replace('# Merge: AST nodes first, semantic nodes deduplicated by id',guard2+'\\n# Merge: AST nodes first, semantic nodes deduplicated by id')\n   s=s.replace('## For --update (incremental re-extraction)','## For --update (incremental reference only)')\n   s=s.replace('For changed/deleted inputs, perform a fresh full rebuild until incremental pruning is verified; the incremental code below is reference-only and must not run in that mode.','For changed/deleted inputs, perform a fresh full rebuild in a new derived output directory and bypass old semantic caches for that rebuild. Reuse immutable sources but regenerate all required chunks. Until incremental pruning is verified, all code in this reference section is non-operational; do not follow its later incremental steps. Preserve prior output as evidence.')\n   s=s.replace(\"# If missing, install in a dedicated sibling project environment using existing uv/Python tools.\\n# Never bypass managed-environment protection with --break-system-packages.\",\"# If missing, stop this block and install in a dedicated sibling project environment.\\n# Rerun import verification with that interpreter before proceeding; no managed-system override.\")\n  save(p,s);ap=root/act/n/'SKILL.md'\n  if ap.exists():save(ap,s)\n(out/'DIRECTIVES-receipts.json').write_text(json.dumps(rows,indent=2),encoding='utf-8')\nprint('Receipt paths',len(rows))\n'@ | python -",max_output_tokens:1000}));

text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"@'\nfrom pathlib import Path\nimport json,hashlib\nroot=Path.cwd();out=root/'audits/skills-2026-10-10-repairs';rows=json.loads((out/'DIRECTIVES-receipts.json').read_text(encoding='utf-8'));errors=[];pairs=[]\nfor r in rows:\n if hashlib.sha256((root/r['path']).read_bytes()).hexdigest()!=r['after']:errors.append('current '+r['path'])\n if hashlib.sha256((root/r['backup']).read_bytes()).hexdigest()!=r['before']:errors.append('backup '+r['path'])\nns='brainstorming using-superpowers discord-configure writing-skills wayfinder watch graphify redesign-existing-projects web-artifacts-builder teach'.split()\nfor rt,act in [('claude','.claude/skills'),('codex','.agents/skills'),('gemini','.gemini/skills')]:\n for n in ns:\n  p=root/act/n/'SKILL.md';c=root/'skills'/rt/n/'SKILL.md'\n  if p.exists() and c.exists():\n   eq=p.read_bytes()==c.read_bytes();pairs.append({'runtime':rt,'skill':n,'equal':eq})\n   if not eq:errors.append('activation '+str(p))\n# Prediction: old basename dedup drops one distinct source; edited guard must reject this collision.\ns=(root/'skills/claude/graphify/SKILL.md').read_text(encoding='utf-8');a=s.index('# Stop cross-file basename-ID collisions');b=s.index('seen = set()',a);guard=s[a:b]\nnodes=[{'id':'session_validate','source_file':'src/a/session.py'},{'id':'session_validate','source_file':'src/b/session.py'}]\nold={x['id']:x for x in nodes};rejected=False\ntry:exec(guard,{'all_nodes':nodes})\nexcept ValueError as e:rejected='Node ID collision' in str(e)\nexec(guard,{'all_nodes':[nodes[0],nodes[0]]})\nfixture={'old_loses_distinct_source':len(old)==1,'edited_guard_rejects_collision':rejected,'same_source_duplicate_allowed':True};assert all(fixture.values())\nfor n,forbidden in {'writing-skills':['Delete it. Start over','Delete means delete','All of these mean: Delete code'], 'using-superpowers':['BEFORE any response or action','Before entering plan mode'], 'watch':['rm -rf','Then write it into'], 'redesign-existing-projects':['Randomize dates to appear real'], 'teach':['Treat the current directory as a teaching workspace','exactly the same number of words'], 'graphify':[\"from graphify.ingest import ingest\",\"password=[REDACTED]}.items():\n for rt in ['claude','codex','gemini']:\n  p=root/'skills'/rt/n/'SKILL.md'\n  if p.exists():\n   text=p.read_text(encoding='utf-8')\n   for f in forbidden:\n    if f in text:errors.append('imperative '+rt+'/'+n+' '+f)\nassert not errors,errors\nchecks={'files':len(rows),'errors':errors,'activation_pairs':pairs,'graph_collision_fixture':fixture,'limit':'Static contract and isolated embedded-snippet checks; no full Graphify/vendor script/native selector/live service execution.'}\n(out/'DIRECTIVES-checks.json').write_text(json.dumps(checks,indent=2),encoding='utf-8');print(json.dumps({'files':len(rows),'activation_pairs':len(pairs),'errors':errors,'fixture':fixture},indent=2))\n'@ | python -",max_output_tokens:1600}));

text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"@'\nfrom pathlib import Path\np=Path('audits/skills-2026-10-10-repairs/DIRECTIVES.md')\np.write_text('''# Remove contradictory body commands instead of relying on a header\n\nCompleted 2026-10-10 after the parent bulk script finished and edit clearance arrived. Full current Claude bodies were read, including all 1,381 Graphify lines and all skill-writing sections. Complete runtime differences were inspected; the parent had reconciled these named bodies to identical text before this pass. No global files or frozen snapshots changed.\n\n| Skill | Conflicting directive repaired |\n|---|---|\n| brainstorming | Replaced universal todo/per-section approval/written-spec approval and forced writing-plans terminal state with unresolved-choice checkpoints. Settled user approval permits direct implementation. Repaired malformed spec-review wording and support-file path. |\n| using-superpowers | Removed mandatory loading before every response/file check, pre-plan brainstorming and universal rationalization table. Explicit workflow reference remains; actual host capability and user authority govern. |\n| discord-configure | Removed token-in-chat command guidance and token-shape/private-value instructions. Presence-only central credential checks; verify installed server loader/reload support instead of claiming central-file compatibility. No channel server was configured. |\n| writing-skills | Replaced delete/start-over/no-exception skill-writing ceremony and destructive example recipes. Preserve originals and test relevant contracts proportionately; user-authorized batch can retain per-skill checks. Publishing remains explicitly authorized only. |\n| wayfinder | Local planning default; remote tracker create/assign/comment/close and research push bounded to actual authorization. Replaced nonexistent native Skill calls and preserved superseded records. |\n| watch | Replaced API-key-required setup/alternate credential scaffold with free tool/caption preflight and --no-whisper. Preserve captures, no inferred cleanup permission, no provider-switch authorization from key presence. Corrected example 3fps to 2fps cap. |\n| graphify | Removed independent URL intake pipeline: existing ingest owns source retrieval/provenance. Missing chunks block complete claims; selected worker capability not hardcoded host names. Added cross-file ID collision checks before both dedup stages; no silent basename merge. Changed source rebuilds bypass stale caches. Removed managed-system package override and secret literal password substitution. Derived answers require requested save and labeling; always-on/hook integration is explicit only. Fixed prose mistakenly inside a shell fence. |\n| redesign-existing-projects | No fake dates, identities or brands; changes are brief-scoped candidates, native scrolling preserved, effects optional with accessibility limits. Preserved aesthetic reference ideas without replacing real product facts. |\n| web-artifacts-builder | Sibling application home, verified absolute script paths and host shell; relevant build/interaction checks before claiming working artifact, rather than optional deferred testing for speed. |\n| teach | Selected sibling learning workspace replaces caller-cwd output home. Comparable quiz options preserve meaning instead of exact word/character counts. |\n\n## Evidence\n\n[DIRECTIVES-receipts.json](DIRECTIVES-receipts.json) records before/after hashes and path-preserving backups for 39 edited catalog/activation files. [DIRECTIVES-checks.json](DIRECTIVES-checks.json) records zero current/backup mismatches and 10 activation pairs matching their runtime catalogs. Static regression checks found none of the targeted destructive/incorrect body directives.\n\nPredicted old basename dedup would discard one of two distinct files sharing the same node ID. It did. The collision guard read directly from the edited Markdown rejected the conflicting two-source input with ValueError and accepted a repeated same-source record. This validates the narrow guard, not the entire Graphify pipeline or path-ID migration. No Python source file was edited; the test executed the actual embedded guard inline.\n\nBundled vendor setup/download/extraction scripts and native routing remain untested here. Watch central credential loading, Discord server credential loading and optional external/API services must be verified before those routes run.
